# apitap 0.57.0 — implementation brief (handoff)

**For the implementer.** This is the whole specification for 0.57.0. Code from it; do
not re-derive. It was produced read-only on 2026-09-27 against the tree at `v0.56.0`
(`d45f932`), from the audit in `docs/review/2026-09-19-prod-readiness-v0.56.0.md`.

**How to read it.**

1. **§0 is authoritative.** Every place two sections disagree is decided in §0 and the
   decision overrides the section text. Where a section sentence is stale, a bracketed
   *[§0 … overrides]* note sits right next to it. Nine such notes are inline.
2. **§3 is the order of work** — one commit per step, each with the test that must be RED
   before and GREEN after. Follow it; the sections are the reference for each step.
3. **§5 is the coverage matrix** and **§6 is how to verify the whole**, the way this
   project ships (PGO wheel, the gate on that exact wheel, pull back from PyPI).
4. The project's hard rules still apply to everything here: engine logic in Rust only;
   one-line API with no new required knob; no reaping on a token's age; every test seen
   RED before it counts; legs ask the server, never the exit code; no Claude attribution
   in commit messages.

**How it was made.** Five root-cause clusters, two independent designs each (one
structural, one surgical-but-root-cause), one judge on two axes (correctness/safety and
root-cause/fit), two adversarial attackers, one writer per cluster that applied the
attack fixes, and one integrator for §0/§1/§3–§6. The judge chose structural for A and
E, surgical for B, C and D. A first attack round ran against a plan whose labels were
wrong (a bug in the orchestration script, fixed); the second round, against the correct
plan, returned *sound with fixes* on both lenses.

**One thing to prove first.** The single remaining fatal attack trace was the BigQuery
fence under several drains sharing one dataset. §2.A (A2) answers it by moving the fence
into a per-run table `_apitap_fence<tok>`, so a sibling's keeper never writes into another
run's script; §0 L10/L11 lock that in. It was not re-attacked after the rewrite. Its proof
is the leg `e2e_bq_multi_drain.py` (§5 row `bq.multi-drain-one-dataset`, whose RED control
is exactly the lease-in-script variant). The premise that concurrent `INSERT`s into
`_apitap_state` from sibling scripts do not conflict is BigQuery's documented behaviour and
this leg is what confirms it on the rig — land it early in §3 step order, not last.

**Citations** in the sections were spot-checked by hand against the tree (run.rs,
dest_pg.rs, dest_bq.rs, sink/clickhouse.rs) and held; the integrator re-read its own list
at the top of §0.

---

*§2.A–§2.E (the five cluster sections) go between §1 and §3. Line numbers are from the v0.56.0 tree (`d45f932`, checked out at `d7b90dc`). I re-read these citations and they hold: run.rs:905-908, 1006-1010, 1220-1224, 1596, 1705, 1728; lease.rs:69-71, 127-129; sink/clickhouse.rs:65; sink/mysql.rs:220; dest_my.rs:280-285, 480-482; dest_pg.rs:94-95; mysource.rs:760-764; naming.rs:72-80, 834-869, 944-948; pipeline/mod.rs:440-495; dispatch.rs:99-116, 331-360; gate.py:44-91, 122-144; py-apitap/src/lib.rs:50-54, 78-86.*

## §0 Decisions already made: implement them, do not reopen them

Where two sections disagree, the decision is stated here and it overrides the section text. "Struck" means: do not implement it, even though a section still describes it.

C §3.3-§3.5 (FinishMarked, BqLoader abort, discard belt) have no dependencies. They are placed in phase 5 but may land earlier. | all |
| G2 | Engine logic lives in apitap-core. There are no changes in `py-apitap/` apart from the version (the workspace sets it, Cargo.toml:6) and the README text. No new `TransferOptions` field, no new env var, no knob. | all |
| G3 | There is no CHANGELOG file in this repo. Release notes go in the release commit message, in the same shape as `d45f932`. Every "CHANGELOG" line in §2.A-§2.E means that message. | §6 |
| G4 | No `.github/workflows/*` change in this release: the push token lacks the `workflow` scope. | §6 |

At intermediate checkpoints the gate's leg verdict must be 0 FAIL and 0 SKIP, and the matrix GAP count must not increase. `gate.py --matrix` must exit 0 only from step 43 on. | §2.E |

## §1 The shape of the release

**0.57.0 is a correctness release with no new features.** It closes all sixteen §3 findings of the v0.56.0 audit, every medium and low item in §6 of that audit except those listed in §4 below, and every gap in audit §8. It does this through five structural changes, one per root cause:

1. **Ownership is a value (§2.A).** A destination write can only happen through a `Held` unit that `Tenure::open` gives out. Each engine fences that unit its own way: a row lock on pg/my, a predicate in every statement on ch, a per-run fence table on bq.
2. **Apply contracts are types (§2.B).** A `TableWindow` cannot exist without its `Layout`. `DeleteSet` and `Resolved` yield each key once. `replay_plan` is the only replay rule. `state_verdict` is the only way to read `_apitap_state`.
3. **Children live inside their owner (§2.C).** One `join_all` loop owns bulk workers, closing a channel without `Finish` aborts, and a bootstrap child is minted within its parent.
4. **The sink declares its memory (§2.D).** Parquet row groups are chosen by the planner, and an Iceberg merge streams its equality deletes.
5. **Declared compatibility, one guard loop, a gate that proves coverage (§2.E).**

Version: workspace `Cargo.toml:6` `0.56.0 → 0.57.0`, `docs/stability.md:47` pin, `Cargo.lock`.

| Workload | 0.56.0 | 0.57.0 | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| Bulk into Postgres/MySQL/ClickHouse | READY | READY | Also: siblings stop and are aborted on the first error (C). |
| Bulk into S3 / BigQuery / Iceberg (replace, append) | CONDITIONS (pin `parallel`; manual cleanup after a worker error) | **READY** | Residency is priced (D, legs E1); every pipe is consumed and `discard` runs after the last write (C, `e2e_worker_cancel`). |
| Bulk into GCS | CONDITIONS | **CONDITIONS** | The same code as S3, but no gate leg (the service account is BigQuery-only). Covered by construction only. |
| Iceberg `merge` | CONDITIONS (delta of a few M rows) | **READY** | Delete files are streamed per data file; memory is flat in the delta (E2). |
| CDC replica into Postgres | CONDITIONS | **READY** | search_path resolved, keeper on its own pool, apply joined on error, lease renewed at commit. |
| CDC into MySQL (pg or MySQL source) | CONDITIONS | **READY** | Cross-lane refusal, TRUNCATE under the fence, claim decided by read, GTID-safe twins. |
| CDC into ClickHouse (any source, including a paused drain) | CONDITIONS / NOT READY | **READY** | Sticky biased claims, no TTL, every statement predicated and bounded to ttl/2. The residuals are documented, not conditions (§4). |
| MySQL/MariaDB-source CDC with TRUNCATE-only windows | NOT READY | **READY** | `Layout` travels with the window (B). |
| Any CDC into BigQuery | NOT READY | **READY, with one upgrade condition** | Fence table, one row per key, job ids, moved-key MERGE. **Condition:** stop every 0.56.0 BigQuery drain before a 0.57.0 run of the same dataset starts. |
| Upgrading `changelog=True` from 0.55.x | NOT READY | **READY, with one condition** | seq continuation (B). **Condition:** go straight to 0.57.0; do not stop at 0.56.0. |
| Rolling upgrade: 0.55.1 bulk beside a newer drain | NOT READY | **READY, with one condition** | The drain also announces a Staging marker (E). **Condition:** a 0.55.1 *drain* is invisible, so upgrade drains before bulk jobs. |
| Iceberg drains | unguarded | **unguarded** | By design (run.rs:144-157). One drain per Iceberg table is the scheduler's job. |

**Upgrade note (one paragraph, also in the release message).** Upgrade `log_based` jobs before bulk jobs. Stop every 0.56.0 BigQuery drain before any 0.57.0 run of the same dataset. Move 0.55.x `changelog=True` destinations straight to 0.57.0.

What changes in place with no operator step:
- ClickHouse `_apitap_lease` loses its TTL.
- ClickHouse `_apitap_cdc_pending` gains `seq_base`, `end_lsn` and `events`.
- BigQuery gets `_apitap_cdc_pending` on first use.

Behaviour changes users will see:
- A capped multi-core parquet run plans fewer pipes; for example 256 MiB on 4 cores: 8 → 5.
- BigQuery at 128 MiB on ≥ 2 cores takes the CSV lane.
- Iceberg merge commits one delete file per data file.
- A cursor switch on an Iceberg table, and a cross-lane switch on MySQL/ClickHouse/BigQuery, now refuses.
- A NULL CDC watermark on BigQuery now errors.
- MySQL-source keys come from the run's key names.

Stranded state and rollback:
- A dead 0.56.0 Postgres drain under a non-`public` search_path leaves a lease that 0.57.0 cannot find. Drop its lock by hand, once.
- Rolling back to 0.56.0 is safe, but costs one manual DROP of a killed 0.57.0 drain's staging marker. It can also re-append one crashed changelog window with repeated pairs.
- Rolling back to 0.55.1 requires dropping `_apitap_lease` and `_apitap_cdc_pending` first (the procedure is in failure-modes.md).

---

---

## §2.A Ownership: one gate for every destination write

### 1. Root cause

Ownership of a destination table is a convention, not a value. `set_run` puts a token string into a `Mutex<Option<String>>` on three destinations (dest_pg.rs:17-31, dest_my.rs:26-28/88-90, dest_ch.rs:50-53/139-141). On BigQuery it is a documented no-op (run.rs:163-172; `BqDest` has no field, dest_bq.rs:76-78). Each engine then decides for itself whether, where and how to consult the token:

- **Postgres / MySQL:** a row lock (dest_pg.rs:56-118, dest_my.rs:128-184). Both treat a missing row as "nothing to fence against" (dest_pg.rs:94-95, dest_my.rs:164-166).
- **ClickHouse:** read-then-act at four hand-picked sites (dest_ch.rs:967, 982, 998, 1258).
- **BigQuery:** no check at all.

The lease store underneath also has a different idea of a claim on each engine:
- ClickHouse renewal overwrites `collected` (dest_ch.rs:173-183 → sink/clickhouse.rs:92 and 108-109).
- MySQL decides a claim by `affected_rows` (sink/mysql.rs:220).
- The ClickHouse TTL deletes rows by age (sink/clickhouse.rs:65).

Every write that is not an "apply" never reaches any of those checks, because nothing in the type system routes a write through one. That covers the no-traffic watermark (dest_my.rs:430-435; dest_ch.rs:848, 868), the source-identity marker (run.rs:1128-1133 → 301-309), `bootstrap_finish`, `clear_state` and the MySQL TRUNCATE (dest_my.rs:478-482).

The run loop holds ownership by convention too. Keeper stop, lock drop and lease close are three sequential calls (run.rs:1029-1043, 1323-1337) that nothing ties to the apply task. The task is spawned at :1596 and joined only at :1728, and the `?` at :1705 and :1720 sits between them.

§3.1, §3.2, §3.8, §3.9 and §3.10, the TTL-longer-than-apply item, the three unfenced watermark writes, the keeper's shutdown exit, the missing `Drop`, the 2-connection starvation and the search_path blindness are one defect. There is no single object whose possession *is* ownership and through which every write must pass.

### 2. The decision

`lease::Tenure` becomes the only constructor of a destination write unit (`Held`). Every CDC write — data, watermark, pending marker, source marker, bootstrap finish, clear — happens between `Fence::open_unit` and `Fence::close_unit`, and each is fenced by the destination's own view of the lease:

- **Postgres / MySQL:** a row lock.
- **ClickHouse:** a predicate inside every statement, with a time margin.
- **BigQuery:** a per-run fence table mutated inside the script transaction.
- **Iceberg:** one named `Unguarded` impl.

Claims become monotonic and read-decided on every store. `Tenure::release` cannot pass an open unit. Cost per window per table:

| Engine | Extra cost |
|---|---|
| Postgres | 0 round trips, plus one schema lookup per run |
| MySQL | +1 tiny `SELECT` |
| ClickHouse | +0 (the `written_rows` header replaces today's conditional pre-watermark check) and a scalar subquery per statement |
| BigQuery | +1 single-row DML per script chunk |
| Keeper (all) | +1 `lease_unclaimed` read per tick, plus one extra Postgres connection |

### 3. What changes

#### 3.0 Amendments to the winning design (what changed and why)

| # | Change | Why |
|---|---|---|
| A1 | `lease::collect_or_refuse`, `Fence::announce/list_peers/drop_lock` and `yield_lock/retake_lock` are **struck**. | Cross-cluster: one collector loop (cluster E's `guard.rs`); bootstrap ownership is C's lineage. |
| A2 | BigQuery fence moved out of the shared `_apitap_lease` into a per-run fence table `_apitap_fence<tok>`. `RENEW_DURING_COMMIT` is deleted and the keeper is never quiet. | Both attack traces: sibling keepers' writes aborted every script, and a quiet keeper livelocked a MERGE longer than the TTL. |
| A3 | BigQuery `close_unit` appends each state row **into that table's own group**. The pending marker is a body op. `Watermark` has no marker variant. | Otherwise `pack_whole_groups` could split data from its watermark again. |
| A4 | ClickHouse ownership predicate = row exists ∧ ¬collected ∧ life > ttl/2. `_apitap_lease` TTL removed. Owned statements are bounded server-side to ttl/2. | TTL-merge resurrection, the weekend wedge, and a statement outliving a claim. |
| A5 | ClickHouse `bookkeeping_ddl(sql)` and `schema_ddl(sql)` string allowlists are replaced by typed ops that compute their own target names. | The patchwork warning. |
| A6 | MySQL TRUNCATE stays O(1): **renew-then-DDL** on the fenced connection, not `DELETE FROM`. Temp twins are created **before** `START TRANSACTION`. | A single huge DELETE wedges Group Replication, Galera and small buffer pools (1206); twins inside the tx hit GTID error 1787. |
| A7 | Postgres schema is the table's own namespace (`to_regclass`), else `current_schema()`, shared by both lanes. | `current_schema()` alone misses a table in the 2nd search_path entry, or with a `"$user"` schema present. |
| A8 | By-value `open`/`close` is the only API. No closure `write`. | Graft: avoids HRTB over `&mut Unit`. |
| A9 | The claim bias comment is corrected. Epoch micros pass 2^51 in **2041**, not never. The real bound is micros < 2^62 (≈ year 148 000). | The winning design's reasoning was wrong. |

#### 3.1 Cross-cluster rules this section imposes (and consumes)

| Topic | Owner | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| Announce / scan / collect | **E** (`guard.rs`) | This cluster requires `GuardStore` to call three things. (a) `LeaseStore::lease_get`, then `lease_claim -> Claim` (collect only on `Taken`, one claim per token). (b) After every marker of the victim token is observed dropped, `LeaseStore::sweep_run(bare, peer_token)` and then `lease_close` gated by E's `Released` proof. (c) `LeaseStore::close_run(peer_token)` once per collected token. `Tenure::acquire/release` call `guard::announce / check_peers / release` and hold its `Vec<Announced>`. |
| Bootstrap ownership | **C** (lineage) | The Tenure **never drops its lock** during bootstrap. The child load runs as `crate::transfer_within(tenure.run(), …)` (C's `mint_within`/`owns`) *[§0 R2 overrides: there is no `owns()`; the parent's artifacts classify `Found::Parent`, and every delete uses `is_self`.]*. Any delete or sweep uses exact-token equality (`RunId::is_self` / a token string), never `owns`. `sweep_run` takes the exact peer token. |
| Watermark **value**, marker, trim | **B** | B computes (`plan.watermark()`, the marker row, the trim). A writes: the watermark only via `Watermark::Set{lsn: plan.watermark()}` in `close_unit`. On ClickHouse the marker is `ChUnit::mark_pending_owned` in the unit body, after the trim and strictly before `insert_owned`. On BigQuery marker, rows and state are statements of that table's group (`BqUnit::push`). B's extra `clear_state` work (pending rows) goes in the `Watermark::Clear` arm of `close_unit`, nowhere else. |
| BigQuery conflict domain | **B, E** | Inside a fenced script, writes to shared bookkeeping tables (`_apitap_state`, `_apitap_cdc_pending`) are **INSERT-only**. The only UPDATE/DELETE allowed in a script are on the run's own fence table and, for `Clear`, `DELETE … _apitap_state`. |
| Docs | **E** | E's failure-modes / stability sentences and CLAIMS rows must use §7 below. |

#### 3.2 `crates/apitap-core/src/lease.rs`

Module doc :37-48 is rewritten. It adds the definition "owner = my row exists and is not collected; `expires_at` is only the collector's permission to claim", plus a per-engine fence paragraph from §5 below. `Lease::lapsed()` (:69-71) is unchanged; it is the collector's test.

```rust
pub(crate) const LOST_MARK: &str = "apitap-lease-lost";
pub(crate) fn owned_margin_secs() -> u64 { ttl_secs() / 2 }         // ClickHouse/BigQuery-DDL time fence
pub(crate) fn no_longer_holds(keys: &[String]) -> Error {             // text of dest_pg.rs:97-102, keys joined
    Error::Locked(format!("{}: this drain no longer holds the table — another run collected its claim, \
      so it is not allowed to write. Nothing more was written. Re-run; the other run either finished or \
      will be collected in turn.", keys.join(", ")))
}
pub(crate) enum Claim { Taken, Refused, Absent }
pub(crate) enum Watermark {
    Set   { table: String, source_id: String, lsn: u64, rows: u64 },
    Clear { table: String, source_id: String },
}
pub(crate) trait LeaseStore: Send + Sync + 'static {
    fn lease_key(&self, dest_table: &str) -> String;
    async fn lease_open(&self, keys: &[String], token: &str) -> Result<()>;       // also creates run-scoped fence objects (BQ)
    /// Never blocks; never touches a collected or missing row.
    async fn lease_renew(&self, keys: &[String], token: &str) -> Result<u64>;
    /// Keys of `token` whose row EXISTS and is NOT collected. Expiry ignored.
    async fn lease_unclaimed(&self, token: &str) -> Result<Vec<String>>;
    async fn lease_get(&self, key: &str, token: &str) -> Result<Option<Lease>>;
    /// Verdict from a READ of the row, never an affected-row count. After `Taken`,
    /// (key, token) is never again "exists ∧ ¬collected". Re-entrant: an already
    /// collected row returns Taken and re-runs any fencing side effect.
    async fn lease_claim(&self, key: &str, token: &str) -> Result<Claim>;
    async fn lease_close(&self, key: &str, token: &str);
    /// Drop run-scoped scratch of `token` for one table (exact token, never lineage).
    async fn sweep_run(&self, dest_table: &str, token: &str);
    /// Drop run-scoped objects not tied to a table (BigQuery fence). No-op elsewhere.
    async fn close_run(&self, token: &str);
}
pub(crate) trait Fence: LeaseStore + crate::guard::GuardStore {
    type Unit<'a>: Send where Self: 'a;
    /// BigQuery: every close_unit of one run mutates one fence table -> serialize them.
    const SERIAL_COMMIT: bool;
    async fn open_unit<'a>(&'a self, keys: &[String], token: &str) -> Result<Self::Unit<'a>>;
    /// Writes every mark (and on BQ/Ice, the data commit) and commits.
    /// Ok(()) = the destination accepted the unit as the owner's.
    async fn close_unit<'a>(&'a self, u: Self::Unit<'a>, token: &str, marks: Vec<Watermark>) -> Result<()>;
}
pub(crate) struct Held<'t, F: Fence + 't> {
    pub(crate) unit: F::Unit<'t>, keys: Vec<String>,
    _inflight: tokio::sync::OwnedRwLockReadGuard<()>,
}
pub(crate) struct Tenure<F: Fence> {
    dest: Arc<F>, run: RunId, tables: Vec<String>, keys: Vec<String>,
    announced: std::sync::Mutex<Vec<crate::guard::Announced>>,
    evicted: Arc<AtomicBool>, closing: AtomicBool,
    units: Arc<tokio::sync::RwLock<()>>, commit_gate: tokio::sync::Mutex<()>,
    keeper: std::sync::Mutex<Option<Keeper>>,
}
```

**`Tenure::acquire(dest: Arc<F>, tables: &[String], run: RunId) -> Result<Self>`**

This replaces run.rs:905-953 and 1138-1180.
1. `keys = tables.map(lease_key)`, then `dest.lease_open(&keys, token)`. The lease comes before the lock; the run.rs:910-915 argument is unchanged.
2. `announced = guard::announce(&*dest, tables, &run).await?` (E rolls back its own partial announce).
3. `guard::check_peers(&*dest, tables, &run).await`. On `Err`, run `self.release_inner()` (step 3 of `release`) and return the error.
4. `Keeper::spawn` (private) and return.

**`Tenure::open(&self, tables: &[&str]) -> Result<Held<'_, F>>`**
1. If `closing` is set, return `Err(Locked("run is winding down"))`. If `evicted` is set, return `Err(no_longer_holds(keys))`. No I/O.
2. `let g = self.units.clone().read_owned().await;`
3. `let unit = dest.open_unit(&keys_of(tables), token).await?;`
4. Return `Held{unit, keys, _inflight: g}`.

**`Tenure::close(&self, h: Held<'_, F>, marks: Vec<Watermark>) -> Result<()>`**
- Every `mark.table` must be one of `h`'s tables; otherwise `Err(Transfer("internal: mark for unheld table"))`.
- `let _g = F::SERIAL_COMMIT.then(|| self.commit_gate.lock())` (awaited).
- `dest.close_unit(h.unit, token, marks).await` (`_inflight` is dropped after it).
- Dropping a `Held` without `close` rolls back:
  - Postgres: sqlx rollback on drop.
  - MySQL: `MyTx::drop` spawns `ROLLBACK` plus the twin drop.
  - ClickHouse / BigQuery / Iceberg: nothing was committed that a watermark confirms.

**`Tenure::release(&self)`**
1. `closing.store(true)`.
2. `let _all = self.units.write().await` — waits for every `Held`.
3. `keeper.take().stop().await` (abort plus join, lease.rs:149-155 unchanged). Then `let released = guard::release(&*dest, take(announced)).await`. For each released member: `dest.sweep_run(t, token)`, then `dest.lease_close(key_t, token)`. If every member was released: `dest.close_run(token)`. This is the same order and the same "lease only after lock observed gone" rule as run.rs:1033-1043.

**Drop and the keeper**

`impl Drop for Tenure` and `impl Drop for Keeper` both call `handle.abort()`; there is no await in `Drop`.

The keeper (rewrites :108-156; `spawn` becomes private, and its argument is `(Arc<F>, keys, token, evicted)`). Every `renew_secs()`:
1. `dest.lease_renew(&keys, token)`: errors are logged under `APITAP_DEBUG`, never fatal (:132-140 kept).
2. `if let Ok(still) = dest.lease_unclaimed(token)`: for each `k ∉ still`, set `evicted = true` and log `eprintln!("apitap: {k}: another run collected this drain's claim; it stops before its next write")`.

The `|| crate::shutdown::requested()` exit at :127-129 is deleted: a SIGTERM wind-down keeps renewing until `release`. Expiry is never an eviction signal.

#### 3.3 `crates/apitap-core/src/naming.rs`

- Add `Artifact::CdcStaging` with suffix `"__apitap_cdc"`, in `ALL` (:72-80) and `suffix()` (:104-114). It is reserved. `"__apitap_cdc"` is not a suffix of `"__apitap_cdc_del"`; the existing ALL-driven tests pin distinctness.
- Add `pub(crate) fn artifact_ident_tok(bare, artifact, limit, token: &str) -> String`. `artifact_ident_run` (:778-786) becomes a call to it with `run.token()`.
- Add `pub(crate) const FENCE_PREFIX: &str = "_apitap_fence";` and `pub(crate) fn fence_ident(token: &str) -> String { format!("{FENCE_PREFIX}{token}") }`. Tokens start with `_` (:300-306), so this yields `_apitap_fence_…`.
  - `is_artifact` (:834-840) gains `|| name.starts_with("_apitap_fence_")`.
  - `sql_exclusion` (:855-869) gains `AND {col} NOT LIKE '\_apitap\_fence\_%'`, with the Postgres/MySQL escaping already used there.
- `is_artifact` is the existing predicate. No `is_own_object` is added; the typed ops in 3.8 make it unnecessary.

#### 3.4 Sinks (the stores; free functions, both lanes call them)

##### `sink/postgres.rs`

**`resolve_parts(pool: &PgPool, dest_table: &str) -> Result<PgParts>`** (new)

`PgParts{ schema, bare }`, with `qualified() -> String = quote_ident_path("{schema}.{bare}")`.
- A qualified name: split as in :332-340.
- Otherwise: `SELECT n.nspname FROM pg_class c JOIN pg_namespace n ON n.oid=c.relnamespace WHERE c.oid = to_regclass($1)` with `$1 = quote_ident(bare)`. If there is no row: `SELECT current_schema()`. If that is NULL: `Err(InvalidInput("no schema on search_path resolves {bare}"))`.
- This is the rule already at :877-898, lifted out.

Other changes in this file:
- **`lock_ident`** (:315-323) takes `&PgParts` and always emits `"{schema}"."{lock}"`, so the lock is created in the schema the scan reads.
- **`PgSink`**: `dest_state` (:877-898) calls `resolve_parts` and, besides `schema`/`dest_key`, **recomputes `lock_t` qualified**. `bind`'s `"public"` default (:485-490) stays only as the pre-`dest_state` placeholder. `dest_state` runs before `prepare` (pipeline/mod.rs:357 before :440), so the lock is announced qualified.
- **`lease_renew`** (:204-224): the inner select gains `AND NOT collected`.
- **`lease_claim`** (:268-295) returns `Claim`. Same SQL plus `RETURNING 1` via `fetch_optional`:
  - `Some` → `Taken`.
  - 55P03 → `Refused`.
  - `None` → `lease_get`: `None` → `Absent`, `Some` → `Refused`.
  - 42P01 → `Absent`.
- **`lease_unclaimed(pool, schema, token)`**: `SELECT dest_key FROM {lease} WHERE token=$1 AND NOT collected`.
- **`check_peers`** (:52-106) keeps only its listing (:58-75) and becomes E's `GuardStore::list` for Postgres. The decision at :76-104 is deleted (E's loop).

##### `sink/mysql.rs`

- **`lease_renew`** (:153-175): `SET SESSION innodb_lock_wait_timeout = 1` becomes checked (`.map_err(…)?`, replacing `let _` at :155). The UPDATE gains `AND collected = 0`.
- **`lease_claim`** (:206-226) returns `Claim`:
  1. `conn = pool.get_conn()`, then the checked `SET SESSION innodb_lock_wait_timeout = 1`.
  2. `START TRANSACTION`, then `SELECT collected, expires_at <= UTC_TIMESTAMP(6) FROM {lease} WHERE dest_key=? AND token=? FOR UPDATE`.
  3. Outcomes:
     - Error 1205 → `ROLLBACK`, `Refused`.
     - 1146 or no row → `ROLLBACK`, `Absent`.
     - `collected=1` → `COMMIT`, `Taken`.
     - Lapsed → `UPDATE {lease} SET collected=1 WHERE dest_key=? AND token=?`, `COMMIT`, `Taken`.
     - Otherwise → `ROLLBACK`, `Refused`.
  - This deletes :212-213 (`OR collected = 1`) and :220 (`affected_rows`).
- **`lease_unclaimed`**: `SELECT dest_key FROM {lease} WHERE token=? AND collected=0`.

##### `sink/clickhouse.rs`

- `const CLAIM_SEQ_BIAS: u64 = 1 << 62;`
- `pub(crate) fn lease_seq(collected: u8, micros: u64) -> u64 { micros + if collected == 1 { CLAIM_SEQ_BIAS } else { 0 } }`.
- **`ensure_lease_table`** (:59-68): created **without** the TTL line :65. Once per process (memo), if `SELECT engine_full FROM system.tables WHERE database=currentDatabase() AND name='_apitap_lease'` contains `" TTL "`, run `ALTER TABLE _apitap_lease REMOVE TTL`. Reason: an uncollected row must never age out (the weekend wedge), and a collected row must not be deleted while an older unclaimed version survives in another part (TTL is per part).
- **`lease_write`** (:77-98): `seq` = `toUnixTimestamp64Micro(now64(6)) + {bias}` with `bias = CLAIM_SEQ_BIAS` when `collected==1`, else 0. Every claim or close version outranks every renewal version, and `ReplacingMergeTree(seq)` keeps the max on merge. Epoch micros stay below 2^62 until about year 148 000; the sum stays below 2^63.
- **`lease_renew(ch, keys, token)`** (new; dest_ch.rs:173-183 no longer calls `lease_write`). Per key:

```
INSERT INTO `_apitap_lease` (dest_key, token, expires_at, collected, seq)
SELECT '{k}','{tok}', now64(6) + INTERVAL {ttl} SECOND, 0, toUnixTimestamp64Micro(now64(6))
WHERE 1 IN (SELECT toUInt8(count() > 0 AND argMax(collected, seq) = 0)
            FROM `_apitap_lease` WHERE dest_key='{k}' AND token='{tok}')
```

- **`lease_claim`** (:142-150) → `Claim`: `lease_get` → `None` → `Absent`; collected → `Taken`; `expires_in > 0` → `Refused`; otherwise `lease_write(k, tok, ttl, 1)` → `Taken`.
- **`lease_close`** (:152-154): unchanged (`collected=1`, now biased).
- **`lease_unclaimed`**: `SELECT dest_key FROM _apitap_lease WHERE token='{tok}' GROUP BY dest_key HAVING count() > 0 AND argMax(collected, seq) = 0 FORMAT TabSeparatedRaw`.
- **`ChConn::exec_with(query, settings: &[(&str,&str)]) -> Result<String>`** and **`exec_written(query, settings) -> Result<Option<u64>>`**: read the `X-ClickHouse-Summary` header's `written_rows` **before** `resp.text()` (the read at :288). `None` = the header is absent.
- **`insert_stream_with(query, body, settings)`**: same as `insert_stream` (:332-376) plus extra settings.
- `ChConn::read(sql)`: sends `readonly=2`, so the server refuses any write.

##### `sink/bigquery.rs`

- **`cdc_script`** (:703-716): before the `retryable` arm, `Err(Error::Transfer(m)) if m.contains(LOST_MARK) => return Err(lease::no_longer_holds(&[]))`. This goes through a pure `fn script_error_class(m: &str) -> ScriptErr { Lost, Retry, Fail }`.
- The bulk collect (:1718-1745) becomes E's loop calling the same `BqStore` impl. The `Found::Mine => table_delete` at :1710 must use exact-token equality (rule 3.1, owned by C/E).

#### 3.5 `logbased/dest_pg.rs`

New `mod store`:

```rust
pub(super) struct PgStore { pool: PgPool /*2*/, keeper_pool: PgPool /*max_connections(1)*/, parts: Mutex<HashMap<String, PgParts>> }
pub(crate) struct PgUnit { tx: sqlx::Transaction<'static, Postgres>, parts: Vec<PgParts> }
impl PgUnit { pub(super) fn tx(&mut self) -> &mut sqlx::Transaction<'static, Postgres>; pub(super) fn table(&self, i) -> &PgParts }
```

`connect` (:203-210) opens both pools. `lease_renew` and `lease_unclaimed` run on `keeper_pool`, so `APITAP_CDC_APPLY_LANES ≥ 2` (run.rs:467-471) can no longer starve renewal of the 2-connection apply pool (:204-205).

**`open_unit`**:
1. `pool.begin()`.
2. For each key, in sorted order (no deadlock between lanes): `SELECT 1 FROM {lease} WHERE dest_key=$1 AND token=$2 AND NOT collected FOR UPDATE`. No row, or 42P01 → drop the tx, `Err(no_longer_holds)`.
- The expiry clause and the "no row → Ok" arm (:82-96) are gone: a tenure always has a row.

**`close_unit`**:
1. For each mark: `Set` → the `upsert_state_tx` SQL (:617-624). `Clear` → `DELETE FROM _apitap_state WHERE dest_table IN ($1,$2) AND source_id=$3` (from :218, keys from `pg_state_keys`).
2. For each key: `UPDATE {lease} SET expires_at = clock_timestamp() + make_interval(secs => $3) WHERE dest_key=$1 AND token=$2 AND NOT collected`. `rows_affected()==1`, otherwise drop the tx and `Err(no_longer_holds)`.
3. `COMMIT`.
- `clock_timestamp()`, not `now()`, because `now()` is the transaction start (:108). This closes the [medium] TTL-longer-than-apply item.

**Apply and other writes**:
- `apply(&self, u: &mut PgUnit, ctx, outcome) -> Result<(u64, Watermark)>` runs :422-583 on `u.tx()`, with `ft = u.table(0).qualified()` replacing `quote_table(dest_table)` at :414.
- The no-traffic branch :399-407 becomes `Ok((0, Set{end}))` with no statement.
- `bootstrap_finish(u, …)` runs the PK probe and `ALTER … ADD PRIMARY KEY` (:245-275) on `u.tx()`.
- `ensure_state_table` (:297-329) and `read_state` (:331-386) become `store` reads; they create or read, never write data.

**Deleted**: `run_token`/`set_run`/`token` (:17-31), `fence_tx` (:33-118), `announce`/`check_peers`/`release`/`lease_*`/`release_ok` (:120-201; E's `GuardStore` impl lives in `store`), `clear_state` (:212-226), `write_marker` (:282-295), and the begin/fence/state/commit lines :402-405, :418-420, :585-586.

#### 3.6 `logbased/dest_my.rs`

New `mod store`:

```rust
pub(super) struct MyStore { shared: MySqlShared }
pub(crate) struct MyTx { conn: Option<mysql_async::Conn>, key: Vec<String>, token: String, in_tx: bool, twins: bool }
```

`open_unit`:
1. `conn = shared.conn()`.
2. The session `SET` at :472-476.
3. `SET SESSION innodb_lock_wait_timeout = 50` (explicit default).
- No transaction yet.

`MyTx` operations — the only way to reach the connection:

| Op | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| `temp_ddl(&mut self, sql)` | Only `CREATE/DROP TEMPORARY TABLE`. `Err` if `in_tx`. Autocommit (no user data). Sets `twins`. |
| `begin(&mut self)` | Idempotent. `START TRANSACTION`, then per key `SELECT 1 FROM {lease} WHERE dest_key=? AND token=? AND collected=0 FOR UPDATE`. No row → `Err(no_longer_holds)`. Called implicitly by `exec`, `load` and `query_first`. |
| `exec(sql)`, `load(infile_id, sql)`, `no_warnings(what)`, `query_first/query` | On the fenced transaction. |
| `owned_ddl(&mut self, sql)` | Used for `TRUNCATE TABLE {ft}` and `ALTER TABLE {ft} ADD PRIMARY KEY (…)`. `Err` if `in_tx`. Runs, in order: `begin()`; per key `UPDATE {lease} SET expires_at = UTC_TIMESTAMP(6) + INTERVAL {ttl} SECOND WHERE dest_key=? AND token=? AND collected=0`; `SELECT collected FROM {lease} WHERE dest_key=? AND token=?` must be 0; `SET SESSION lock_wait_timeout = {owned_margin_secs()}`; `{sql}` (its implicit commit publishes the renewal, then the DDL runs); `SET SESSION lock_wait_timeout = DEFAULT`; `in_tx = false`. The statement list is built by the pure `fn my_owned_ddl_plan(lease_t, keys, token, ttl, margin, sql) -> Vec<String>`. |
| `Drop` | If the connection is still held: `tokio::spawn(async move { ROLLBACK; DROP TEMPORARY TABLE IF EXISTS _ap_del, _ap_up })`. |

`close_unit`:
1. `begin()`.
2. For each `Set`: `state_upsert_sql` (:705-722). For each `Clear`: `DELETE FROM _apitap_state WHERE dest_table=? AND source_id=?`.
3. Per key: the renew UPDATE with `collected=0`, then `SELECT collected …` must be 0. The verdict comes from a read, never `affected_rows`.
4. `COMMIT`, then `DROP TEMPORARY TABLE IF EXISTS _ap_del, _ap_up` if `twins` (:671-674).

`apply(u: &mut MyTx, …)`:
- `if c.truncate { u.owned_ddl("TRUNCATE TABLE {ft}") }` before any `begin` (replacing :478-482).
- The twins (:484-525) are created via `temp_ddl`, **before** the transaction.
- :537-664 runs on `u.exec` / `u.load`.
- The no-traffic branch :430-435 becomes `Ok((0, Set{end}))`.

`bootstrap_finish(u, …)`: the has-PK probe (:327-336), then `u.owned_ddl("ALTER TABLE {ft} ADD PRIMARY KEY ({pk})")`.

Module doc :1-6 is rewritten: "A WAL TRUNCATE is applied O(1) under the fence: the lease row is renewed while held, and the TRUNCATE's implicit commit publishes that renewal as the statement begins."

**Deleted**: `run_token`/`set_run` (:26-28, 88-90), `fence_tx` (:128-184), `check_peers`/`release`/`release_ok` (:208-272; listing moves to E's `GuardStore`), `clear_state` (:359-379), `write_marker` (:381-396), the pre-tx state write (:430-435), the tx begin and fence (:527-535), and the state write and commit (:666-669).

#### 3.7 `logbased/dest_ch.rs`

New `mod store`:

```rust
pub(super) struct ChStore { ch: ChConn, ensured: Mutex<HashSet<String>>, patch: Mutex<Option<bool>>,
                            structures: Mutex<HashMap<String /*table*/, HashMap<String,String> /*col->type*/>> }
pub(crate) struct ChUnit<'a> { s: &'a ChStore, keys: Vec<String>, token: String, ops: Vec<&'static str> /* test log */ }
pub(super) fn owner_pred(key: &str, tok: &str, margin: u64) -> String
// = "1 IN (SELECT toUInt8(count() > 0 AND argMax(collected, seq) = 0 AND \
//     argMax(expires_at, seq) > now64(6) + INTERVAL {margin} SECOND) \
//     FROM `_apitap_lease` WHERE dest_key = '{ch_str(key)}' AND token = '{ch_str(tok)}')"
fn owned_settings() -> [(&'static str, String); 2]  // max_execution_time, http_receive_timeout = owned_margin_secs()
```

The IN-subquery form is the shape the lane already runs inside a lightweight DELETE (dest_ch.rs:1082-1094). For a multi-key unit, `pred` = the `AND` of each key's predicate.

**`open_unit`**: for each key, `lease_get(key, token)`. `None`, `collected`, or `expires_in ≤ owned_margin_secs()` → `Err(no_longer_holds)`. This replaces `check_still_mine` (:143-154), `lease_still_mine` (:199-212) and their calls at :967, :982, :998, :1258.

`ChUnit` operations — typed, no string allowlist; every op pushes to `ops`:

| Op | SQL |
|---|---|
| `insert_owned(table, cols, body)` | `INSERT INTO {t} ({cols}) SELECT {cols} FROM input('{S}') WHERE {pred} FORMAT TabSeparated` via `insert_stream_with(…, owned_settings())`. `S = ch_str(cols.map(\|c\| "{ch_ident(c)} {type}").join(", "))`. Types come from `SELECT name, type FROM system.columns WHERE database=currentDatabase() AND table='{t}' FORMAT TabSeparatedRaw` (memoized; invalidated by `changelog_rebuild` and `key_table_reset`). Replaces the plain inserts at :969-974, 1076-1081, 1102-1107, 1164-1169, 1180-1185, 1246-1251. |
| `delete_owned(table, where_sql)` | `DELETE FROM {t} WHERE ({where}) AND {pred}{lightweight_mode}` via `exec_with(owned_settings())`. Replaces :1092-1094, 1161, 1177, 1189, 1243. |
| `clear_owned(table)` | `DELETE FROM {t} WHERE {pred}{mode}`. Replaces `TRUNCATE TABLE {ft}` at :1021. |
| `key_table()` | Returns `artifact_ident_tok(table, CdcDelete, ROOMY, token)`, run-scoped. Replaces :1027-1028. |
| `key_table_reset(table, pk)` | First time per run: `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS {kt}`, then `CREATE TABLE {kt} ENGINE=MergeTree ORDER BY tuple() AS SELECT {pk} FROM {ft} WHERE 0`, then (if patch) `ALTER TABLE {ft} MODIFY SETTING enable_block_number_column=1, enable_block_offset_column=1`. Otherwise `TRUNCATE TABLE {kt}` (:1048-1067). The name is computed here, never passed in. |
| `mark_pending_owned(…)` | Columns decided by **B**. `INSERT INTO _apitap_cdc_pending (…) SELECT … WHERE {pred}` via `exec_written`: `written_rows == 1`, otherwise `Err(no_longer_holds)`. |
| `changelog_rebuild(table, sel, part, order, lsn)` | See below. Replaces :576-599. |
| `current_view(table, pk)` | :667-696. |
| `read(sql)` | `ChConn::read` with `readonly=2`. Used for `read_current`, `appended_prefix`, `pending_window`, residue readbacks. |

`changelog_rebuild` sequence:
1. `tmp = artifact_ident_tok(table, ChangelogTmp, ROOMY, token)`.
2. `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS {tmp}`, then `CREATE TABLE {tmp} … AS SELECT … FROM {ft}` (non-destructive).
3. `assert_owned()` (the `open_unit` read), then `EXCHANGE TABLES {tmp} AND {ft}` with `owned_settings()`.
4. `assert_owned()`, then `DROP TABLE {tmp}` (now the old replica shape).
5. If the second `assert_owned` fails, return `Locked` naming `{tmp}`, and do **not** drop it.

`close_unit`:
- `Set` → `INSERT INTO _apitap_state (dest_table, source_id, cursor_col, watermark, mode, last_rows) SELECT '{dt}','{sid}','_lsn','{lsn}','log_based',{rows} WHERE {pred}` via `exec_written`. Handling of the result:
  - `Some(1)` → ok.
  - `Some(0)` → `Err(no_longer_holds)`.
  - `None` (a proxy stripped the header) → confirm with `SELECT argMax(watermark, synced_at) FROM _apitap_state WHERE dest_table='{dt}' AND source_id='{sid}'` == `'{lsn}'`. A window is only sent when `end > cur` (run.rs:1693), so this value differs from the previous one.
- `Clear` → `ALTER TABLE _apitap_state DELETE WHERE dest_table='{dt}' AND source_id='{sid}' AND {pred} SETTINGS mutations_sync=1` (from :641-651).

Parent-module apply bodies:
- **`apply_changelog(u, …)`**: in B's order, `u.read(appended_prefix)` → B's plan → `u.mark_pending_owned` → `u.insert_owned`. Returns `(count, Set{plan.watermark()})`.
- **`apply(u, …)`**: `if c.truncate { u.clear_owned(t) }` → `key_table_reset` → `insert_owned(key_table)` → `delete_owned(ft, "{pk} IN (SELECT {pk} FROM {kt})")` → `insert_owned(ft)` → residue via `read` / `delete_owned` / `insert_owned`. Returns `(events, Set{end})`.
- The no-traffic branches :847-849, :867-869, :1001-1004 return `(0, Set{end})` with no statement.

`sweep_run(t, tok)` drops `artifact_ident_tok(t, CdcDelete|ChangelogTmp, ROOMY, tok)`. `bootstrap_finish` additionally drops the legacy untokenized `artifact_ident(t, CdcDelete, ROOMY)` and `artifact_ident(t, ChangelogTmp, ROOMY)`; while a table bootstraps, the guard excludes any live drain.

**Deleted**: `run_token`/`set_run` (:50-53, 139-141), `check_still_mine`/`lease_still_mine` and the four calls, `check_peers`/`release`/`release_ok` (:239-301), public `write_state` (:732-754) and its six calls (:601, 848, 868, 925, 984, 1003, 1259), `write_marker` (:723-730), and `clear_state` (:641-651, moved into `close_unit`).

#### 3.8 `logbased/dest_bq.rs`

New `mod store`:

```rust
pub(super) struct BqStore { conn: BqConn }
pub(crate) struct BqUnit<'a> { s: &'a BqStore, token: String, keys: Vec<String>,
                               groups: Vec<(String /*table*/, Vec<String>)> }
pub(super) fn fenced_script(fence_fq: &str, token: &str, chunk: &[String]) -> String
// "BEGIN TRANSACTION;\n\
//  UPDATE {fence_fq} SET n = n + 1 WHERE NOT claimed;\n\
//  IF @@row_count <> 1 THEN RAISE USING MESSAGE = '{LOST_MARK}: run {token} no longer holds its tables'; END IF;\n\
//  {chunk joined by \n}\n\
//  COMMIT TRANSACTION;"
```

**Fence table**: `lease_open` also runs, via `cdc_script`, `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS {fq(fence_ident(tok))} (claimed BOOL, n INT64); INSERT INTO … SELECT FALSE, 0 FROM UNNEST([1]) WHERE NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM …);`. This happens before the lock (it sits inside "lease before lock"). `close_run(tok)` → `table_delete(fence_ident(tok))`.

**`lease_claim`** (replaces :119-135 and :240-253) → `Claim`:
1. `cdc_script("UPDATE {lease} SET collected = TRUE WHERE dest_key='{k}' AND token='{tok}' AND (expires_at <= CURRENT_TIMESTAMP() OR collected);")`.
2. `lease_get`: `None` → `Absent`; not collected → `Refused`.
3. Collected → **fence the victim run**: `cdc_script("UPDATE {fq(fence_ident(tok))} SET claimed = TRUE WHERE TRUE;")`. A "Not found" is ignored (a 0.56.0 victim, or already swept). This standalone DML is queued behind a victim transaction that has already mutated the fence, and returns only after it ends.
4. `table_delete(fence_ident(tok))`, then `Taken`.

Store queries:
- `lease_renew` (:196-211): `WHERE token='{tok}' AND NOT collected`.
- `lease_unclaimed`: `SELECT dest_key FROM {lease} WHERE token='{tok}' AND NOT collected`.

**`open_unit`**: no I/O.

**Unit ops**:
- `load(table, fields, ndjson)` → `cdc_load_ndjson(artifact_ident_tok(table, CdcStaging, ROOMY, tok), …)`. Replaces `cdc_staging()` at :86-88, used at :398, 498, 844, 879, 1162, 1307; `merge_sql` (:1275) takes the staging name as a parameter.
- `push(table, stmts)` appends to that table's group, creating the group if absent. B's marker statements go here, before data.
- `query(sql)` for reads.

**`close_unit`**:
1. Each `Set` → append `"{state_insert_sql};"` (:1197-1209) **to that table's group**. `Clear` → append `DELETE FROM {state} WHERE dest_table='{dt}' AND source_id='{sid}';`.
2. `for chunk in pack_whole_groups(groups, CHUNK_BYTES)` (:53-69, now also used by the replica path) run `cdc_script(fenced_script(…))`. `Err` with `LOST_MARK`, or "Not found" naming the fence table → `Err(no_longer_holds)`.
- The ';' check at :1014-1020 moves into `fenced_script`'s caller.

**Guarded DDL** (bootstrap only, non-transactional). `guarded_ddl_script(tok, keys, body)` prepends:

```
ASSERT (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM {fence} WHERE NOT claimed) = 1
   AND (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM {lease} WHERE token='{tok}' AND dest_key IN ({keys})
        AND NOT collected AND expires_at > TIMESTAMP_ADD(CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(), INTERVAL {margin} SECOND)) = {N}
   AS '{LOST_MARK}: …';
```

It is used for:
- the changelog rebuild (:576-594) and `cluster_target` (:910-919), with `tmp = artifact_ident_tok(t, ChangelogTmp, ROOMY, tok)`;
- the heal path (:505-517).

The swap is one script that is idempotent under `cdc_script`'s whole-script retry:

```
IF (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM `{ds}`.INFORMATION_SCHEMA.TABLES WHERE table_name='{tmp}') = 1 THEN
  DROP TABLE IF EXISTS {t};
  ALTER TABLE {tmp} RENAME TO `{table}`;
END IF;
```

The CTAS stays a separate job, as today (:568-575 argument).

Bodies:

#### 3.10 `logbased/run.rs` and `myrun.rs`

`impl LeaseStore for Dest`, `impl GuardStore for Dest` and `impl Fence for Dest` dispatch by enum (`SERIAL_COMMIT` is true iff the variant is `Bq`; it is a const, so it is spelled as a `fn serial_commit(&self)` checked by `Tenure::close`). The unit type is `enum Unit<'a> { Pg(PgUnit), My(MyTx), Ch(ChUnit<'a>), Bq(BqUnit<'a>), Ice(IceUnit) }`. `Dest::apply` / `apply_no_src` / `bootstrap_finish` take `&mut Unit<'_>`; a variant mismatch is `Err(Transfer("internal: unit/dest mismatch"))`.

**Deleted**: `Dest::announce`/`set_run`/`lease_open`/`lease_renew`/`lease_close`/`lease_key`/`release_ok`/`check_peers`/`release` (:135-257) and `write_marker`/`clear_state`/`bootstrap_finish` (:298-368; the last becomes the unit-taking dispatch).

**`run_group`**:
- `let tenure = Arc::new(Tenure::acquire(dest.clone(), &dest_tables, run).await?);` replaces :905-953.
- The `out = async {…}` arm (:960-1028) stays, and `tenure.release().await; out` replaces :1029-1044.
- The handover :990-1011 is deleted. `bootstrap_group(…, &tenure)` loads through C's `transfer_within(tenure.run(), …)` at :1419.
- Each finish (:1462-1472): `let mut h = tenure.open(&[dt]).await?; let (n, m) = dest.bootstrap_finish(&mut h.unit, …).await?; tenure.close(h, vec![m]).await`.
- The rollback (:1476-1478): the same pattern with `vec![Clear{…}]`, best-effort.

**`run_group_mysql`**: the same, replacing :1138-1180, :1211-1223 and :1323-1338.
- `stamp` (:1128-1133) becomes `tenure.open(&[t])` → `tenure.close(h, vec![Set{table: t, source_id: marker_id, lsn: server, rows: 0}])`.
- The `drain_windows` closure (:1288-1315): per member `open` → `apply_no_src(&mut h.unit, …)` → `close(h, vec![m])`.

**`drain_group`** takes `tenure: Arc<Tenure<Dest>>`. Extract:

```rust
async fn run_overlapped<D, A>(drain: D, apply: A) -> Result<Vec<u64>>
// D: the loop body (:1667-1714 + :1717-1722) as `async { …; Ok::<(), Error>(()) }`
// A: JoinHandle wrapped in `struct AbortOnDrop(Option<JoinHandle<Result<Vec<u64>>>>)` (Drop → abort)
```

Order: `let loop_res = drain.await;` → `drop(win_tx)` (unconditional) → `let joined = apply.take().await` → `ws.stop_replication().await.ok()` → `loop_res?; drain_err?; joined…`. The `?` at :1705 and :1720 now live inside `drain`; they can no longer return past the join.

The apply task per window:
- `Bq`: `tenure.open(all)` → `apply_group` → `close(all marks)`.
- Lanes > 1: per-table `open`/`close` inside `buffer_unordered`.
- Otherwise: serial per-table `open`/`close`.

#### 3.11 Constants

| Constant | Value | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `CLAIM_SEQ_BIAS` | 1 << 62 | Every claim/close version outranks every renewal until year ≈ 148 000. |
| `owned_margin_secs()` | `ttl/2` | A ClickHouse statement or BigQuery DDL runs only with more than half a TTL of life left. Server-bounded to half a TTL, so a claim (which needs `expires_at ≤ now`) cannot land before it ends. |
| Keeper pool | 1 connection | One renewal in flight is all a keeper needs. |
| `ttl_secs` / `renew_secs` | unchanged (lease.rs:81-99) | |
| `CHUNK_BYTES` | 256 KiB, unchanged (:41) | |
| `LOST_MARK` | `"apitap-lease-lost"` | Substring carried by the BigQuery RAISE and ASSERT. |

No knob is added.

#### 3.12 Implementation order (after E's `guard.rs` lands; before B)

1. `lease.rs` types, Tenure, keeper, and unit tests RED with a fake `Fence`.
2. Sink stores.
3. `naming` additions.
4. Each `dest_*::store`, its `Fence` impl, and unit-taking bodies.
5. `run.rs`/`myrun` wiring and `run_overlapped`.
6. Legs RED on the 0.56.0 wheel, then GREEN; gate rows.
7. Docs.

B lands on top: its marker and trim are unit ops.

### 4. Invariants and the argument

**I1 — The only writable handle is a Unit, and the only Unit constructor is `Tenure::open`.** Connection types live only in `dest_*::store`; the parent module has no field it could write through. `COMMIT`, `START TRANSACTION` and `ROLLBACK` literals exist only in `store`. Both are pinned by `no_connection_types_outside_store`.

**I2 — Ownership = the row exists ∧ ¬collected, on every store.**
- `open_unit` / `begin` refuse a missing row. The 0.56.0 `None → Ok` at dest_pg.rs:94-95 and dest_my.rs:164-166 is gone, and so is §3.10's "row gone ⇒ unfenced".
- A lapsed-but-unclaimed drain keeps writing on Postgres, MySQL and BigQuery data scripts. On ClickHouse, and in BigQuery DDL, it must also have more than ttl/2 of life left (the time fence). Owning with less stops the drain loudly with nothing written (§5).

**I3 — Claims are monotonic and read-decided.**
- Postgres: `RETURNING`.
- MySQL: `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` in the claim transaction. Result 1205 → `Refused`; `CLIENT_FOUND_ROWS` is irrelevant, so §3.9's dead disjunct becomes a `Taken` on re-entry.
- ClickHouse: a biased row. Renewal is predicated on `count()>0 ∧ ¬collected`; there is no TTL; `ReplacingMergeTree(seq)` keeps the max seq, and the max is a claim.
- BigQuery: renewal has `AND NOT collected`.

Walks for the attack traces:
- **ClickHouse day-long pause.** No TTL removes rows. If an operator deletes them anyway, `count()>0` fails in all three predicates, so the victim neither renews nor passes `OWNER_PRED`.
- **ClickHouse weekend wedge.** The uncollected row survives; Monday's run sees it lapsed → `Taken`.
- **§3.2.** The victim's renewal after the claim evaluates `argMax(collected)=1` and inserts nothing. Even if it raced the claim, its unbiased seq loses.

**I4 — A write is fenced by the destination.**
- **Postgres / MySQL.** The row is locked from `open_unit`/`begin` to COMMIT. The claim's NOWAIT or 1 s wait refuses while it is held. The renewal is the last statement, stamped `clock_timestamp()` / `UTC_TIMESTAMP(6)`, so a unit longer than the TTL commits with a fresh lease.
- **MySQL TRUNCATE / ADD PK (§3.8).** The fence row is held from `begin` until the DDL's implicit commit, which MySQL performs before the statement opens tables and takes MDL. The renewal published by that commit gives ttl more life, so no claim can be taken for ttl. The DDL's MDL wait is capped at ttl/2 (`lock_wait_timeout`). A peer can start writing only after its claim, drop, state read, drain and fence — all after A's MDL-X request, which blocks new shared-write MDL. So A's truncate precedes any peer write, and the peer replays the TRUNCATE event from the same watermark.
  - Residual: the server-internal gap between the implicit commit and the MDL-X request inside one TRUNCATE statement.
  - If A's session is killed while paused (`wait_timeout`), the renewal rolls back and the next statement fails on the dead socket; `mysql_async` does not silently reconnect a held `Conn`.
- **ClickHouse.** Every INSERT and DELETE carries `OWNER_PRED` with the ttl/2 margin and runs with `max_execution_time = http_receive_timeout = ttl/2`. The predicate is true at analysis ⇒ the lease has more than ttl/2 left ⇒ no claim before `expires_at` ⇒ the statement ends or is killed first. Watermark and pending marker are predicated INSERTs confirmed by `written_rows`.
  - Residual: a lightweight-DELETE mutation killed by the timeout may finish in the background. It only touches parts that existed when it was created, for keys the next owner deletes and re-inserts in its replay of the same window.
- **BigQuery.** A script's first statement mutates `_apitap_fence<tok>`; a collector marks that same table claimed. Case by case:
  - The claim committed before the script's BEGIN → the fence UPDATE matches 0 rows → RAISE → rollback.
  - The claim committed between BEGIN and the fence UPDATE → write–write conflict at COMMIT ("concurrent update") → `cdc_script` retries → 0 rows → `LOST_MARK` → `Locked`.
  - A claim arriving after the fence UPDATE is standalone DML, queued behind the transaction; its `cdc_script` returns only afterwards, and the collector's first write follows it. The script therefore committed as the owner, and the collector reads its watermark.
  - Sibling drains' scripts mutate different fence tables; keepers write only `_apitap_lease`, which no script touches. This closes both livelock traces, and the keeper is never quiet.
  - The guard depends on documented BigQuery concurrency: conflicting transactions are cancelled; conflicting non-transaction DML is queued.

**I5 — `release` cannot pass an open unit.** `release` takes `units.write()`; every `Held` carries a read guard. `run_overlapped` awaits the apply task on every path, and `AbortOnDrop` aborts it if `drain_group`'s future is dropped. Two independent locks close §3.10: the apply task is joined, and a detached apply cannot open a unit after `closing`, nor finish one past `release`.

**I6 — Keeper lifetime.** The keeper renews until `release`. `shutdown::requested()` is ignored (closes the [low] item). `Drop` aborts it (closes the [low] panic item). `evicted` is set only when `lease_unclaimed` omits a key — a claim or deletion, never expiry. On Postgres it cannot be starved, having its own pool.

**I7 — Scratch is run-scoped.** The ClickHouse key table and changelog tmp, and the BigQuery staging, changelog tmp and fence, all carry the exact token. They are swept by the owner's `release` and by the collector after a `Taken` claim. Two runs never share one; the 0.56.0 shared `t__apitap_cdc` and `…__apitap_cdc_del` could each be wiped by the other run.

**I8 — Postgres schema.** The lock, lease and scan all use `resolve_parts`, which is also the table's namespace for data statements. A bulk run and a drain under any `search_path` agree, including a table in the 2nd entry and a `"$user"` schema.

**I9 — Bootstrap is owned.** Under C's lineage the lock is never dropped. `bootstrap_finish` and `clear_state` are units; there is no handover gap (closes the [low] item at run.rs:990-1011).
- ClickHouse rebuild: tokenized tmp; `assert_owned` before `EXCHANGE` and again before dropping the old copy. A pause between an assert and the EXCHANGE arriving leaves both tables intact and named in the error, instead of today's `DROP ft`.
- BigQuery rebuild: the ASSERT runs server-side in the same script as the swap, so a client pause cannot separate them.

**I10 — The watermark travels with its data.**
- BigQuery: the state row is appended to its table's group; `pack_whole_groups` never splits a group.
- Iceberg: `cdc_commit` carries it.
- Postgres / MySQL: the same transaction.
- ClickHouse: pending marker < data insert < state (B's replay argument).

### 5. Per engine

| Engine | Mechanism | What is weaker there, honestly |
|---|---|---|
| Postgres | Row lock `FOR UPDATE … NOT collected` from `open_unit` to COMMIT. State and `clock_timestamp()` renewal are the last statements. Claim is `NOWAIT RETURNING`. Keeper on its own 1-connection pool. Schema via `to_regclass`. | Nothing structural. One schema lookup per member per run. |
| MySQL / MariaDB | Row lock, taken on the first data op or at close. Renewal plus a `SELECT collected` read before COMMIT. TRUNCATE and ADD PK via renew-then-DDL on the same connection, MDL wait capped at ttl/2. Claim is `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` with 1 s wait. | TRUNCATE ownership relies on the implicit commit preceding MDL acquisition inside one statement: a server-internal gap, not a lock. The temp twins are autocommit (they hold no user data). |
| ClickHouse | Predicate inside every INSERT/DELETE (`count()>0 ∧ ¬collected ∧ life>ttl/2`). Statements server-bounded to ttl/2. Watermark and marker confirmed by `written_rows`. Biased claim rows, no TTL, predicated renewal. | A time fence, not a lock. It depends on the server clock and on `max_execution_time` being enforced. A killed lightweight-DELETE mutation may complete in the background (§4 I4). WAL TRUNCATE is a lightweight DELETE (O(rows), masked rows until merge). A drain whose keeper has failed for ttl/2 stops, where Postgres would continue. `_apitap_lease` rows are no longer aged out (one row per run per table after merges, like the other engines). |
| BigQuery | Per-run fence table mutated as the script's first statement; the collector marks it claimed as standalone DML (queued behind any open fenced transaction). `LOST_MARK` is never retried. Serialized commits within one run. Keeper always on. DDL guarded by a server-side ASSERT in the same script. | Relies on BigQuery's documented conflict and queue rules, which the client cannot assert. A collector can wait for one in-flight script (≤ the 6 h transaction cap). Each chunk is its own transaction: a fence failure on chunk 2 leaves chunk 1 committed, and the next owner replays it. +1 DML child job per chunk. |
| Iceberg | The single `Unguarded` store. `close_unit` = `cdc_commit` (data plus watermark) or `cdc_set_watermark`. | Unguarded by design (run.rs:144-150; failure-modes.md:353-359). |

### 6. Tests

Each test must be seen RED with its control before the fix counts.

#### Unit tests

| Test | Asserts | RED control |
|---|---|---|
| `lease::tests::open_refuses_after_eviction` | Fake `Fence` counting calls. After `evicted=true`, `open` returns `Locked` and `open_unit` was called 0 times. | Remove the `evicted` check → count 1. |
| `lease::tests::release_waits_for_open_units` | A `Held` whose holder awaits a oneshot; `release` spawned concurrently. The event log is exactly `[open_unit, close_unit, keeper_stop, drop_marker, sweep_run, lease_close, close_run]`. | Remove `units.write()` → `drop_marker` before `close_unit`. |
| `lease::tests::keeper_flags_claim_not_expiry` | Key A collected → `evicted`. Key B only `expires_in=-1` → not evicted. | Fake `lease_unclaimed` filtering on `lapsed()` → second assertion fails. |
