#### New e2e legs

Each leg asks the server; none trusts an exit code or a printed line.

- **`e2e_cdc_lease_paused.py`** (MariaDB→ClickHouse, TTL=30, capability `mariadb`):
  1. Drain A with a 200k-row backlog; `kill -STOP` once its lock appears. After TTL+3 s drain B completes.
  2. `SELECT argMax(collected, seq) FROM _apitap_lease WHERE token='<A>'` = 1.
  3. `OPTIMIZE TABLE _apitap_lease FINAL`, then again = 1.
  4. `kill -CONT` A. A exits non-zero with "no longer holds". After 3×renew_secs the value is still 1.
  5. `argMax(watermark, synced_at)` for T equals the value read right after B.
  6. `SELECT count(), uniqExact(id) FROM T` are equal and equal the MariaDB count.
  7. Sub-leg: create `_apitap_lease` with the 0.56.0 DDL (TTL); run a drain; `engine_full` lacks `TTL`.
  - RED on 0.56.0: the flag returns to 0 (dest_ch.rs:178-179), the watermark moves after B, and `count > uniqExact`.
- **`e2e_cdc_lease_bq.py`** (pg→BigQuery, `bq`):
  - Leg 1 mirrors `e2e_cdc_lease.py`: kill → refused → after TTL collected.
  - Leg 2: `STOP` A mid-backlog; after TTL run B, which collects. The server shows the fence table `_apitap_fence<A>` gone and `collected=TRUE`. `CONT` A → A exits "no longer holds". `SELECT COUNT(*)` of T equals the source; no duplicate PK (`COUNT(*) = COUNT(DISTINCT id)`). `INFORMATION_SCHEMA.TABLES` has no `T_<A>__apitap_cdc`.
  - RED on 0.56.0: A commits further scripts, exits 0, and its staging overlaps B's.
- **`e2e_bq_multi_drain.py`** (`bq`): 4 drains on 4 tables of one dataset, looping for 10 min with a source writer.
  - 0 non-zero exits.
  - `JOBS_BY_USER` count of jobs with error "concurrent update" is logged; `LOST_MARK` count = 0.
  - Each table count equals its source.
  - MERGE-longer-than-TTL cell: `APITAP_LEASE_TTL_SECS=30` with a target whose MERGE exceeds 30 s → completes with 0 non-zero exits.
  - RED: the winning design's `_apitap_lease` fence produces non-zero exits.
- **`e2e_cdc_fence_my.py`** (pg→MySQL):
  - Leg 1: evict A by `UPDATE _apitap_lease SET collected=1` at lock appearance; `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM T` is unchanged after A exits.
  - Leg 2 (§3.8): queue `TRUNCATE T; INSERT 5000`; `STOP` A after its lock; after TTL run B; `CONT` A; MySQL count = Postgres count. RED: A's :481 truncate empties B's rows.
  - Leg 3 (§3.9): plant `('db.T','_0000000l000abcd', UTC_TIMESTAMP(6)-INTERVAL 1 SECOND, 1)` and the lock table; the drain proceeds; `information_schema.tables` count of that lock = 0. RED: refused "Nothing for you to do".
  - Leg 4: truncate a 2M-row table; `SELECT COUNT(*)` = post-truncate rows, and `SHOW ENGINE INNODB STATUS` history length did not grow by ≥ 2M (O(1) proof).
- **`e2e_my_gtid_dest.py`** (new capability `mysql-gtid`: container `apitap-bench-my-gtid` with `--gtid-mode=ON --enforce-gtid-consistency=ON`): pg→MySQL drain with upserts runs; `@@gtid_executed` advanced. RED: twins inside the tx → error 1787.
- **`e2e_cdc_apply_orphan.py`** (pg→pg, new capability `cpu` read from cgroup `cpu.max`, needs > 0.6): slow-apply trigger; after the first `synced_at` advance, `pg_terminate_backend` the walsender. `transfer()` raises; for 20 s `synced_at` stays ≤ the exception timestamp; the lock is gone only after the last state advance; the next run is not refused "BEHIND". RED: an orphan commit after the raise.

#### Existing legs that must change

- **`e2e_cdc_fence.py`**:
  - Replace the tautology at :180-182: the evicting `UPDATE … RETURNING clock_timestamp()` gives `t_ev`; assert `SELECT count(*) FROM _apitap_state WHERE dest_table=T AND synced_at > t_ev` = 0.
  - Add leg 4 (keeper starvation): 2 tables, `APITAP_CDC_APPLY_LANES=2`, `BEFORE INSERT` trigger `pg_sleep(0.005)`. Three samples 10 s apart of `max(now()-expires_at)` for token A are < 0. RED: 0.56.0 pool starvation.
- **`e2e_cdc_guard.py`**: add search_path legs (a) table in `cdcdest` first, (b) table only in `public` with `search_path = cdcdest, public`, (c) `"$user"` schema present.
  - Each: drain A running → `transfer(mode='replace')` raises `LockedError`.
  - `pg_class` / `pg_namespace` shows the lock in the table's own schema.
  - RED: 0.56.0 scans `public` only.
- **`e2e_cdc_lease.py`** (pg→ClickHouse): additionally assert the tokenized key table `T_<tok>__apitap_cdc_del` of the killed run is gone after collection.
- **ClickHouse throughput receipt**: pg→ClickHouse CDC 10M at 0.5 cpu / 256 MB, 0.56.0 vs 0.57.0, window apply seconds recorded in `benchmarks/profiling.md`. A regression > 10 % is reported to the owner before release, not shipped silently.

#### `gate.py` rows

Add `e2e_cdc_lease_paused.py` (mariadb), `e2e_cdc_lease_bq.py` (bq), `e2e_bq_multi_drain.py` (bq), `e2e_cdc_fence_my.py` (none), `e2e_my_gtid_dest.py` (mysql-gtid), `e2e_cdc_apply_orphan.py` (cpu). `capabilities()` (:122-142) gains `cpu` and `mysql-gtid`.

### 7. Docs

- **failure-modes.md:335-343** becomes:
  - "Postgres and MySQL fence each window with a row lock on the lease, held from the first statement to COMMIT.
  - ClickHouse evaluates the ownership predicate inside every data statement and inside the watermark INSERT, requires more than half a TTL of life, and bounds each such statement to half a TTL on the server — so an evicted drain writes at most the statement already running.
  - BigQuery fences every commit script on a per-run table that a collector marks before it proceeds — an evicted drain's next script rolls back whole.
  - Iceberg is unguarded."
- **failure-modes.md:346-351** "a partition longer than the TTL now ENDS a drain" becomes "a partition long enough for a peer to CLAIM the lease ends a drain at its next write; a lapse with no claimant does not (on ClickHouse, a lapse past half the TTL stops it loudly)".
- **failure-modes.md, new paragraph:** "Claims are monotonic on every store and decided by reading the row. The bootstrap keeps its lock and lease for the whole first run. A source TRUNCATE on MySQL stays O(1) and is applied under the fence."
- **stability.md:25**, add: "once a peer has collected a drain's claim, that drain writes nothing more on any guarded destination, watermark included." Rolling upgrade: stop every 0.56.0 BigQuery drain before any 0.57.0 run of the same dataset. On ClickHouse a 0.56.0 collector against a 0.56.0 victim keeps the pre-0.57 behaviour. A dead 0.56.0 Postgres drain under a non-`public` search_path leaves a lease 0.57.0 cannot find; drop its lock by hand.
- **README.md:204-205**: "A drain also renews a lease while it runs, and every write it makes — rows and watermark — is conditional on that lease still being its own."
- **lease.rs** module doc (:37-48) and **dest_my.rs:1-6**: as in 3.2 and 3.6. The dest_ch.rs:1-9 module doc and sink/clickhouse.rs:48-58 and 136-141 comments are rewritten to the predicated fence and no-TTL.
- **usage.md**, CDC section: "A source TRUNCATE on a ClickHouse destination is applied as a lightweight row delete, so its cost is proportional to the table. On MySQL it stays a TRUNCATE."
- **docs/design/log_based.md**, new "Tenure" section: the traits, `open`/`close`, `owner_pred`, `fenced_script`, `my_owned_ddl_plan`, the `mod store` boundary, and "engine modules spell statements; lease.rs and guard.rs decide".

### 8. What this does not fix

- **ClickHouse** remains a time fence. A statement killed at ttl/2 whose lightweight-delete mutation continues in the background, and server clock steps, are residual. WAL TRUNCATE is O(rows).
- **MySQL TRUNCATE:** the implicit-commit-to-MDL gap inside one statement is a residual.
- **BigQuery:** correctness rests on documented conflict and queue semantics. A collector may wait for one in-flight script. Chunk-level partial commit is unchanged. A 0.56.0 BigQuery victim beside a 0.57.0 collector is still two winners (procedure in §7).
- **Iceberg** stays unguarded. Bulk-lane locks still carry no lease.
- **Postgres bulk staging** is still created unqualified (lands in `current_schema()`) while the scan uses the resolved schema. Only the lock is qualified here. `_apitap_state` on the CDC lane stays unqualified; moving it would orphan existing watermarks.
- `_apitap_lease` rows are never garbage-collected on any engine, now including ClickHouse ([low]).
- **Cross-cluster**, not decided here:
  - the MySQL `read_state` mode filter (§3.7);
  - replay, trim and marker values, and the BigQuery `jobId` (§3.12, §3.14 — B);
  - bootstrap lineage and `is_self` (C);
  - the rolling-upgrade shadow marker (§3.5 — E);
  - the Layout cache (§3.4 — B).
- The `mod store` boundary against a future `pub(super)` raw writer is pinned only by the lint test and review.

---

## §2.B Apply contracts shared across the five destinations

### 1. Root cause

The drain gives each destination a loose set of parallel maps: `DrainOutcome { tables, changes, wal_cols, wal_oids, start_lsn, end_lsn }` (drain.rs:14-43). Each of the five `dest_*.rs` files then rebuilds a table's window and works out its contracts for itself. Nothing ties a table's body to its column layout. So a MySQL-source TRUNCATE-only window is `Collapser::new(Vec::new())` (mysource.rs:760-764) with no `wal_cols`, and every apply fails at `.ok_or_else("missing WAL column list")` (dest_ch.rs:1006-1009, dest_pg.rs:408-411, dest_my.rs:437-440, dest_bq.rs:1056-1059, dest_ice.rs:111-114; the changelog twin is dest_ch.rs:851-854) (§3.4).

The replay identity has no single rule:
- ClickHouse records only an attempt's START (dest_ch.rs:339-349). It skips `n.min(len)` and writes the replay's own end (dest_ch.rs:906, :923-926) (§3.14).
- ClickHouse restarts `seq` at 0 on a stamp an older version already used (dest_ch.rs:898, :938) (§3.6).
- BigQuery records nothing and posts scripts with no job id (sink/bigquery.rs:718-724) (§3.12).

The other contracts are also left to each consumer:
- `Collapsed::deletes` says "(dedup'd)" (collapse.rs:32), but it is a `Vec` that the collapser pushes to again on Delete→Insert→Delete (collapse.rs:158-163, :269-274, :277-280). Each consumer deduplicates on its own terms, or does not: dest_bq.rs:1102-1106 leaves `Gone` keys out of `landed`, dest_ice.rs:144-148 keeps a `seen` set (§3.11).
- A changelog `Change` is `{op, row}` (changelog.rs:48-51). The U half of a moved key has no record of where the row was (§3.13).
- `_apitap_state` is read by eight SELECTs with eight different predicates. dest_my.rs:283-284 adds `AND mode = 'log_based'`. The MySQL, ClickHouse and BigQuery bulk lanes read `watermark` alone (sink/mysql.rs:1125, sink/clickhouse.rs:1640, sink/bigquery.rs:2147) (§3.7).

Every finding here is one destination answering on its own a question that has exactly one right answer.

### 2. The decision

Six contracts become types or pure functions in apitap-core, and destinations can only spell SQL against them:
- `TableWindow<B>` cannot exist without a `Layout`, and a window never spans a layout change.
- `DeleteSet` is a set by construction, and `Resolved::rows()` yields each key once.
- `Change.moved_from` gives the one "where did this row live" rule, shared with the BigQuery replica MERGE.
- `replay::replay_plan` is the only source of a changelog stamp, seq, skip or trim. A client job id makes a BigQuery script run at most once.
- `naming::state_verdict` is the only way to turn a state row into a watermark, in both lanes.

Cost:
- **Replica windows:** zero extra round trips.
- **Changelog, per run:** one probe query per group per run (ClickHouse: per table).
- **Changelog, on replay/upgrade only:** one facts query and one trim DELETE, only on a replayed or upgrade-collided window.
- **Per BigQuery changelog transaction:** one marker INSERT.
- **Moved-key TOAST windows on BigQuery:** a LEFT JOIN in the MERGE.
- **Bulk incremental runs:** two more columns in the state read, moved ahead of the empty-table early return.

### 3. What changes

All paths are under `crates/apitap-core/src/` unless noted.

#### 3.0 Boundaries with other clusters, and deviations from the winning text

These rules bind the other clusters:
- **R-B1 (watermark and marker):** this cluster computes, cluster A writes.
  - Every apply ends by handing cluster A `Watermark::Set { lsn }`. For replica windows `lsn = id.end()`; for changelog windows `lsn = plan.watermark()`.
  - The changelog **pending marker and trim are body operations of the unit, never in `close_unit`.**
    - ClickHouse order: TRIM → MARK → APPEND, each through A's owned-statement primitive so it carries `OWNER_PRED`; the watermark follows in `close_unit`.
    - BigQuery: the table's group Vec `[trim?, marker, INSERT…SELECT]` is given to A's `BqUnit::push_group(table, stmts)`, and **A's `close_unit` appends the state INSERT into that same Vec** so `pack_whole_groups` (dest_bq.rs:53-69) can never separate them.
  - `Watermark` needs no marker variant.
  - A's `OWNER_PRED` must be monotone (once false for a token, false for ever — A's sticky claim, with "no row ⇒ not owned"). The argument in §4 D relies on this.
- **R-B2 (state clear):** whoever deletes a table's `_apitap_state` row, and the changelog bootstrap rebuild (dest_ch.rs:487-602, dest_bq.rs changelog_bootstrap_finish ~500-597), also deletes that table's `_apitap_cdc_pending` rows in the same unit:
  - ClickHouse: `ALTER TABLE _apitap_cdc_pending DELETE WHERE dest_table='{t}' AND source_id='{s}' SETTINGS mutations_sync = 1`.
  - BigQuery: `DELETE FROM {P} WHERE dest_table='{t}' AND source_id='{s}'`.
  - A deletes the `Dest::clear_state` / `bootstrap_finish` wrappers (run.rs:311-368), so this goes into A's unit bodies. Without it, an old marker whose start is past a new stream's start trips R0 (§3.D) on every run.
- **R-B3:** `_apitap_cdc_pending` is INSERT-only inside every BigQuery script. The only non-INSERT DML on it is the best-effort compaction in §3.D, which runs outside scripts.
- **R-B4:** the `Dest::read_state → Option<CdcWatermark>` signature (§3.E) lands after A's signature moves.
- **Struck from the winning design:** §F in full.
  - F2 (`PARENT_RUN` task-local, `RunId::child/is_mine`) is replaced by cluster C's lineage (`mint_within` / `owns` + `transfer_within`, with `is_self` for every delete). *[§0 R2 overrides: there is no `owns()`; the parent's artifacts classify `Found::Parent`, and every delete uses `is_self`.]*
  - F1 (a `lease_close` after each collector DROP) is replaced by cluster E's `guard.rs` collect step, which closes a lease only with a `Released` proof covering every marker of that token.
  - This cluster ships no code for the handover, the double bootstrap or lease GC.
- **Other deviations, with reasons:**
  - `WindowId` has no `events` field. `replay_plan` takes the table's own event count; a window total panics or clamps on groups (operations attack).
  - `replay_plan` runs for every changelog member on every window, including absent members with 0 events.
  - An unmatched marker/stamp combination is an `Err`.
  - The BigQuery marker lives in `_apitap_cdc_pending` (already in `OWN_TABLES`, naming.rs:947-948), not in a reserved `source_id`. It is read by one probe per run instead of one query job per window (graft).
  - `DeleteSet` is derived from the map in `finish()` (graft), not kept with an `in_deletes` flag at six push sites.
  - The apply dispatches on the body kind; the `changelog: bool` parameters go (graft).
  - Moved-key TOAST resolution is extended to the BigQuery replica MERGE (races attack).
  - The MySQL layout cache is invalidated on DDL, and a window is cut at DDL (races attack).
  - Job ids are 128-bit random, and a 409 is adopted only after the query text matches (races attack).

#### 3.A `logbased/window.rs` (new): a table's window carries its layout (closes §3.4)

In `logbased/mod.rs`, add `pub(crate) mod window; pub(crate) mod replay;`.

```rust
#[derive(Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub(crate) struct Layout { cols: Vec<String>, oids: Vec<u32>, key_cols: Vec<String>, key_idx: Vec<usize> }
impl Layout {
    /// pgoutput Relation. Ok(None) = untracked relation. Absorbs rel_state's body
    /// (drain.rs:449-491): REPLICA IDENTITY NOTHING refusal, key-by-NAME lookup,
    /// key-must-be-flagged check under DEFAULT/INDEX.
    pub(crate) fn from_relation(r: &Relation, key_cols: &HashMap<String, Vec<String>>) -> Result<Option<Layout>>;
    /// MySQL: information_schema (mybinlog.rs:160-170). Keys by NAME from the run's
    /// key list (MySession.tracked, myrun.rs:257-260) — the same names every apply
    /// already uses via pk_indices; the PRI flags (mybinlog.rs:993) stop deciding keys.
    /// oids all 0. Err naming the column if a key column is absent.
    pub(crate) fn from_mysql(table: &str, sc: &TableSchema, key_cols: &[String]) -> Result<Layout>;
    pub(crate) fn cols(&self) -> &[String]; pub(crate) fn oids(&self) -> &[u32];
    pub(crate) fn key_cols(&self) -> &[String]; pub(crate) fn key_idx(&self) -> &[usize];
    pub(crate) fn key_oids(&self) -> Vec<u32>;   // key_idx.map(|i| oids[i])
}
pub(crate) trait WindowBody { fn rows_fit(&self, ncols: usize) -> std::result::Result<(), usize>; fn event_count(&self) -> u64; }
pub(crate) struct TableWindow<B> { layout: Arc<Layout>, body: B }          // fields private
impl<B: WindowBody> TableWindow<B> {
    pub(crate) fn seal(table: &str, layout: Arc<Layout>, body: B) -> Result<Self>;  // only constructor
    pub(crate) fn layout(&self) -> &Layout; pub(crate) fn body(&self) -> &B;
}
pub(crate) enum Bodies { Replica(HashMap<String, TableWindow<Collapsed>>), Changelog(HashMap<String, TableWindow<Changes>>) }
pub(crate) enum Slice<'a> { Replica(Option<&'a TableWindow<Collapsed>>), Changelog(Option<&'a TableWindow<Changes>>) }
pub(crate) struct DrainOutcome { pub bodies: Bodies, pub id: crate::logbased::replay::WindowId, pub hit_budget: bool }
impl DrainOutcome {
    pub(crate) fn slice(&self, qualified: &str) -> Slice<'_>;
    pub(crate) fn events(&self) -> u64;      // Σ event_count, for the debug lines
    pub(crate) fn is_empty(&self) -> bool;   // no bodies
}
```

`rows_fit` checks:
- **`Collapsed`:** every upsert `Tuple::len()`, and every residue row (`MaskedUpdate`/`Upsert`/`Rekey`), equals `ncols`.
- **`Changes`:** I/U rows equal `ncols`; a D row may be shorter (a short old image is a real wire shape, changelog.rs:219-222).

`seal` fails with `Error::Transfer("log_based: {table}: a row in this window has {got} columns but the window's layout has {n} — the table's definition changed inside one source transaction. Clear this table's apitap state on the destination and re-run to bootstrap it from its current schema.")`.

`hit_budget` keeps its name and now means "apply this window and drain again at once". The DDL/layout cut below sets it too.

**Collapser and Changes.**
- `Collapser::new(layout: Arc<Layout>)` replaces `new(key_idx)` (collapse.rs:100-110). The key comes from `layout.key_idx()`. `Collapser::seal(self, table) -> Result<TableWindow<Collapsed>>` replaces `finish` for callers; `finish` stays private.
- `Changes` gains a private `layout: Arc<Layout>`, set by `Changes::new(layout)`; `#[derive(Default)]` at changelog.rs:54 goes.
- `update(old, row)`, `mask_plan()` and `resolve_masked(cols, base)` drop their `pk_idx`/`names` parameters (changelog.rs:83, :101, :139-145) and read `self.layout`.
- `Changes::seal(self, table) -> Result<TableWindow<Changes>>`.

**drain.rs (Postgres).**
- `DrainSession.key_idx/wal_cols/wal_oids` (63-66) become `layouts: HashMap<String, Arc<Layout>>` plus `carry: Option<(Vec<(Arc<str>, StreamOp)>, u64)>`.
- `RelState` (45-49) keeps `table`, `tracked` and drops `key_idx`.
- In the Relation arm (293-311), for a tracked table: `let new = Layout::from_relation(&r, key_cols)?.expect("tracked")`. If `sess.layouts.get(t).map_or(true, |o| **o != new)`, insert `Arc::new(new)`, and if `collapsers`/`changelogs` already hold a body for `t`, set the local `relayout = true`. A value-equal Relation keeps the old `Arc`; pgoutput re-sends Relation on relcache invalidation, and that must not cut a window.
- At `Commit` (219-229) and `StreamCommit` (235-252), before `flush_ops`: if `relayout`, then `sess.carry = Some((ops, e)); hit_budget = true; break;`. `end_lsn` stays at the previous commit.
- At the top of `drain()`: `if let Some((ops, e)) = sess.carry.take() { buf_bytes += Σop_bytes; flush_ops(ops, …)?; end_lsn = e; if e >= stop_line { return … } }`.
- `flush_ops` (408-447) takes `&HashMap<String, Arc<Layout>>` and builds `Collapser::new(l.clone())` / `Changes::new(l.clone())` with `entry().or_insert_with`.
- Output (391-402) becomes `Bodies::Replica(collapsers.into_iter().map(|(t,c)| Ok((t.clone(), c.seal(&t)?))).collect::<Result<_>>()?)`, or `Changelog` when `changelog`.
- `id: WindowId::new(start_lsn, end_lsn)`.
- **Deleted:** `DrainOutcome` here (14-43), `rel_state` (449-491; its body moves to `Layout::from_relation`), the per-window `wal_cols.clone()`/`wal_oids.clone()` (399-400).

**mysource.rs (MySQL/MariaDB).**
- `MySession` (419-427) gains `layouts: HashMap<String, Arc<Layout>>`, which persists across windows like `DrainSession`.
- One resolver, used by the TABLE_MAP arm and the TRUNCATE arm:

```rust
async fn layout_for(sess: &mut MySession, pool: &MySqlPool, q: &str) -> Result<Arc<Layout>> {
    if let Some(l) = sess.layouts.get(q) { return Ok(l.clone()); }
    let (db, tb) = q.split_once('.').expect("db.table");
    if !sess.st.schemas.contains_key(q) { sess.st.schemas.insert(q.to_string(), fetch_schema(pool, db, tb).await?); }
    let keys = sess.tracked.get(q).ok_or_else(|| Error::Transfer(format!("log_based: {q} is not tracked")))?;
    let l = Arc::new(Layout::from_mysql(q, &sess.st.schemas[q], keys)?);
    sess.layouts.insert(q.to_string(), l.clone()); Ok(l)
}
```

- **TABLE_MAP arm (532-583):** after the length check at 560-575, call `layout_for(sess, pool, &q).await?`.
- **Rows-path Relation arm (599-622):** becomes `PgoMessage::Relation(_) => {}`. Bodies are created in `drain_tx`.
- **DDL arm (654-716):**
  - `sess.st.schemas.retain(...)` (656) becomes `sess.st.schemas.clear(); sess.layouts.clear();`. `maps.clear()` (657) stays. The QUERY event's `db` is the session default database, not the altered table's, so invalidating by `db` was already wrong for `ALTER TABLE other.t`.
  - TRUNCATE branch: `drain_tx(...)` first (as today), then `layout_for(sess, pool, &t).await?`, then `tx_buf.push((t, TxOp::Truncate)); drain_tx(...)`, **unconditionally**. The `changelog || collapsers.contains_key` fork and `pending_truncate` (459, 690-696) are deleted.
  - After any DDL (TRUNCATE included): `if !collapsers.is_empty() || !changelogs.is_empty() { end_mark = pack_pos(&sess.file, h.log_pos); hit_budget = true; break; }`. A DDL QUERY event is its own commit boundary (the comment at 658-663), so a window ends there and never spans a layout change.
- **`drain_tx` (807-844)** takes `layouts: &HashMap<String, Arc<Layout>>` instead of `key_idx`. For each op: `let l = layouts.get(t).ok_or_else(|| Error::Transfer(format!("log_based: no column layout for {t} — decoder invariant broken")))?`, then `changelogs.entry(t).or_insert_with(|| Changes::new(l.clone()))` or `collapsers.entry(t).or_insert_with(|| Collapser::new(l.clone()))`.
  - This deletes both silent `continue`s (823, 833-835), which dropped row events, and `.or_default()` (820, 824).
- **Deleted:** the locals `key_idx`, `wal_cols`, `wal_oids` (464-466), the `pending_truncate` window-end loop and `Collapser::new(Vec::new())` (755-765).
- **Output (766-778):** `bodies` via `seal`, and `id: WindowId::new(start, end_mark)`.

**myrun.rs:279** becomes `if o.id.end() == watermark && o.is_empty() { break; }`.

**run.rs.**
- `Dest::apply_no_src` (373-404) is deleted. `Dest::apply` (406-438) becomes `apply(&self, dest_table, qualified, source_id, o: &DrainOutcome, src: Option<&PgPool>)`, dispatching on `(self, o.slice(qualified))`:
  - `(Ch, Changelog(w))` → `d.apply_changelog(dest_table, w, &o.id, source_id)`.
  - `(Bq, Changelog(_))` → the single-member group.
  - `(_, Changelog(_))` → `CHANGELOG_DEST_MSG`.
  - `(Pg|My|Ch|Bq, Replica(w))` → `d.apply(dest_table, w, &o.id, source_id)`.
  - `(Ice, Replica(w))` → `d.apply(dest_table, w, &o.id, source_id, src.ok_or_else(|| InvalidInput("log_based: iceberg needs a Postgres source in this release"))?)`.
- The `changelog: bool` parameters, the call at 1298 and the `clog` plumbing at 1595/1633/1644 go. The BigQuery group tuple `(dest_table, qualified, pk_cols, source_id)` (1588-1593) loses `pk_cols`.
- `outcome.end_lsn` becomes `outcome.id.end()` at 1293, 1656 and 1691.
- The debug `o.tables.values().map(|c| c.events)` at 1648 and 1682 becomes `o.events()`.

**Destination applies.**
- **Signatures:** `apply(dest_table, w: Option<&TableWindow<Collapsed>>, id: &WindowId, source_id)`; changelog: `apply_changelog(dest_table, w: Option<&TableWindow<Changes>>, id, source_id)`.
- **`pk_cols` removed:** every engine apply drops its `pk_cols` parameter and reads `w.layout().key_cols()`, because the key names the collapser hashed are the ones the SQL uses.
- **Read-site replacements:**
  - `wal_cols` → `layout.cols()`
  - `oids` → `layout.oids()`
  - `pk_indices(...)` → `layout.key_idx()`
  - `pk_oids` → `layout.key_oids()`
- **Deleted "missing WAL column/type list" sites (all ten):** dest_ch.rs:851-858, :1006-1013; dest_pg.rs:408-411; dest_my.rs:437-444; dest_bq.rs:797-800, :809-812, :1056-1059, :1068-1071; dest_ice.rs:111-118.
- **`pk_indices` call sites (rowtext.rs:108-117):** dest_ch.rs:877 and :1015, dest_pg.rs:429, dest_my.rs:446, dest_bq.rs:826 and :1072, dest_ice.rs:120 now read `layout.key_idx()`. `pk_indices` survives only inside `Layout::from_*`.
- **`w == None`** does nothing but hand A `Watermark::Set { lsn: id.end() }`. The changelog path runs §3.D even when `w == None`.

#### 3.B `DeleteSet` and `Resolved` (closes §3.11)

**collapse.rs.**
- `Slot` (72-80) becomes `enum Slot { Upsert { seq: usize, del: bool }, Delete, Residue { del: bool } }`, with `fn del(&self) -> bool` (Delete ⇒ true). `del` means "this key's pre-window row is removed in the set phase".
- The private `deletes: Vec<Key>` field (93) and every push (272, 278, 307, 313) are deleted.
- Transitions:
  - `insert`: Vacant → `Upsert{del:false}`; `Delete` → `Upsert{del:true}` (158-163); `Upsert` keeps `del` (154-157).
  - `update` non-rekey: Vacant as 245-254 with `del:false` / `Residue{del:false}`; `Delete if !toast` → `Upsert{del:true}` (230-234); `_` arm (235-243) → `Residue{del: e.get().del()}`.
  - Rekey (206-207): `let d_old = self.map.get(&old_key).map_or(false, Slot::del)`, same for `new_key`, then insert `Residue{del: d_old}` and `Residue{del: d_new}`.
  - `delete` / `put_delete`: `Upsert{seq,..}` → `upserts[seq] = None; e.insert(Slot::Delete)`; Vacant → `Delete`.
  - `truncate` clears the map (289).
- `finish` (319-327): `deletes: DeleteSet(self.map.into_iter().filter(|(_, s)| s.del()).map(|(k, _)| k).collect())`. It runs one pass over the key map at window end, has no per-event cost, and saves one key clone per deleted key.

```rust
#[derive(Debug, Default)] pub(crate) struct DeleteSet(Vec<Key>);          // field private
impl DeleteSet { pub(crate) fn iter(&self) -> std::slice::Iter<'_, Key>; pub(crate) fn len(&self) -> usize; pub(crate) fn is_empty(&self) -> bool;
                 #[cfg(test)] pub(crate) fn from_keys(v: Vec<Key>) -> Self /* sort+dedup */; #[cfg(test)] pub(crate) fn sorted(&self) -> Vec<Key>; }
```

- `Collapsed.deletes: DeleteSet`. The doc at 32 becomes "Keys whose destination rows must go, each once. Order unspecified." The pg/my/ch loops (dest_pg.rs:446, dest_my.rs:539, dest_ch.rs:1069) change `&c.deletes` to `c.deletes.iter()`.

**resolve.rs.**

```rust
pub(crate) enum Fin<'a> { Row(&'a [Cell]), Owned(Vec<Cell>), Masked { row: Vec<Cell>, moved_from: Option<Key> }, Gone }
pub(crate) enum Image<'a> { Row(&'a [Cell]), Masked { row: &'a [Cell], moved_from: Option<&'a Key> }, Delete }
pub(crate) struct Resolved<'a> { finals: Vec<(Key, Fin<'a>)>, delete_only: Vec<&'a Key> }   // private
impl<'a> Resolved<'a> {
    /// finals in order, then delete_only. Each key EXACTLY once.
    pub(crate) fn rows(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = (&Key, Image<'_>)>;
    pub(crate) fn any_moved_mask(&self) -> bool;
    /// Iceberg's source refetch: every Masked entry; the caller overwrites it with Fin::Owned.
    pub(crate) fn masked_mut(&mut self) -> impl Iterator<Item = (&Key, &mut Fin<'a>)>;
}
pub(crate) fn resolve_window<'a>(w: &'a TableWindow<Collapsed>) -> Resolved<'a>;   // key_idx from layout
```

- `delete_only = if c.truncate { vec![] } else { c.deletes.iter().filter(|k| !index.contains_key(*k)).collect() }`.
- The fold (34-117) is unchanged except that `Fin::Refetch(x)` becomes `Fin::Masked{row: x, moved_from}` (see §3.C).
- This is now the only place that decides which keys get a delete row and how many. "A TRUNCATE window has nothing old to delete" is decided here for both set-image consumers.

**dest_bq.rs `stage` (1095-1142)** is one loop over `r.rows()`:
- `Row(c)` → `push_upsert(c, None, None)`
- `Masked{row, moved_from}` → `push_upsert(row, Some(mask), moved_from)`
- `Delete` → `push_delete`

`staged` is the count. Deleted: `landed` (1102-1106), the `c.deletes` loop (1133-1142), `use std::collections::HashSet` (28).

**dest_ice.rs (135-160):**
- `let mut r = resolve_window(w); refetch_masked(&mut r, …)` (248; it iterates `r.masked_mut()`).
- If `!w.body().truncate`, the equality-delete keys are `r.rows().map(|(k, _)| k)`, every key exactly once.
- Data rows are `r.rows()` filtered to `Row`; an `Image::Masked` there stays the existing "survived the refetch — bug" error.
- `n_rows` counts non-`Delete`.
- Deleted: `seen` (144-148).

#### 3.C Where a moved row's untouched cells live (closes §3.13, plus the BigQuery replica twin)

**changelog.rs.**
- `Change { op, row, moved_from: Option<CKey> }` (47-51).
- `update` (83-92): when `key_of(old) != key_of(row)`, push `D{row: old, moved_from: None}`, then `U{row, moved_from: Some(key_of(old))}`. Every other constructor sets `None`.
- `mask_plan` (101-122): the readback key of a masked row is `ev.moved_from.clone().unwrap_or(key_of(row))`.
- `resolve_masked` (139-206) keeps `carry: HashMap<CKey, HashMap<usize, Option<Bytes>>>` and a local `truncated = false`. Per event:
  - **T** → `carry.clear(); truncated = true; continue`.
  - **D** → `continue`. D images do not write `carry`: under REPLICA IDENTITY DEFAULT a D image is key + NULLs, and writing it would overwrite the old key's values before the moved U reads them.
  - **I/U** → render cells. If masked, for each masked `i`: `let src = ev.moved_from.as_ref().unwrap_or(&key)`, `from_window = carry.get(src).and_then(|s| s.get(&i)).cloned()`, `from_dest = if truncated { None } else { base[src][pos(i)] }`. If both are missing, raise the existing torn error (190-196). After resolution, write **every** cell (resolved values included) into `carry[key]`. Today only unmasked cells reach the slot (165-176), so a second masked update in the same window fell through to the destination's pre-window value.
- The D half still carries the old image, and `__current` still ranks by `(lsn, seq, op='B')`.

**resolve.rs (Rekey, 84-114; MaskedUpdate, 63-83).** The masked result carries its origin:
- If the base from `index` is `Fin::Masked{moved_from: Some(m)}` → `moved_from = Some(m.clone())` (a chain 1→9→5 still points at 1).
- Else, for a Rekey → `Some(old_key.clone())`.
- For a MaskedUpdate → `None` (the row is at its own key).

The comment at 88-93 becomes "…BigQuery reads the hole from the target's row at `moved_from` inside the MERGE; Iceberg refetches from the source."

**dest_bq.rs (replica MERGE)** fixes the permanent `ERROR(...)` at 1316-1320 for `UPDATE SET id=9 WHERE id=1` with an untouched TOASTed column:
- **Staging and NDJSON:**
  - `ApplyPlan::staging_fields` (1249-1258) always adds nullable STRING `_apitap_from_{j}` for each `j in 0..pk.len()`.
  - `push_upsert(out, cols, cells, mask, from: Option<&Key>)` writes `_apitap_from_{j}` = key part `j` when `from` is `Some`.
  - The reserved-name check (1060-1067) also refuses any source column starting with `_apitap_from_`.
- **`cast_expr` split:** `cast_expr(name, oid, ty)` (1338) becomes `cast_expr_of(src_ref: &str, oid, ty)` plus the wrapper `cast_expr(name,..) = cast_expr_of(&format!("`{name}`"),..)`. `ApplyPlan` keeps `oids` and target `types` for the pk columns.
- **Two MERGE forms:** `merge_sql(conn, table, truncate, moved: bool)`.
  - With `moved == false`, it is byte-identical to today's text (pinned by a golden test).
  - With `moved == true` (`r.any_moved_mask()`), the USING body becomes:

```sql
SELECT S._apitap_op,
       IF(S.`_apitap_from_0` IS NOT NULL, NULL, S._apitap_mask) AS _apitap_mask,
       -- per data column c at 1-based position p:
       IF(S.`_apitap_from_0` IS NOT NULL AND SUBSTR(S._apitap_mask, p, 1) = '1',
          IF(F.`<pk0>` IS NULL,
             ERROR('log_based: <table>: a moved row''s untouched column has nowhere to come from — no row at its old key'),
             F.`c`),
          <cast_expr_of("S.`c`", oid_c, ty_c)>) AS `c`
FROM <staging> S
LEFT JOIN <target> F
  ON F.`<pk0>` = <cast_expr_of("S.`_apitap_from_0`", oid_pk0, ty_pk0)> [AND … for every pk column]
```

  - The rest of the MERGE (ON, WHEN arms) is unchanged. The USING subquery reads the target's pre-MERGE snapshot, so key 1's row is visible to key 9's patch while key 1's own `D` row deletes it.
  - It costs one extra target read, only on windows that hold a moved masked key.

#### 3.D `logbased/replay.rs` (new): window identity and the one replay rule (closes §3.14, §3.6, §3.12's duplicate)

```rust
pub(crate) struct WindowId { start: u64, end: u64 }        // start is private to this module
impl WindowId { pub(crate) fn new(start: u64, end: u64) -> Self; pub(crate) fn end(&self) -> u64; }

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub(crate) struct Pending { start: u64, seq_base: Option<u32> }   // None = written by 0.56.0 (events column = 0)
impl Pending { pub(crate) fn recorded(start: u64, seq_base: u32) -> Self; pub(crate) fn legacy(start: u64) -> Self;
               pub(crate) fn start(&self) -> u64; fn query_base(&self) -> u32 { self.seq_base.unwrap_or(0) } }
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default)] pub(crate) struct Landed { pub count: u64, pub max_seq: Option<u32> }
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default)] pub(crate) struct StampFacts { pub all: Landed, pub from_base: Landed, pub distinct_seq: u64 }
pub(crate) struct MarkerRow { pub start: u64, pub seq_base: u32, pub end: u64, pub events: u64 }
pub(crate) struct ReplayPlan { stamp: u64, seq_base: u32, skip: usize, events: usize, trim_from: Option<u32>, mark: bool, watermark: u64 }
impl ReplayPlan {
    pub(crate) fn stamp(&self) -> u64; pub(crate) fn watermark(&self) -> u64; pub(crate) fn trim_from(&self) -> Option<u32>;
    pub(crate) fn to_append(&self) -> std::ops::Range<usize>;          // skip..events
    pub(crate) fn seq_of(&self, ordinal: usize) -> u32;                // seq_base + ordinal; debug_assert ordinal in to_append()
    pub(crate) fn marker(&self) -> Option<MarkerRow>;                  // Some iff mark
}
pub(crate) fn replay_plan(p: Option<Pending>, f: Option<&StampFacts>, id: &WindowId, table_events: usize) -> Result<ReplayPlan>;
/// Shared parses of the engines' result rows ("landed_from_row"): -1 means none.
pub(crate) fn parse_pending(n: &str, lsn: &str, seq_base: &str, events: &str) -> Result<Option<Pending>>;  // n=="0" → None; events=="0" → legacy
pub(crate) fn parse_facts(cells: &[&str]) -> Result<StampFacts>;   // [n_all, mx_all, n_b, mx_b, distinct]
```

**`replay_plan` rules**, with `n_ev = table_events` = `w.body().events.len()` (rows to append; D+U of a moved key are 2), or 0 when `w` is `None`:
- **R0.** `p.start > id.start` → `Err(Transfer("log_based: {table}: the destination records an append attempt at position {p.start}, past this window's start {id.start} — the watermark was moved backwards. Either put it back, or delete this table's rows with _apitap_lsn >= {id.start} AND _apitap_op != 'B' together with its _apitap_cdc_pending rows, then re-run."))`.
- **R1.** `p` is `None`, or `p.start < id.start`: rows at this stamp belong to another writer (a 0.55.x window stamped with its END, which equals this start, or a pre-marker attempt). Set `seq_base = f.all.max_seq.map_or(0, |m| m + 1)` (`f` is `None` ⇒ 0), `skip 0`, `trim None`, `mark = n_ev > 0`.
- **R2.** `p.start == id.start` (needs `f`; if `f` is missing, `Err` "internal: facts required"):
  - `base = match p.seq_base { Some(b) => b, None => if f.all.count != f.distinct_seq { Err(legacy-collision) } else { 0 } }`.
    - legacy-collision text: "log_based: {table}: a 0.56.0 run was interrupted while appending the window at {start}, and rows of an older version share that stamp; apitap cannot tell them apart. Inspect `SELECT _apitap_at, count(), min(_apitap_seq), max(_apitap_seq) FROM t WHERE _apitap_lsn = {start} AND _apitap_op != 'B' GROUP BY 1`, delete the newest group, clear this table's _apitap_cdc_pending rows, and re-run."
  - `n = f.from_base.count`. `prefix = n == 0 || f.from_base.max_seq == base.checked_add(n as u32 - 1)`.
  - `!prefix` (torn) → `trim_from Some(base)`, `skip 0`, `mark = n_ev > 0`. The 0.56.0 re-append-with-duplicate-pairs branch (dest_ch.rs:907-921) is deleted.
  - `prefix && n <= n_ev` → `skip n`, `trim None`, `mark = n < n_ev`.
  - `prefix && n > n_ev` (the shorter replay, including an absent member with `n_ev = 0`) → `skip n_ev`, `trim_from Some(base + n_ev)`, `mark false`.
- **Always.** `base.checked_add(n_ev as u32)` → on overflow, `Err("seq overflow")`. `watermark = id.end`.
- **Why R2's trim rule.** It makes the log at a stamp exactly `[start, id.end)`. The stream cannot seek (run.rs:1662-1714, myrun.rs:264-291 confirm `id.end`). The trimmed events come back in the next window under that window's stamp.

**`Memo`** (in replay.rs, one per `ChDest`/`BqDest`, i.e. per run):

```rust
#[derive(Default)] pub(crate) struct Memo(std::sync::Mutex<HashMap<(String, String), Seen>>);
#[derive(Clone, Copy)] struct Seen { pending: Option<Pending>, ceiling: Option<u64> }
pub(crate) enum Ask { Probe, Facts { base: u32 }, Nothing }
impl Memo {
    pub(crate) fn ask(&self, t: &str, sid: &str, id: &WindowId) -> Ask;
    //   unseen → Probe; pending.start == id.start → Facts{base: p.query_base()};
    //   ceiling >= id.start → Facts{base: 0}; else Nothing.
    pub(crate) fn probed(&self, t: &str, sid: &str, pending: Option<Pending>, ceiling: Option<u64>);
    pub(crate) fn plan(&self, t: &str, sid: &str, f: Option<&StampFacts>, id: &WindowId, events: usize) -> Result<ReplayPlan>;
    pub(crate) fn committed(&self, t: &str, sid: &str, plan: &ReplayPlan); // mark ⇒ pending = recorded(stamp, seq_base)
    pub(crate) fn forget(&self, t: &str, sid: &str);                       // on any apply error
}
```

- After `Probe`, the engine calls `probed`, then `ask` again. `ceiling` is the maximum non-`B` stamp present when the run started.
- Rows at a stamp exist only if something wrote there. Earlier runs are bounded by `ceiling`. This run writes only at its own window starts, which strictly increase. Concurrent writers are excluded by cluster A.
- So after the first window of a run (steady state), `ask` returns `Nothing` and no query runs.
- `committed` is called only after cluster A reports the table's unit committed: on ClickHouse, the confirmed watermark; on BigQuery, the commit of the transaction holding the table's group.

**ClickHouse (dest_ch.rs).**
- **`ensure_pending_table` (309-322):** CREATE gains `seq_base UInt32 DEFAULT 0, end_lsn UInt64 DEFAULT 0, events UInt64 DEFAULT 0`. It is followed by `ALTER TABLE _apitap_cdc_pending ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS seq_base UInt32 DEFAULT 0, ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS end_lsn UInt64 DEFAULT 0, ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS events UInt64 DEFAULT 0`, memoized once per run. On a 0.56.0 table, existing rows read `events = 0`, which marks them legacy.
- **`probe(t, s)`** replaces `pending_window` (324-337):

```sql
SELECT count(), toString(argMax(lsn,(lsn,at))), toString(argMax(seq_base,(lsn,at))), toString(argMax(events,(lsn,at))),
       (SELECT toString(count()) FROM {t} WHERE _apitap_op != 'B'),
       (SELECT toString(max(_apitap_lsn)) FROM {t} WHERE _apitap_op != 'B')
FROM _apitap_cdc_pending WHERE dest_table = '{t}' AND source_id = '{s}' FORMAT TabSeparatedRaw
```

  - Versions are ranked by `(lsn, at)`, not by `at` alone. Marker starts only rise for a table, so a backward clock step cannot pick an older marker at read time (merge-time residual in §8).
  - Ceiling is `None` when the count is 0.
- **`facts(t, stamp, base)`** replaces `appended_prefix` (351-382):

```sql
SELECT count(), if(count() = 0, -1, toInt64(max(_apitap_seq))),
       countIf(_apitap_seq >= {base}),
       if(countIf(_apitap_seq >= {base}) = 0, -1, toInt64(maxIf(_apitap_seq, _apitap_seq >= {base}))),
       uniqExact(_apitap_seq)
FROM {t} WHERE _apitap_lsn = {stamp} AND _apitap_op != 'B' FORMAT TabSeparatedRaw
```

- **`fn changelog_steps(plan: &ReplayPlan) -> Vec<ChStep>`** (pure), with `enum ChStep { Trim(u32), Mark(MarkerRow), Append }`, returns `[Trim?] ++ [Mark?] ++ [Append if !to_append().is_empty()]`. It is the only order.
- **`apply_changelog` (836-986) becomes:**
  1. `refuse_clustered` (846).
  2. `memo.ask` → `probe` / `facts` → `memo.plan`.
  3. Reserved-name check (859-866) on `layout.cols()`.
  4. Masked readback (876-888) through `resolve_masked`.
  5. Build the TSV for `plan.to_append()` with `plan.stamp()` and `plan.seq_of(i)` (replacing 898, 938, 958-960).
  6. Execute `changelog_steps` through A's unit:
     - `Trim(x)` → A's owned DELETE: `DELETE FROM {t} WHERE _apitap_lsn = {stamp} AND _apitap_op != 'B' AND _apitap_seq >= {x}`.
     - `Mark(m)` → A's owned literal INSERT: `INSERT INTO _apitap_cdc_pending (dest_table, source_id, lsn, seq_base, end_lsn, events) SELECT '{t}','{s}',{m.start},{m.seq_base},{m.end},{m.events} WHERE {OWNER_PRED}`.
     - `Append` → A's `insert_owned`.
  7. Return `Watermark::Set { lsn: plan.watermark() }` to A's `close_unit`.
  8. After A's confirmation, `memo.committed`; on any `Err`, `memo.forget`.
- **Deleted:** `STATE_CURSOR` (21), `pending_window`/`mark_pending`/`appended_prefix` in their 0.56.0 form, the eprintln branch (912-920), the `skip >= len` early return (923-927), and the no-event branches (847-850, 867-870; an absent member goes through the plan).
- **ChangelogTmp tokenization and EXCHANGE in the rebuild:** owned by cluster A (operations attack). R-B2's marker clear rides that same unit.

**BigQuery (dest_bq.rs).** `P = conn.fq(naming::CDC_PENDING_TABLE)`.
- **`ensure_pending_table()`**, once per run via `cdc_script`, outside any transaction: `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS {P} (dest_table STRING NOT NULL, source_id STRING NOT NULL, lsn INT64 NOT NULL, seq_base INT64 NOT NULL, end_lsn INT64 NOT NULL, events INT64 NOT NULL, at TIMESTAMP NOT NULL) CLUSTER BY dest_table, source_id`.
- **`read_state` (330-374)** also selects `FORMAT_TIMESTAMP('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%E6SZ', synced_at)` and stores it in `self.hint: Mutex<HashMap<(t,s), String>>`.
  - `PRUNE(t) = AND _apitap_at >= TIMESTAMP_SUB(TIMESTAMP '{hint}', INTERVAL 31 DAY)`, or empty when there is no hint.
  - A constant, so the default `TIMESTAMP_TRUNC(_apitap_at, MONTH)` partitions prune.
  - Every row at a stamp ≥ this run's first window start was committed in or after the transaction that wrote the table's current watermark row (one transaction holds rows + marker + state), so the bound excludes none of them (the one exception is in §8).
- **`probe_group(members, now)`**, one query job per group per run, run after `ensure_pending_table`:

```sql
WITH k AS (SELECT * FROM UNNEST([STRUCT('{t1}' AS t, '{s1}' AS s), …])),
m AS (SELECT p.dest_table, p.lsn, p.seq_base, p.events,
             ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY p.dest_table, p.source_id ORDER BY p.lsn DESC, p.at DESC) AS rn
      FROM {P} p JOIN k ON p.dest_table = k.t AND p.source_id = k.s)
SELECT 'm', dest_table, CAST(lsn AS STRING), CAST(seq_base AS STRING), CAST(events AS STRING) FROM m WHERE rn = 1
UNION ALL SELECT 'c', '{t1}', CAST(COUNT(*) AS STRING), CAST(IFNULL(MAX(_apitap_lsn), -1) AS STRING), NULL
          FROM {T1} WHERE _apitap_op != 'B' {PRUNE(t1)}
UNION ALL …
```

  A table with no `'m'` row has `pending = None`. It is followed by `compact_pending_if_bloated()` (best effort, never fails the run): when `tables.get(P).numRows > 4096`, `DELETE FROM {P} x WHERE x.at < TIMESTAMP_SUB(CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(), INTERVAL 7 DAY) AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM {P} y WHERE y.dest_table = x.dest_table AND y.source_id = x.source_id AND y.at > x.at)`. It never deletes the newest row per key, so a race cannot lose a live marker. A WRITE_TRUNCATE compaction like `compact_state` (sink/bigquery.rs:634-682) is rejected here: it loses rows appended between its read and its truncate.
- **`facts_group(asks)`**: `UNION ALL` of `SELECT '{t}', CAST(COUNT(*) AS STRING), CAST(IFNULL(MAX(_apitap_seq),-1) AS STRING), CAST(COUNTIF(_apitap_seq >= {b}) AS STRING), CAST(IFNULL(MAX(IF(_apitap_seq >= {b}, _apitap_seq, NULL)),-1) AS STRING), CAST(COUNT(DISTINCT _apitap_seq) AS STRING) FROM {T} WHERE _apitap_lsn = {stamp} AND _apitap_op != 'B' {PRUNE}`.
- **`apply_group_changelog` (680-719):**
  1. Probe (members with `Ask::Probe`) → `memo.probed`.
  2. `facts_group` for `Ask::Facts`.
  3. `stage_changelog` per member (`buffer_unordered(lanes)` as at 688-695): `memo.plan`; if `!to_append().is_empty()`, NDJSON of `events[to_append()]` with `push_change(..., plan.seq_of(i))` (1455-1460, `seq: u32`) and the load (843-845).
  4. `fn changelog_group_sql(plan, …) -> Vec<String>` (pure) returns, in order:
     - `DELETE FROM {T} WHERE _apitap_lsn = {stamp} AND _apitap_op != 'B' AND _apitap_seq >= {x} {PRUNE};` if trim.
     - `INSERT INTO {P} (dest_table, source_id, lsn, seq_base, end_lsn, events, at) VALUES ('{t}','{s}',{start},{seq_base},{end},{events},CURRENT_TIMESTAMP());` if marker.
     - The existing `INSERT INTO {T} (…) SELECT …, CAST({stamp} AS INT64), CAST(_apitap_seq AS INT64), CURRENT_TIMESTAMP() FROM {staging};` if append.
  5. `BqUnit::push_group(table, that Vec)`. A's `close_unit` appends the state INSERT for `Watermark::Set{lsn: plan.watermark()}` into the same Vec.
  6. `memo.committed` after that transaction's commit.
- **Deleted:** `state_sql` inside `stage_changelog` (790, 792, 795, 881); the no-event early returns (791-796).
- **Why BigQuery needs a marker although INSERT and state are one transaction:** a group is packed into several transactions (`CHUNK_BYTES`, dest_bq.rs:41). If table A's transaction commits and table B's fails, the next run restarts from the group minimum (run.rs:1013, :1261) and re-delivers A's window from the same start.

**BigQuery job identity (sink/bigquery.rs; closes §3.12's lost-POST/failed-poll re-execution).**

```rust
fn fresh_job_id() -> String { format!("apitap_cdc_{}", uuid::Uuid::new_v4().simple()) }  // 43 chars, [a-z0-9_]; uuid v4 is a dep (Cargo.toml:79)
enum ScriptErr { Transport(String), Failed(String) }                                       // module-private
enum Retry { SameId, NewId }
fn next_try(e: &ScriptErr, attempt: u32) -> Option<Retry>;  // Transport & retryable → SameId; Failed & retryable → NewId; else None; attempt >= 5 → None
fn script_body(sql: &str, project: &str, job_id: &str, location: &str) -> Value;         // configuration.query + jobReference{projectId, jobId, location}
fn adopt_existing(job: &Value, sql: &str) -> bool;  // job["configuration"]["query"]["query"] == sql
async fn job_location(&self) -> Result<String>;     // self.location, else GET {BQ_BASE}/projects/{p}/datasets/{d} → "location", cached in a tokio OnceCell field
async fn cdc_script_once(&self, sql: &str, job_id: &str) -> std::result::Result<(), ScriptErr>;
```

- **`cdc_script_once` (718-738):**
  - Always sends `jobReference` with `jobId` and `location`; today it sends no `jobId` and a location only when configured (721-724).
  - An `api()` error whose text contains `"bigquery 409 "` → `GET jobs/{id}?location=`. If `adopt_existing`, poll it. Otherwise `Failed("bigquery job id {id} belongs to another query")`, which is not retryable.
  - Any other POST error → `Transport`.
- **`poll_job` (519-541)** is split: an `api()` error → `Transport`; `status.errorResult` → `Failed`; the 30-minute timeout → `Failed`.
- **`cdc_script` (703-716):** `let mut id = fresh_job_id();`, then loop on `next_try` with the existing backoff `200 << attempt` ms. `NewId` re-mints `id`. A job id runs at most once, so a lost POST or a failed poll re-polls the same job and never re-executes a committed script.
- **`cdc_load_ndjson_once`:** gets the same jobId/409 treatment. That is consistency only: WRITE_TRUNCATE makes a repeated load identical.
- `retryable` (891-903) is unchanged.

#### 3.E `naming::state_verdict`: who may read a state row (closes §3.7 and the bulk-lane twins)

In naming.rs, after `pg_state_keys` (903-908):

```rust
pub(crate) const STATE_CURSOR_LSN: &str = "_lsn";      // replaces dest_pg.rs:13, dest_my.rs:22, dest_ch.rs:21
pub(crate) const STATE_MODE_CDC: &str = "log_based";
#[derive(Debug, Clone)] pub(crate) struct StateRow { watermark: Option<String>, cursor_col: String, mode: String }
impl StateRow { pub(crate) fn new(watermark: Option<String>, cursor_col: Option<String>, mode: Option<String>) -> Self } // NULL → ""
pub(crate) enum Lane<'a> { Cdc, Cursor { cursor: &'a str } }
pub(crate) enum StateVerdict { Absent, Mine(Option<String>), Foreign(StateRow) }
pub(crate) fn state_verdict(row: Option<StateRow>, lane: Lane<'_>) -> StateVerdict;
//   None → Absent
//   Cdc:    mode == CDC && cursor_col == "_lsn" → Mine, else Foreign
//   Cursor: mode == CDC → Foreign; cursor_col non-empty && != cursor → Foreign; else Mine
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord)] pub(crate) struct CdcWatermark(u64);   // private ctor
impl CdcWatermark { pub(crate) fn get(self) -> u64 }
pub(crate) fn cdc_watermark(dest: &str, row: Option<StateRow>) -> Result<Option<CdcWatermark>>;
//   Absent → None; Mine(None) → Transfer("log_based: {dest}: state row has NULL watermark");
//   Mine(Some(w)) → parse u64 else Transfer("log_based: bad LSN state '{w}'"); Foreign → foreign_state_error
pub(crate) fn cursor_watermark(dest: &str, cursor: &str, row: Option<StateRow>) -> Result<Option<String>>;
pub(crate) fn foreign_state_error(dest: &str, lane: Lane<'_>, row: &StateRow) -> Error;  // InvalidInput, three texts:
```

- **CDC row, cursor lane:** "{dest}: this destination table is CDC-managed — its state watermark is an LSN, which a cursor-lane run cannot resume from. Keep using mode=\"log_based\", or clear this table's _apitap_state rows to hand it to the cursor lane (the next run then re-bootstraps)."
- **Cursor row, CDC lane:** "log_based: {dest} is managed by mode='{mode}' — its state watermark tracks cursor '{cursor_col}', not an LSN, so a CDC drain cannot resume from it. Keep using that mode, or clear this table's _apitap_state rows (every spelling of its name) to hand it to CDC, which then re-bootstraps with a full load."
- **Cursor mismatch:** "{dest}: the state row tracks cursor '{row}' but this run uses cursor '{cursor}' — a watermark in one column's terms cannot resume another's. Re-run with cursor=\"{row}\", or clear this table's _apitap_state rows to restart from a full load."

These keep every phrase e2e_state_contract.py greps: "CDC-managed"/"LSN", "cursor"/"LSN", and both quoted cursors.

The contract every read obeys: select `watermark, cursor_col, mode`, keyed by the lane's canonical keys, with **no predicate on mode or cursor_col**; build a `StateRow`; go through `state_verdict`.

**CDC lane.** `Dest::read_state` (run.rs:259-266) returns `Result<Option<CdcWatermark>>`. Callers at run.rs:965/1013 (`.min()` then `.get()`), 1105 (`prev.get() != server`) and 1190.
- **dest_pg.rs:349-385:** the same dual-spelling SELECT (350-364); the inline verdict (365-384) becomes `cdc_watermark(dest_table, row.map(|(w,c,m)| StateRow::new(w, Some(c), Some(m))))`.
- **dest_my.rs:274-315:** `SELECT watermark, cursor_col, mode FROM {db}._apitap_state WHERE dest_table IN (?, ?) AND source_id = ? ORDER BY (dest_table = ?) DESC LIMIT 1`, bound `(bare(dest_table), dest_table, source_id, dest_table)`. This deletes `AND mode = 'log_based'` (284) and the check at 298-313. The bulk lane keys `self.bare` (sink/mysql.rs:1128) and this lane writes `dest_table` as given, so both spellings are read.
- **dest_ch.rs:430-472:** the verdict at 461-471 becomes `cdc_watermark`.
- **dest_bq.rs:355-373:** likewise. A NULL watermark now errors where BigQuery returned `Ok(None)` (368-369). Returning `Ok(None)` would silently re-bootstrap a torn CDC row.
- **Iceberg:** `cdc_read_state` (sink/iceberg.rs:1556-1585) returns `Option<StateRow>`. If the cursor property is absent → `None` (as today, 1565-1566). Otherwise `StateRow::new(props[wm], Some(cur), Some(if cur == STATE_CURSOR_LSN { STATE_MODE_CDC } else { "cursor" }))`. `IceDest::read_state` (dest_ice.rs:56-62) → `cdc_watermark`.

**Bulk lane.** In every case the verdict runs **before** the empty-table early return, so `TRUNCATE`-to-resync still works for a table the lane owns, and a table owned by the other lane is refused whether or not it has rows.
- **sink/postgres.rs dest_state:** move the state read (975-1014) above the emptiness check (945-956); its row goes through `cursor_watermark` (deleting the inline branches at 987-1012).
- **sink/mysql.rs dest_state:** move the `has_state_table` block (1113-1145) above `n == 0` (1090-1101). The SELECT (1125) becomes `SELECT watermark, cursor_col, mode FROM … WHERE dest_table='{bare}' AND source_id='{sid}'` → `cursor_watermark`.
- **sink/clickhouse.rs dest_state:** the state read (1636-1649) becomes `SELECT watermark, cursor_col, mode FROM _apitap_state FINAL WHERE dest_table = '{final_bare}' AND source_id = '{sid}' FORMAT TabSeparatedRaw` and moves above `n == 0` (1613-1618). A missing `_apitap_state` (`UNKNOWN_TABLE`) → `None`.
- **sink/bigquery.rs dest_state:** the state query (2141-2170) moves above `data_wm.is_none()` (2131); its own-row subquery selects `watermark, cursor_col, mode` → `cursor_watermark`; the siblings flag is unchanged.
- **sink/iceberg.rs dest_state:** `own` (991-994) = `cursor_watermark(table, cursor, row)?` with the same `StateRow` construction. An Iceberg cursor switch now refuses instead of re-bootstrapping from data.

**Enforcement.** `naming::tests::no_state_read_filters_on_mode`:
- It `include_str!`s the eight files (dest_pg/my/ch/bq.rs, sink/postgres/mysql/clickhouse/bigquery.rs).
- It skips lines whose trimmed start is `//` and lowercases and strips whitespace from the rest.
- It asserts none contains `andmode=` or `wheremode=`.
- It is RED on v0.56.0 (dest_my.rs:284).

#### 3.F Order of implementation (each step lands with its RED-first test)

The cross-cluster order is E guard, then A + C lineage, then **B**, then D, then C pipes. Inside B:
1. naming.rs E + E1-E4 and the grep test (RED: dest_my.rs:284).
2. collapse.rs `DeleteSet` (B1 RED).
3. resolve.rs `Resolved`/`Image`/`moved_from` (B2, C2).
4. changelog.rs `moved_from` + carry (C1 RED).
5. window.rs + drain.rs + mysource.rs + apply signatures (A1/A2 RED, T1).
6. replay.rs + ClickHouse/BigQuery changelog paths on A's unit API (D1-D9, T2/T2b/T3/T3b).
7. sink/bigquery.rs job ids (D10).
8. BigQuery moved-mask MERGE (C3, T5b).
9. Bulk verdict sites (T6).

**Upgrade from 0.56.0 needs no operator step:**
- The ClickHouse pending table is ALTERed in place, and old rows read `events = 0` (legacy).
- The BigQuery pending table is created on first use.
- R1 absorbs any 0.55.x stamp collision.
- The idle-drain upgrade procedure cluster E drafted is **dropped** (rule for E): leg T3 below is N8's RED control.

### 4. Invariants and the argument

- **A1. No layout, no window.**
  - `TableWindow::seal` is the only constructor, and it takes an `Arc<Layout>`. Every apply reads columns, oids and keys from `w.layout()`.
  - After this change, `grep -rn "missing WAL column list" crates/` is empty.
  - MySQL TRUNCATE-only walk:
    1. The TRUNCATE arm calls `layout_for`, which reads `information_schema` if needed.
    2. `drain_tx` builds `Collapser::new(layout)` and `.truncate()`.
    3. The window is cut at the DDL.
    4. `seal` → `tables[t] = TableWindow{layout, Collapsed{truncate: true, ..}}`.
    5. Every engine truncates and moves the watermark to `id.end()`.
  - The changelog lane builds `Changes::new(layout)` the same way.
- **A2. A window never spans a layout change, and a straddling transaction is loud.**
  - MySQL: every DDL invalidates all layouts and cuts a window that holds a body.
  - Postgres: a value-changed Relation for a table with a body carries the whole transaction into the next window.
  - A single source transaction that alters a tracked table between two of its own row changes makes `seal` fail with the message above. Today that case misrenders or panics: `push_upsert` indexes `cols[i]` past its end (dest_bq.rs:1437).
  - Closes the races-attack column shift: the stale-cache path is gone because the cache is cleared in the same statement as the schemas.
- **B1. `Collapsed::deletes` has no duplicate for any sequence.** It is built from map keys in `finish`. `debug_assert!` is unnecessary: map keys are unique.
- **B2. `Resolved::rows()` yields each key exactly once.** `finals` is keyed by `index`, and `delete_only` excludes every `index` key including `Gone`. So a BigQuery staging body carries at most one row per key, and the MERGE's "at most one source row" condition holds. Both routes of §3.11 are covered: D/I/D and Gone+deletes.
- **C1. A moved row's masked cells equal the last value the window carried for the OLD key, else the destination's row at the OLD key.**
  - Changelog: from the `carry`/`base` lookup on `moved_from`.
  - BigQuery replica: from the MERGE's LEFT JOIN on `_apitap_from_*`.
  - Chains resolve to the first origin.
  - D images never write `carry`, so the D half cannot erase the value the U half needs.
  - After a T in-window, `base` is not consulted, because pre-truncate values are not the row's.
- **D1. After an apply completes, the non-`B` seqs at stamp `s` are exactly `{seq_base .. seq_base+k-1}` above any foreign rows, and no `(lsn, seq)` pair repeats.**
  - Rule by rule:
    - R1 appends above `max+1`.
    - R2 torn trims `≥ base`, then appends from `base`.
    - R2 intact appends `[base+n, base+n_ev)`.
    - R2 shorter trims `≥ base+n_ev`.
    - Nothing else writes the stamp.
  - ClickHouse crash walk (steps TRIM → MARK → APPEND → WATERMARK):
    - Kill before MARK → the marker still names `s` (the attempt's replay reached R2) or an earlier start, and nothing was appended (R1 correct).
    - Kill after MARK, before or during APPEND → R2 sees a prefix or a torn set and resumes or trims.
    - Kill after APPEND → R2 prefix, `skip = n`.
  - The ordering attack (marker in `close_unit`) is closed by R-B1: MARK precedes APPEND in `changelog_steps`, pinned by test D8.
  - BigQuery: the trim, marker, rows and state for one table are one transaction (R-B1, `pack_whole_groups`), so each attempt is atomic. A group split across transactions is healed by the marker on the next run's first window.
- **D2. Each source event appears exactly once in the log, under exactly one stamp.**
  - Shorter replay: R2c trims the tail. `id.end()` becomes the watermark, and the next window re-delivers the tail under its own stamp, which is > `s` and marker-free, so R1 applies with no foreign rows.
  - Absent member (both attacks): `n_ev = 0`, the marker names `s` → R2c trims everything the attempt landed.
  - A clean 0.56.0-on-0.56.0 crash with a legacy marker → base 0, identical to 0.56.0.
- **D3. Rule 1 continues seq past foreign rows, and `__current` ranks the new window's events above them.**
  - The first window of each run is always probed, and `ceiling` covers every earlier stamp. `ask` returns `Nothing` only for `start > ceiling` with no marker at `start`.
  - On BigQuery the `PRUNE` bound holds for every row at a stamp ≥ this run's first start (argued in §3.D).
- **D4. An unmatched combination is loud.**
  - A marker past `start` → R0 `Err` (the rewind case; the gate's `rewind()` rewinds exactly to the marker's start, so R2 applies).
  - A legacy marker with duplicate seqs at the stamp (the attacker's 0.55→0.56→0.57 case) → `Err`; 0.57 no longer silently trims 0.55.x rows.
- **D5. A BigQuery script runs under one client job id.**
  - Transport failures re-POST the same id: 409 → verify the text → poll.
  - Only a DONE-and-failed job is re-issued under a new id, and a failed transaction committed nothing.
  - Cross-process collision needs two identical 128-bit v4 UUIDs. Even then, `adopt_existing` refuses a job whose text differs (the theoretical attack).
- **E1. Every `_apitap_state` read in both lanes on all four SQL engines selects the three columns with no mode predicate, and `state_verdict` decides.** The grep test enforces it. `CdcWatermark` cannot be built outside naming.rs, so no engine can parse a watermark past the verdict.
- **E2. In both directions, a cross-lane run on MySQL, ClickHouse, BigQuery, Postgres and Iceberg exits non-zero before it writes anything.** The bulk verdict runs before staging; the CDC verdict runs at admission (run.rs:963-970).
- **Cross-cluster dependency, stated not re-argued:** "a claimed drain writes nothing more" is cluster A's (OWNER_PRED monotone, BigQuery fence). D1 relies on it only to exclude a second concurrent writer at the same stamp.

### 5. Per engine

| engine | mechanism | weaker there, honestly |
|---|---|---|
| Postgres dest (dest_pg.rs) | `Layout` replaces `wal_cols`/`pk_indices` (408-429); `cdc_watermark` in `read_state`; `DeleteSet` into `_ap_del`; bulk verdict moved above emptiness | Unaffected by D (no changelog). §3.D residue apply unchanged. |
| MySQL dest (dest_my.rs) | Same as Postgres; `read_state` reads both spellings with no mode predicate; bulk `dest_state` verdict first | TRUNCATE-under-fence (§3.8) and dead `OR collected` (§3.9) belong to cluster A. |
| ClickHouse dest (dest_ch.rs) | Layout; `probe`/`facts`/`changelog_steps` via A's owned statements; marker ALTERed in place; legacy detection by `events = 0`; verdict on both lanes | TRIM is a lightweight DELETE: `_apitap_lsn` is not a sort-key prefix, so it scans the table's parts, only on a replay. `probe` scans `_apitap_lsn` once per table per run. The marker table is `ReplacingMergeTree(at)`: a merge after a backward clock step can keep the older version (§8). |
| BigQuery dest (dest_bq.rs, sink/bigquery.rs) | Layout; one-loop `Resolved` staging; moved-mask MERGE join; marker in `_apitap_cdc_pending` inside the table's transaction; probe once per group per run; client job ids | +1 query job per group per run (probe) and +1 per replayed window (facts). The replay trim DELETEs committed log rows, transactionally, with the next window re-appending them under its own stamp: a consumer that read them in between sees them again under a new `(lsn, seq)`. `PRUNE` needs the default `_apitap_at` partitioning to save bytes; any other partition key scans. |
| Iceberg dest (dest_ice.rs) | Layout; `Resolved::rows()` as the delete set and data; `masked_mut` for the refetch; `cdc_watermark` over a synthesized `StateRow` | Mode is synthesized from the cursor property. The cursor-switch behaviour changes from data re-bootstrap to refusal (documented). Changelog is refused upstream (run.rs:343-359). |
| MySQL/MariaDB source (mysource.rs) | `MySession.layouts` + `layout_for`; truncate always lands in order; DDL invalidates all and cuts the window; `drain_tx` never skips silently | The layout comes from `information_schema` at drain time, not from the log. A DDL between event and catalog is still caught only by the length check (560-575), so a same-count reshuffle is undetectable (pre-existing). |
| Postgres source (drain.rs) | `DrainSession.layouts`; value-compared Relations; transaction carried past a layout change | A single transaction that alters a tracked table between its own row changes is refused, not split. |

### 6. Tests

**Unit tests. Each is RED on v0.56.0 or against a named transcription of the old rule.**

| id | test | asserts | RED control |
|---|---|---|---|
