The lock and lease stay held, renewed by the keeper, through the load and `bootstrap_finish`; the exit arm below is the only release."
- MySQL twin: **delete** :1220-1224 and rewrite :1212-1219 the same way. The closure at :1226-1235 becomes `let run_c = run.clone(); myrun::bootstrap(&pool, &ctxs, opts, |table_arg, o2| { let (su, du, r) = (su.clone(), du.clone(), run_c.clone()); async move { let rep = Box::pin(crate::transfer_within(&r, &su, &du, &table_arg, &o2)).await?; Ok((rep.rows, rep.parallel)) } })`.
- myrun.rs:143-178 is untouched.

**If cluster A lands first,** these deletions apply to whatever form A's `Tenure` takes. The rule is "no code releases a CDC lock or lease before the exit arm".

Order for a first run after the change:
1. mint `run` (Cdc), `lease_open`, `announce` each, `check_peers` each, keeper start (:945-953), `read_state` (none).
2. For each member, `transfer_within(run, replace)`: the child mints a Swap token with `parent = run`, announces its own lock, scans, sees the parent lock as `Parent`, loads, swaps and releases its own lock.
3. `bootstrap_finish` for each member, under the still-present Cdc lock.
4. Keeper stop (:1032 / :1326), `release_ok` + `lease_close` (:1038-1043 / :1332-1337).

#### 3.9 Implementation order across clusters

E (guard) first, then A together with C Part §3.8 (lineage), then B, then D (planner, pipe count, read_impl), then C §3.1-§3.7 (pipes). §3.3-§3.5 are independent and can land any time.

### 4. Invariants and the argument

**I1: single exit.** `join_all` has no `return` inside its loop and runs until `join_next()` yields `None`, so the `JoinSet` is empty at return. `Pipes::run`, `run_tasks` and hence every `run_workers` resolve only through it. `run_inline` awaits `drive` itself. The only other way out is the `JoinSet` being dropped from above, which happens only on the read path (`ReadHandle::drop`, read.rs:94-98) and aborts every task. This holds on the shared runtime and on current_thread alike, since nothing is detached.

**I2: exactly-once consumption.**
- A source never holds an `L`: `run_workers` receives `Pipes<L>`, whose field is module-private, and `Pipe<L>` exposes no `finish`, `abort` or `into_inner`.
- `drive` is module-private and takes `self`, so a `&mut Pipe` cannot reach it.
- In `drive`, `inner.take()` happens once, and every arm (Ok, Err, panic) calls `finish` or `abort` on that value.
- `Pipes::open` consumes partially opened loaders via `abort`.
- The one un-driven drop is the read-path cancel, where the `ArrowLoader` owes nothing.
- On pg-overlap, MySQL and ClickHouse, even a drop now aborts server-side (`FinishMarked`: no `Finish`, so an `Err` item).

**I3: a failed set never begins a commit.** `claim()` is the only writer of the latch and runs in every failure arm before `abort`. After it:
- `Spans::next`, `Pipe::send` and `Pipe::send_framed` return `Err(cancelled())`.
- A body that returns `Ok` sees `is_set()` and takes the abort arm.

The one window left: a pipe whose `is_set()` check passed before the claim may be inside `finish` when the claim lands. That stream commits into this run's staging. I5 then sweeps it, because `discard` starts only after `join_all` returns, and that task's `finish` has completed by then.

**I4: the cause is preserved.** `claim()` is a CAS, so exactly one task per set returns `Outcome::Cause`. Every task that fails after it returns `Cancelled`. `join_all` returns the `Cause` whatever the completion order. Walk: worker 0 (BigQuery) fails. It claims, then spends 90 s in `abort` waiting for a load job. Siblings see the latch at their next send, return `Err(cancelled)`, lose the CAS, abort and complete first as `Cancelled`. `join_all` keeps looping until worker 0 completes with `Cause(e0)`, and returns `e0`.

**I5: discard after the last write.** `pipeline::run` calls `sink.discard()` (pipeline/mod.rs:489) only after `run_workers` returns (I1), and by then every loader has returned from `finish` or `abort` (I2). Each loader's `abort` returns only when nothing it started can still write:
- PgCopyLoader awaits its sender (:1376-1381).
- MySQL awaits `join` (:1325).
- ClickHouse awaits the request (:2088).
- S3 and Iceberg run `abort_multipart` synchronously (s3.rs:1087-1090, iceberg.rs:2061-2064).
- GCS cancels the session (gcs.rs:1030-1034).
- BigQuery awaits every job to DONE (§3.4).

The §3.3 wedge cannot occur: a late `complete_multipart` or load job re-creating `<token>/part-*` or `<staging>_i` after `discard`'s list-and-delete would need a `finish` begun after the claim (excluded by I3) or a job not yet terminal (excluded by §3.4). The residual is BigQuery when both the poll task and the second `poll_job` hit transport errors. Then the job's terminal state is unproven and a late `CREATE_IF_NEEDED` can re-create one `<staging>_i`. The next run's `locked:` error names it; that is the 0.56.0 behaviour, now requiring two failures.

**I6: no draining after failure.** Spans pulled after the claim: 0, because `next()` checks the latch first. A sibling's over-read is bounded by the span it is executing: it stops at its next `send` (≤ `chunk`, 4 MiB default) or its next `next()`. Its COPY/SELECT connection is dropped on return, and the server stops on its next socket write. Because the claim precedes `abort` (D1), this holds during a slow BigQuery abort or an S3 `abort_multipart`.

**I7: lock continuity.** No statement between the end of the announce loop (run.rs:917-926 / :1149-1158) and the exit arm (:1032-1043 / :1326-1337) drops the Cdc lock or closes its lease. The only such code (:1006-1010, :1220-1224) is deleted. The keeper (:945-953 / :1176-1182) renews through the bootstrap. The child's `release()` drops only its own token's lock: `release_ok(dest_table, &child_run)` builds the name from the child's token.

**I8: parent ownership is one-directional.**
- `classify(parent lock, mine=child)`: `is_self` is false, `is_parent` is true, so `Parent`.
- `classify(child lock, mine=parent)`: the parent has `parent: None`, so `Live`.
- `classify(other Cdc lock, mine=child)`: `Live`.

The delete arms match `Found::Mine` only, so the parent's artifacts, including cluster E's shadow marker `<t>_<cdcTok>__apitap_staging`, are never deleted by the child. This closes the attack on bigquery.rs:1709-1710 and on E's `mine_staging`.

**I9: a second first run is refused.** Run B starts at any instant of A's bootstrap. B announces, then scans. A's Cdc lock is present (I7), and for B it is `Live(Cdc)` because B's `parent` is `None`. `lock_blocks` (naming.rs:379-384) refuses Cdc against Cdc, so B gets `Error::Locked` and its error arm (:927-936 / :1160-1169) releases its own lock and lease. B never reaches `read_state`, so it writes no `_apitap_state` row.

The MySQL twin announces and checks at :1147-1170, before `present == 0` at :1211. That closes the §6 medium item "two MySQL-source first runs can both bootstrap".

The pg slot `active` check (run.rs:1353-1366) stays as a second line of defence.

**I10: semantics unchanged elsewhere.** `Rows` reproduces each source's current return expression exactly (table in §3.7), so the value fed to `sink.rows_staged` (pipeline/mod.rs:476) is identical. Progress bytes: the bulk lane is counted exactly as `Counted` did, and the read lane is not, as before (read_impl.rs never wrapped). Read cancellation still goes through `ReadHandle`.

**Attack corners:**
- "Cancel latency = the failing worker's abort" is closed by D1.
- "Child deletes the parent's marker" is closed by D2 and I8.
- "Bootstrap livelock under a per-minute probe" is not closed; see §8 for why the proposed skip is unsound.
- The loader-open drop site is closed by D4.
- The Serial PgCopyIn drop depends on the sqlx verification in §3.3. It is reachable only if the Serial loader is ever dropped rather than aborted, which I2 excludes.

### 5. Per engine

| Engine | Mechanism | Weaker there |
|---|---|---|
| Postgres source | `PgBody`. Raw (`Walsender`) and sqlx planes loop on `spans.next()?` and stop at the next `pipe.send`. | Over-read is bounded by one span: `span_mult` is 6 (dispatch.rs:68), so parallel=4 gives 24 spans, about 4% of the table per in-flight span. |
| MySQL source | `MyBody::{Raw,Row}` for transfer; `ArrowBody` plus `run_tasks` for the read lane. | Same one-span bound. |
| ClickHouse source | `ChBody`; `next()` before `count_of`. | Same. |
| csv, github, gsheets, github+api | `run_inline` into the same `drive`. | None: single stream, no siblings. |
| Postgres sink | COPY abort plus awaited sender; drop now aborts (`FinishMarked`). `discard`'s DROP no longer waits behind sibling COPY INs. | Serial path relies on sqlx's `PgCopyIn::drop` (verify, §3.3). |
| MySQL sink | Err item plus awaited join; drop now aborts. | None. |
| ClickHouse sink | Body error plus awaited request; drop now aborts. | With `APITAP_CH_MAX_BODY`, already-rolled requests committed into staging; `discard` drops them (as today). |
| S3 / Iceberg | Synchronous `abort_multipart`; `discard` also aborts incomplete uploads under the run prefix. | None for correctness. |
| GCS | Session cancel. | No gate leg: the SA is BigQuery-only. Covered by construction only. |
| BigQuery | Best-effort `jobs.cancel` for each pending job, then await to DONE. | Failure-path wall time is bounded by the in-flight job (seconds to about 2 min for ≤ `ROTATE_HARD_BYTES` = 96 MiB, :55). A double transport failure leaves the job's end unproven (I5 residual). |
| CDC lineage on pg, MySQL, ClickHouse, BigQuery destinations | `Found::Parent` through `blockers` (pg) or the sink's own `classify` match (my/ch/bq); the release loops are deleted. | Iceberg CDC is unguarded by design (run.rs:144-157) and stays so. |

### 6. Tests

All unit tests live in `pipe.rs` `mod tests` unless noted. They use a `FakeLoader { id, log: Arc<Mutex<Vec<Ev>>>, fail_send_at: Option<usize>, abort_delay: Duration, sends: usize }` with `enum Ev { Send(usize), Finish(usize), Abort(usize, String) }`.

1. **`first_error_cancels_siblings_and_consumes_every_pipe`.** 4 pipes, 400 spans. Body 0 returns `Err("boom")` after one send. Bodies 1-3 loop `spans.next()?` → `pipe.send(vec![0;8]).await?` → `yield_now`. An `alive: AtomicUsize` is incremented at body start and decremented at end.
   - Assert: `Err` contains "boom"; the log has 4 `Abort`, 0 `Finish`; `spans.remaining() > 0`; `alive == 0` when `run` returns; the siblings' abort causes start with "cancelled:".
   - **RED:** a test-local copy of the 0.56.0 loop (`tokio::spawn` per loader, `for t in tasks { t.await?? }`, explicit `finish` at body end, a `pop` queue) returns after 1 `Abort`. It later logs 3 `Finish` with `remaining()==0`. Run it first, see it fail the assertions, then delete the copy.
2. **`the_cause_wins_even_when_its_abort_is_slowest`.** Body 0 fails and its `abort_delay` is 300 ms. Siblings stop immediately.
   - Assert: result is "boom"; every sibling's `Abort` timestamp is before body 0's abort completes (I6 under a slow abort); `remaining() > 0`.
   - **RED:** move `claim()` after `abort` in `drive`. The siblings drain (`remaining()==0`), and with a completion-order `first` the error is `cancelled:`.
3. **`a_failing_send_is_still_aborted`.** `fail_send_at = Some(2)`, body in the `?` shape, via `run_inline`.
   - Assert: exactly 1 `Abort` carrying the send error, 0 `Finish`, the same error returned.
   - **RED:** the csvfile.rs:330-341 shape on an owned `L`: the log is empty.
4. **`panicking_body_still_aborts_its_pipe`.** Body 2 of 3 panics after one send.
   - Assert: 3 `Abort` (one containing "worker panicked"), 0 `Finish`, `Err`, `alive == 0`.
   - **RED:** remove `catch_unwind`: 2 `Abort`, and the `JoinError` path fires.
5. **`a_body_that_drains_after_the_failure_does_not_finish`.** 2 pipes, 1 span each. Body 0 fails; body 1 completes its span and returns `Ok` without calling `next()` again.
   - Assert: body 1's pipe is `Abort("cancelled:…")`, not `Finish`.
   - **RED:** drop the `if !self.latch.is_set()` guard.
6. **`partial_open_aborts_the_open_pipes`.** `Pipes::open(4, ..)` where the third open fails.
   - Assert: 2 `Abort("a sibling pipe failed to open")`, `Err` returned.
   - **RED:** the 0.56.0 loop (pipeline/mod.rs:467-472 copied): 0 events.
7. **`rows_rule_matches_each_source`.** `Rows::{FromLoader, Own, LoaderElseOwn}` × fake `finish` returning 0 or 7, against the table in §3.7.
   - **RED:** replace `resolve` with `rep > 0 ? rep : own`: `Own(5)` with `rep=7` gives 7, not 5.
8. **`run_tasks_loaderless_stops_siblings`.** A `SpanBody` fails; siblings stop at `next()`; the first error is returned; `remaining() > 0`.
   - **RED:** the mysql.rs:1509-1536 loop copied.
9. **`no_spawn_outside_the_crew`** (lint).
   - Via `include_str!`, for each of `source/{mod,postgres,mysql,clickhouse,csvfile,github,gsheets,github_api}.rs`, `pipeline/mod.rs`, `pipeline/dispatch.rs` and `sink/mod.rs`: take the text before the first `#[cfg(test)]`, and assert it contains none of `tokio::spawn`, `task::spawn`, `JoinSet`, `JoinHandle`, `WorkQueue`, `fn pop(`.
   - Also assert that the listed source file set equals `std::fs::read_dir(concat!(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"), "/src/source"))`, so a new source fails until it is listed.
   - **RED:** written before §3.7, it fails on the 0.56.0 tree (postgres.rs:499 and others).
10. **`pipe_surface_is_pinned`** (lint on `include_str!("pipe.rs")`).
    - The `pub(crate) fn` names inside `impl<L: Loader> Pipe<L>` are exactly {send, send_framed, reclaim, framed_capable}.
    - Inside `impl<L: Loader> Pipes<L>`: exactly {open, for_read, len, run, run_inline}.
    - No `pub` on any struct field in the file.
    - **RED:** add `pub(crate) fn into_inner`. This replaces the rejected `compile_fail` doctests (D6).
11. **`sink::tests::finish_marked_errors_when_dropped_without_finish`.**
    - `Buf, Buf, <drop tx>` collects `[Ok, Ok, Err(DROPPED)]`.
    - `Buf, Finish` collects `[Ok]`.
    - `Finish, Buf` collects `[]`.
    - **RED:** the plain `Receiver<io::Result<B>>` of 0.56.0 ends cleanly on drop, `[Ok, Ok]`, which is the commit-on-drop.
12. **`naming::tests::a_child_run_owns_its_parents_lock_one_way`.** `parent = RunId::mint(Cdc, src)`, `child = RunId::mint_within(Swap, src, &parent)`, `other = RunId::mint(Cdc, src)`, using `artifact_ident_run(bare, Artifact::Lock, PG_IDENT_MAX, &x)`.
    - `classify(parent lock, mine=child) == Found::Parent`.
    - `classify(child lock, mine=parent)` is `Found::Live(_)`.
    - `classify(other lock, mine=child)` is `Found::Live(_)`.
    - `blockers(bare, PG_IDENT_MAX, &child, [parent_lock, other_lock])` has exactly one entry, naming `other`'s lock.
    - **RED:** on the v0.56.0 classify (token equality, :628) the first assertion yields `Live`.
13. **`naming::tests::a_childs_view_of_its_parents_staging_is_never_mine`.** `classify(artifact_ident_run(bare, Artifact::Staging, .., &parent), .., Artifact::Staging, .., mine=&child, ..) == Found::Parent`, never `Found::Mine`. This is what keeps the delete arms (bigquery.rs:1710, mysql.rs:613, clickhouse.rs:654, E's `mine_staging`) away from the parent.
    - **RED:** implement `owns()` → `Mine` as the judged design did: `Mine`.

**E2E: `benchmarks/e2e_worker_cancel.py <pg|ch|s3|bq>` (new).**

Setup:
- Source `wc_src` on PG :5544: `CREATE TABLE wc_src(id bigint PRIMARY KEY, pad text)`, filled with 4M rows of `repeat('x',200)` via `generate_series`, then `ANALYZE`.
- Assert `server_version_num >= 140000`, otherwise FAIL with "needs TID range scan".
- The child script runs `apitap.transfer(PG, DST, "wc_src", mode="replace", parallel=4)` in `try`. It prints `RETURNED` or `RAISED <type> <msg>`, **then sleeps 12 s** (D9).

Steps:
1. **Calibration** (controls the metric): `pg_stat_reset_single_table_counters('wc_src'::regclass)`, a full successful run, then `pg_stat_clear_snapshot()` and wait 3 s. `seq_tup_read + coalesce(idx_tup_fetch,0)` must be in [0.9N, 1.3N], else FAIL "counter does not measure the read".
2. Reset the counters and start the child. Poll the source every 50 ms for `SELECT pid FROM pg_stat_activity WHERE datname=current_database() AND state='active' AND query LIKE 'COPY (SELECT%wc_src%' ORDER BY backend_start` until 4 rows are seen (30 s deadline, else FAIL "rig too fast: never saw 4 COPY OUT backends").
3. `pg_terminate_backend(first pid)`. Wait for `RAISED` (180 s); `RETURNED` is a FAIL.

Facts, each asked of the server:
- **F1, source, at RAISED + 2 s** (child alive): the active-COPY count is 0.
- **F2, source, after child exit + 3 s:** tuples read < 0.5 × 4M. After the claim the bound is about 4 in-flight spans ≈ 17%, plus what was read before the kill.
- **F3, destination:**
  - `pg`: `SELECT count(*) FROM pg_class WHERE relname LIKE 'wc\_src\_%\_\_apitap\_%'` = 0.
  - `ch`: `system.tables WHERE name LIKE 'wc_src_%__apitap_%'` = 0, and `system.processes WHERE query ILIKE 'INSERT INTO%wc_src_%apitap%'` = 0, at RAISED+2 and RAISED+10.
  - The destination `wc_src` from the calibration still has 4M rows, an immediate re-run returns OK, and `count` is 4M.
- **`s3` (capability `iceberg`):** DST = `s3://apitap-e2e/wc?format=parquet&endpoint=http://127.0.0.1:9100&access_key_id=bench&secret_access_key=benchbench`. The bucket is created by the leg with a stdlib SigV4 `PUT /apitap-e2e`. At RAISED+2 and RAISED+60:
  - `GET /apitap-e2e?list-type=2&prefix=wc/wc_src__apitap_staging/` returns 0 keys (staging root, s3.rs:725-726).
  - `GET /apitap-e2e?uploads&prefix=wc/` returns 0 `<Upload>`.
  - The re-run raises no `LockedError`, and DuckDB `count(*)` over `read_parquet('s3://apitap-e2e/wc/wc_src/*.parquet')` is 4M (httpfs settings as in e2e_logbased_dests.py:83-85).
- **`bq` (capability `bq`):** DST as in e2e_bq_cdc.py:16-20.
  - Precondition before the kill: `INFORMATION_SCHEMA.JOBS_BY_USER` (not `JOBS_BY_PROJECT`, which needs `jobs.listAll`) in the dataset's region shows ≥1 `job_type='LOAD' AND state='RUNNING' AND destination_table.table_id LIKE 'wc_src%apitap_staging%'` created after the leg start. Otherwise FAIL "no in-flight job to prove against".
  - At RAISED: that count with `state!='DONE'` is 0.
  - At RAISED+2 and RAISED+90: `tables.list` shows 0 tables LIKE `wc_src%__apitap_%`.
  - The re-run raises no `LockedError`.

**RED control:** the same leg under the PyPI 0.56.0 wheel in qa-venv.
- `pg` and `ch`: F2 reads about 0.96N (23 of 24 spans drained). F1 is 0 on pg, because `discard`'s DROP waits behind the siblings (the delayed error); F2 carries these two.
- `s3`: `part-*` under the failed token appears after `RAISED` and the re-run raises `locked:`.
- `bq`: a `<staging>_i` re-appears by +90 s, or the RUNNING job outlives `RAISED`.

**E2E: `benchmarks/e2e_bootstrap_lock.py <pg|my>` (new).**

Setup:
- `pg`: source PG :5544 with `bl_a` (3M rows, PK) and `bl_b` (1k rows, PK), destination PGD :5545.
- `my` (capability `mariadb`): the same tables on MariaDB :3309 (e2e_mariadb_cdc.py:19), destination PGD.
- Warm-up: a `log_based` bootstrap of a 1-row `bl_warm` so that `_apitap_state` exists; then drop `bl_warm`'s state and slot.

Run:
1. Start child A: `transfer(SRC, PGD, ['bl_a','bl_b'], mode='log_based')`.
2. **F1:** poll the destination every 50 ms with one statement:

   ```sql
   SELECT (SELECT count(*) FROM pg_class WHERE relname LIKE 'bl\_a\_%\_\_apitap\_staging'),
          (SELECT count(*) FROM pg_class WHERE relname LIKE 'bl\_a\_%\_\_apitap\_lock'
                                            AND substr(relname, length('bl_a')+9, 1) = 'l')
   ```

   On the first row with staging > 0, assert lock count = 1. If staging is never seen, FAIL "rig too fast".
3. Immediately, on a separate destination session: `BEGIN; LOCK TABLE _apitap_state IN SHARE MODE;` and hold it (D10).
4. **F2:** wait until the destination's `pg_stat_activity` shows A's backend with `wait_event_type='Lock'` on a statement containing `_apitap_state` (120 s). Record which statement it is. Then:
   - the 'l' lock count is 1;
   - `SELECT count(*) FROM _apitap_lease WHERE dest_key='public.bl_a' AND token=<A's token = substr(relname, length('bl_a')+1, 16)> AND NOT collected AND expires_at > now()` is 1;
   - start child B with the same call; B exits non-zero with `LockedError`, and the 'l' lock count is still 1.
5. `COMMIT` the SHARE lock and wait for A to exit 0.
6. **F3:**
   - `SELECT count(*) FROM _apitap_state WHERE mode='log_based' AND dest_table IN ('bl_a','bl_b','public.bl_a','public.bl_b')` = 2;
   - `bl_a` has a PK (`pg_index.indisprimary`);
   - 0 `bl_a%__apitap_lock` relations;
   - 0 `_apitap_lease` rows for A's token.

Cleanup: drop the slot, state rows and tables.

**RED:** under 0.56.0, F1 sees 0 'l' locks during the load (released at run.rs:1006-1010 / :1220-1224), and F2 would see B proceed.

**Gate (benchmarks/gate.py LEGS :44-90), add:**
- `("e2e_worker_cancel.py", "a failed worker stops its siblings before staging is swept", {}, None, ["pg"])`
- the same description with `["ch"]`, and with `"iceberg", ["s3"]`, and with `"bq", ["bq"]`
- `("e2e_bootstrap_lock.py", "a bootstrap keeps the drain's lock to its last statement", {}, None, ["pg"])`
- the same with `"mariadb", ["my"]`

**Existing legs:**
- e2e_cdc_guard.py (:109-117, :157) must still pass: after a bootstrap, `locks() == []`, which is now asserted at run end rather than at handover.
- e2e_failure_modes.py case 3 (:240-275) kills **all** COPY backends, so it cannot see siblings. It is unchanged; the new leg covers the sibling case.
- A happy-path receipt is owed (the project's rule): pg→pg 10M at 0.5 CPU / 256 MB, once, before and after.

### 7. Docs

- **docs/failure-modes.md:287-294.** Replace the "A bootstrap hands the guard over" bullet with: "**A bootstrap keeps the drain's lock.** A first run's full load is a nested run: it takes a swap lock of its own, and that lock recognises the drain that spawned it, so the drain never releases its announcement. The lock and its lease are held from the scan to the last statement of the bootstrap, including the primary-key and watermark writes. A second first run of the same group started at any point in between is refused with `LockedError` and writes nothing." Delete the sentences "What that gives up is the instant between the two … Loudly, either way."
- **docs/failure-modes.md, table row "Source connection cut mid-COPY" (:48), column 2.** Append: "With `parallel > 1`, the other workers stop within one buffer (≤ `chunk_bytes`) of the failure, every destination stream is aborted rather than committed, and staging is swept only after the last of them has ended. On BigQuery the sweep waits for load jobs already submitted to finish or be cancelled." Column 4: add "`e2e_worker_cancel.py`".
- **sink/mod.rs Loader doc (:21-65).** Add "`finish`/`abort` are called only by `pipe::Pipe::drive`; sources hold `&mut Pipe<L>`". Delete the `Counted` doc (:67-72) with the struct.
- **source/mod.rs:79-81.** "Spawn one worker per loader over a shared span queue" becomes "Consume `pipes` with `Pipes::run` or `Pipes::run_inline`, the only two ways; the queue is `pipe::Spans`, which refuses once a sibling failed."
- **naming.rs:565-568.** Add step 3a (§3.8).
- **run.rs:991-1005 and :1212-1219.** Replace as specified in §3.8.
- **README.md, usage.md.** No sentence becomes false; the handover appears only in failure-modes.md.

### 8. What this does not fix

- **Hung sibling.** `join_all` waits for every sibling, so a sibling blocked in a server read that never returns now delays the error. The success path waits for it the same way today. The bounds are the existing HTTP deadlines and pg keepalives. There is no grace-period `abort_all`: aborting drops loaders, and although pg-overlap, MySQL and ClickHouse would now abort server-side, BigQuery and S3 would not.
- **SIGTERM on a bulk run.** `Spans::next` does not consult `crate::shutdown::requested()`. Wiring it in is one line, but it changes bulk SIGTERM semantics; that is a separate decision.
- **Same class, other lanes.** `drain_group` returning past a live `apply_task` (§3.10, run.rs:1596/:1705/:1728) and `Keeper` having no `Drop` (lease.rs:108-156). The CDC-lifecycle cluster owns them. `pipe::Latch` and `join_all` may be reused, but that is not decided here.
- **Parent evicted mid-bootstrap.** If A is paused past the TTL and a new run collects A's lock, A's child continues loading and A's `bootstrap_finish` is an unfenced write. Fencing it is cluster A's `Tenure`. This cluster guarantees continuity only for a live parent (I7).
- **Bootstrap livelock under a frequent schedule** (attack, minor). Probes of a per-minute cron briefly hold their own Cdc lock. They are now refused by the parent's continuous lock (I9), but a child's scan that meets one refuses. The proposed fix, "a child with a live parent ignores lock peers", is unsound. Counterexample: parent P is evicted, a collector Q starts its own bootstrap, and Q's child would ignore P's child's Swap lock. Both replace the same table. This is not a regression; 0.56.0 has the same symmetry. Mitigation: do not schedule first runs more often than a bootstrap takes.
- **Kill during the bootstrap.** A SIGKILL during the full load leaves the child's Swap staging. It carries data and is never collectable (naming.rs:489-503), so the next run refuses and names it. usage.md must say so; that text is owned by cluster E's doc rewrite.
- **BigQuery `jobs.cancel` is best effort.** Only wall time depends on Google. Correctness rests on the await, with the double-transport-failure residual stated under I5.
- **GCS has no gate leg** (the SA is BigQuery-only). It is covered by construction: session cancel plus I1-I5.
- **Serial Postgres COPY-in drop** depends on sqlx's `PgCopyIn::drop` sending `CopyFail`. Verify it on the VPS; it is reachable only outside I2.

---

## §2.D Memory model: the pipe budget counts what the sink holds

### 1. Root cause

The pipeline sets the pipe budget on its own. `mem_capped` (pipeline/mod.rs:263-268) prices every pipe at `10 × chunk` plus a 40 MiB reserve. That figure was fitted on the pg→ch ladder (:245-255). Nothing in `trait Sink` (sink/mod.rs:109-232) lets a destination say what its loader keeps resident.

The four parquet sinks hold memory that does not depend on `chunk`:
- builders up to `ROW_GROUP_BYTES = 24 MiB` (bqparquet.rs:33), whose only flush check runs once per input chunk (:482-484);
- a frame buffer that keeps up to 1 MiB of consumed prefix (:437);
- up to 8 MiB of encoded output before a part ships (s3.rs:26/:1049, gcs.rs:37/:967, iceberg.rs:62/:2015, bigquery.rs:44/:1389).

The repo already has the measurement. At 8 pipes × 4 MiB the whole-container peaks were 758 MB for pg→s3 (benchmarks/README.md:631), 787 MB for pg→gcs (:648) and 773 MB for pg→iceberg (iceberg-showdown.md:18). That is about 85-88 MiB per pipe. The planner prices the same pipe at 40 MiB, so it under-counts by 2.2×. Its thin-chunk lever (:299-310) then buys pipes whose real price it never saw (§3.15).

Iceberg merge has the same cause from the other side. `KeyCap` (bqparquet.rs:319-332) is filled per row (:551-583), never cleared by `flush_row_group` (:658-663) or `finish_file` (:670-677), and concatenated twice at commit (iceberg.rs:1188-1197, :1206). Its size is proportional to the delta, so no per-pipe declaration could state it (§3.16).

There is a third, unreported member of the class. The builder bound is checked only after a whole input chunk has decoded (:482). The builders can therefore overshoot the row group by `expansion × chunk`. For many short text columns that expansion is up to about 10×, because of the 48 B per ByteArray at :144-148.

The class is "memory a sink holds that the one planner never asks about".

### 2. The decision

1. **The sink declares its residency.** Every `Sink` must declare `pipe_residency(mode)`. The single planner `fit()` prices that declaration into `pipe_bytes = (PIPE_CHUNKS + chunks)·chunk + fixed + k·row_group`. It picks `(chunk, row_group, pipes)` together, so the parquet row group becomes a planner output (the `RowGroup` enum 24 → 8 → 4 MiB) and shrinks before a pipe is given up. The encoder enforces its row-group bound per row with an O(1) counter.
2. **Iceberg merge streams its delete file.** Merge writes a one-column equality-delete file in lockstep with each data file, from the same row-group builders, and streams it by multipart. `KeyCap` is deleted, so nothing grows with the delta.
3. **Cost.**
   - Postgres, ClickHouse and MySQL routes, and the Arrow read lane: bit-identical.
   - The headline tier (0.5 CPU / 256 MiB) changes for one route only: Iceberg merge, whose row group drops from 24 to 8 MiB.
   - Capped multi-core parquet cells lose pipes they could not afford (for example 256 MiB / 4 cores: 8 → 5).
   - Merge pays one extra multipart per pipe, and readers see N delete files instead of 1.

### 3. What changes

#### 3.0 Where each fact lives

Each fact is written once.

**crates/apitap-core/src/wire/bqparquet.rs** owns the buffers, so the buffers and their declaration cannot drift apart.

```rust
/// Encoded bytes a parquet loader holds before shipping a part / resumable chunk.
/// S3: ≥ MIN_PART (s3.rs:23, keep its const assert :27). GCS/BigQuery: a multiple of
/// UPLOAD_ALIGN (add `const _: () = assert!(SEND_THRESHOLD % UPLOAD_ALIGN == 0);` in gcs.rs and bigquery.rs).
pub(crate) const SEND_THRESHOLD: usize = 8 << 20;
pub(crate) const FRAME_BUF: usize = 1 << 20;      // replaces the literals at :409 (initial capacity) and :437 (drain threshold)
pub(crate) const PAGE_TRANSIENT: usize = 2 << 20; // one open data page + dictionary page; parquet-rs defaults (1 MiB each), props at :403-409 do not override them
pub(crate) const PER_ROW_GROUP: u64 = 2;          // builders (≤ 1 rg + one row, §3.3) + compressed pages landing in `out` before the part ships (≤ 1 rg)
pub(crate) const LOADER_CHUNKS: u64 = 2;          // `push` copies the input into `buf` (:441) while the worker's `buf: Vec<u8>` argument is alive until `send` returns (s3.rs:1045-1054)

/// A row-group size the planner can choose. There is no integer constructor: every value is a
/// rung the planner priced, or the CDC window size.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub(crate) enum RowGroup { Mib24, Mib8, Mib4, #[cfg(test)] Test(usize) }
impl RowGroup {
    /// Top rung = 0.56.0's ROW_GROUP_BYTES. 8 MiB lets 4 pipes fit 256 MiB/2 cores and
    /// 2 merge pipes fit 256 MiB/0.5 CPU. 4 MiB (floor) lets 2 pipes fit 128 MiB (2×43+40 = 126).
    /// Below 4 MiB, per-row-group metadata and page count start to cost readers.
    pub(crate) const LADDER: [RowGroup; 3] = [RowGroup::Mib24, RowGroup::Mib8, RowGroup::Mib4];
    pub(crate) const fn bytes(self) -> usize { match self { Self::Mib24 => 24 << 20, Self::Mib8 => 8 << 20, Self::Mib4 => 4 << 20, #[cfg(test)] Self::Test(n) => n } }
    /// dest_ice.rs: CDC window files are bounded by the drain's byte budget (logbased/run.rs:492), not by pipes.
    pub(crate) const fn cdc_window() -> Self { Self::Mib24 }
}

/// The only spelling of a parquet lane's per-pipe residency.
pub(crate) fn parquet_residency(key_companion: bool) -> PipeResidency {
    let companion = if key_companion { SEND_THRESHOLD + PAGE_TRANSIENT } else { 0 };
    PipeResidency { fixed: (SEND_THRESHOLD + FRAME_BUF + PAGE_TRANSIENT + companion) as u64,
                    per_row_group: PER_ROW_GROUP, chunks: LOADER_CHUNKS }
}

/// Merge-key type gate, written once. Used by new_ext and by iceberg.rs write_delete_parquet.
pub(crate) fn merge_key_ok(d: &Delivered) -> Result<()>   // Int | Uuid | Text → Ok, else today's InvalidInput text (:367-372)
```

Deleted:
- `ROW_GROUP_BYTES` (:33);
- the local `SEND_THRESHOLD` at s3.rs:26, gcs.rs:37 and iceberg.rs:62;
- `UPLOAD_CHUNK` at bigquery.rs:44 (its one use at :1389 reads `SEND_THRESHOLD`).

Each sink imports `crate::wire::bqparquet::SEND_THRESHOLD`.

`SharedBuf` (:287) gains two methods, so the threshold comparison lives in this file:
- `take_ready(&self) -> Option<Vec<u8>>`: takes the buffer if it holds `>= SEND_THRESHOLD`.
- `take_all(&self) -> Vec<u8>`.

S3Loader (s3.rs:1047-1053), IcebergLoader (iceberg.rs:2013-2019) and the GCS parquet arm (gcs.rs:964-970) call `take_ready`. BigQuery's `drain_aligned` (:1386-1395) covers both of its lanes and keeps its own comparison against `SEND_THRESHOLD`.

**Measurement check.** Non-merge `pipe_bytes` at 4 MiB / Mib24 is `12·4 + 2·24 + 11 = 107 MiB`. The showdowns measured 85-88 MiB, so the model sits about 22 % above measurement. That is the same headroom class as the 10×chunk term (:251-252).

#### 3.1 The declaration: `sink/mod.rs`

```rust
/// What ONE pipe of this sink's loader keeps resident at its worst instant, BEYOND the
/// pipeline's measured chunk-scale term (`pipeline::PIPE_CHUNKS × chunk`), for `mode`.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub(crate) struct PipeResidency {
    pub fixed: u64,         // chunk- and row-group-independent bytes
    pub per_row_group: u64, // multiplier on the planner-chosen RowGroup; 0 = the sink has none
    pub chunks: u64,        // chunk-sized copies the LOADER holds on top of the pipeline term
}
impl PipeResidency {
    /// The loader holds only chunk-scale buffers already inside the measured 10×chunk term.
    pub const STREAMING: Self = Self { fixed: 0, per_row_group: 0, chunks: 0 };
}
```

`trait Sink` (sink/mod.rs:109) gains a **required** associated function with no default, placed after `accepts` (:115):

```rust
/// REQUIRED. A sink that says nothing is a sink the planner cannot see. That is how four
/// parquet pipes were planned into 128 MiB as "4 × 20 MiB" (0.56.0 audit §3.15).
fn pipe_residency(mode: Mode) -> PipeResidency;
```

The seven implementations, one line each with its citation as a comment:

| impl | body | justification |
|---|---|---|
| `PgSink` (postgres.rs:710) | `PipeResidency::STREAMING` | 2-slot send + 4-slot reclaim of chunk Vecs (:1285, :1288) sit inside the term. profiling.md: pg→pg 285 MB @ 8 pipes |
| `ChSink` (clickhouse.rs:1130) | `STREAMING` | zero-copy body, `reclaim` None (sink/mod.rs:36-39); the ladder was fitted on this route |
| `MySqlSink` (mysql.rs:791) | `STREAMING` | profiling.md session 3: 303 MB @ 8 pipes / 1 GB |
| `S3Sink` (s3.rs:882) | `parquet_residency(false)` | README.md:631 |
| `GcsSink` (gcs.rs:702) | `parquet_residency(false)` | worst of its two formats; CSV lane = ≤ SEND_THRESHOLD gzip (:1000) + chunk-scale `csv` |
| `BqSink` (bigquery.rs:1956) | `parquet_residency(false)` | `drain_aligned` ships at ≥ SEND_THRESHOLD (:1389); CSV lane strictly less |
| `IcebergSink` (iceberg.rs:931) | `parquet_residency(mode == Mode::Merge)` | companion = key file's output buffer + page |

#### 3.2 The planner: `pipeline/mod.rs`

**Delete:**
- `mem_capped_parallel` (:256-261), `mem_capped` (:263-268) and the body of `knobs` (:287-312);
- the doc at :245-255, which moves onto `PIPE_CHUNKS`/`RESERVE`.

**Add:**

```rust
pub(crate) const RESERVE: u64 = 40 << 20;   // today's :264
pub(crate) const PIPE_CHUNKS: u64 = 10;     // today's :265: the measured whole-pipe term of the pg→ch ladder
const DEFAULT_CHUNK: usize = 4 << 20;       // from knobs :291
const THIN_CHUNK: usize = 2 << 20;          // from knobs :305
// The Arrow read lane's swept calibration (read_impl.rs:130-153, receipts move here verbatim):
const READ_RESERVE: u64 = 16 << 20;
const READ_PIPE: u64 = 22 << 20;
pub(crate) const READ_PIPE_CAP: usize = 8;

/// One price function for every lane.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
pub(crate) struct PipeCost { pub reserve: u64, pub pipe_chunks: u64, pub residency: PipeResidency }
impl PipeCost {
    pub(crate) const fn transfer(r: PipeResidency) -> Self { Self { reserve: RESERVE, pipe_chunks: PIPE_CHUNKS, residency: r } }
    pub(crate) const READ: Self = Self { reserve: READ_RESERVE, pipe_chunks: 0,
        residency: PipeResidency { fixed: READ_PIPE, per_row_group: 0, chunks: 0 } };
    pub(crate) fn pipe_bytes(&self, chunk: usize, rg: RowGroup) -> u64 {
        let r = &self.residency;
        (self.pipe_chunks + r.chunks) * chunk as u64 + r.fixed + r.per_row_group * rg.bytes() as u64
    }
    fn allowed(&self, chunk: usize, rg: RowGroup, mem: u64) -> u64 { mem.saturating_sub(self.reserve) / self.pipe_bytes(chunk, rg).max(1) }
    fn rungs(&self) -> &'static [RowGroup] { if self.residency.per_row_group > 0 { &RowGroup::LADDER } else { &RowGroup::LADDER[..1] } }
    fn largest_rung(&self, chunk: usize, pipes: u64, mem: u64) -> Option<RowGroup> {
        self.rungs().iter().copied().find(|&rg| self.allowed(chunk, rg, mem) >= pipes)
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub(crate) struct PipeShape { pub chunk: usize, pub row_group: RowGroup, pub pipes: usize }
impl PipeShape {
    pub(crate) fn model_peak(&self, cost: &PipeCost) -> u64 { self.pipes as u64 * cost.pipe_bytes(self.chunk, self.row_group) + cost.reserve }
}

/// Pure. Enumeration order is the tie-break: fat before thin, larger row group before smaller.
/// A candidate replaces the best only on STRICTLY more pipes. That is today's `thin > fat` (:307)
/// and "big boxes keep the deeper buffers" (:302-303), and it makes the row group irrelevant for
/// STREAMING. Scores are clamped to ≥1 exactly like `allowed.max(1)` (:267). One pipe always runs,
/// with the fat chunk (measured at 44-80 MB, profiling.md), and its row group is the largest rung
/// one fat pipe fits, else the floor.
pub(crate) fn fit(ask: usize, pinned: Option<usize>, mem: Option<u64>, cost: &PipeCost) -> PipeShape {
    let ask = ask.max(1) as u64;
    let fat = pinned.unwrap_or(DEFAULT_CHUNK);
    let only = [fat];
    let chunks: &[usize] = if pinned.is_some() { &only } else { &[DEFAULT_CHUNK, THIN_CHUNK] };
    let rungs = cost.rungs();
    let Some(mem) = mem else { return PipeShape { chunk: fat, row_group: rungs[0], pipes: ask as usize } };
    let mut best = (0u64, fat, rungs[0]);
    for &c in chunks { for &rg in rungs {
        let s = cost.allowed(c, rg, mem).min(ask).max(1);
        if s > best.0 { best = (s, c, rg); }
    } }
    if best.0 <= 1 {
        let rg = cost.largest_rung(fat, 1, mem).unwrap_or(*rungs.last().expect("ladder"));
        return PipeShape { chunk: fat, row_group: rg, pipes: 1 };
    }
    PipeShape { chunk: best.1, row_group: best.2, pipes: best.0 as usize }
}

/// An EXPLICIT parallel is never overridden (:294-296). Its row group is the largest rung whose
/// p pipes fit, else the floor. A note is printed only when the engine chose a smaller row group,
/// i.e. never on a STREAMING route.
fn explicit(p: usize, pinned: Option<usize>, mem: Option<u64>, cost: &PipeCost) -> PipeShape {
    let chunk = pinned.unwrap_or(DEFAULT_CHUNK);
    let rungs = cost.rungs();
    let row_group = match mem {
        None => rungs[0],
        Some(m) => cost.largest_rung(chunk, p as u64, m).unwrap_or(*rungs.last().expect("ladder")),
    };
    if row_group != rungs[0] {
        let m = mem.expect("smaller rung implies a limit");
        if cost.allowed(chunk, row_group, m) >= p as u64 {
            crate::progress::note(&format!("parallel={p}: parquet row groups sized to {} MiB so {p} pipes fit the {} MiB memory limit", row_group.bytes() >> 20, m >> 20));
        } else {
            crate::progress::note(&format!("parallel={p} is more than this destination's memory model fits in {} MiB ({} pipes); running {p} as asked with {} MiB row groups", m >> 20, fit(p, pinned, mem, cost).pipes, row_group.bytes() >> 20));
        }
    }
    PipeShape { chunk, row_group, pipes: p }
}

pub(crate) fn knobs(opts: &TransferOptions, profile: &Profile, residency: PipeResidency) -> Result<PipeShape> {
    if opts.parallel == Some(0) { return Err(Error::InvalidInput("parallel must be at least 1".into())); } // :288-290
    let pinned = opts.chunk_bytes.map(|c| c.max(64 * 1024));                                             // :292
    let (cost, mem) = (PipeCost::transfer(residency), mem_limit_bytes());
    let shape = match opts.parallel {
        Some(p) => explicit(p, pinned, mem, &cost),
        None => fit((profile.auto_parallel)(num_cpus::get()), pinned, mem, &cost),                        // :297
    };
    if std::env::var_os("APITAP_DEBUG").is_some() {
        eprintln!("[plan] chunk={}KiB row_group={}MiB pipes={} model_peak={}MiB mem_limit={}",
            shape.chunk >> 10, shape.row_group.bytes() >> 20, shape.pipes, shape.model_peak(&cost) >> 20,
            mem.map_or("none".into(), |m| format!("{}MiB", m >> 20)));
    }
    Ok(shape)
}
```

`APITAP_DEBUG` already exists (lease.rs:137), so no environment variable is added. The `run_many` doc (:592-593) becomes "`budget × PipeCost::pipe_bytes + reserve`".

**Bit-identity for STREAMING.** `pipe_bytes = 10c`, the rungs are `[Mib24]`, and `allowed` equals `mem_capped` before `max(1)`. Scoring `min(ask).max(1)` with strict replacement reproduces `fat`, then `thin` only if `thin > fat`. The `best ≤ 1` arm returns the fat chunk, which is 0.56.0's result whenever `fat == thin == 1`. Test T1 proves this over a grid.

**This changes the design as proposed.** The proposal compared raw zero scores. At 64 MiB that picks `(2 MiB, 1)` where 0.56.0 runs `(4 MiB, 1)`, a regression on a measured cell. Clamped scoring fixes it. The "fits the cage" guarantee therefore applies to `pipes ≥ 2`. One pipe is the documented floor, as in 0.56.0.

#### 3.3 The encoder: `wire/bqparquet.rs`

- **Constructors.** `ParquetEncoder::new(names, delivered, cursor, row_group: RowGroup)` and `new_ext(names, delivered, cursor, ids, key_col: Option<usize>, row_group: RowGroup)`. The new fields are `row_group: RowGroup`, `group_bytes: usize` and `pub(crate) key_file: Option<KeyFile>`.
- **The row-group bound is enforced per row.**
  - `ColBuf::push_pg` (:166) returns `Result<usize>`, the resident bytes it added, using the same unit costs as `bytes()` (:141-150): I64/Ts/Date/F64 8, F32 4, Bool 1, Dec 48, Bytes `48 + stored_len` (uuid stored_len = 36, jsonb = f.len()-1).
  - `try_tuple` adds that plus 2 per def pushed (NULL included) to `self.group_bytes`.
  - The check moves from after the loop (:482-484) into the tuple loop (:462-477): after each non-trailer tuple, `if self.group_bytes >= self.row_group.bytes() { self.flush_row_group()?; }`. `buf` is already a local there (:461), so the borrow is free.
  - `fn group_bytes()` (:590-593) becomes a field read. `ColBuf::bytes()` survives only as `#[cfg(test)]`, as the oracle for T4b.
  - `flush_row_group` resets `group_bytes = 0` next to the clears (:658-663).
  - Effect: builders are bounded by `rg + one row`, independent of chunk width and expansion. As a side benefit, the O(rows) re-sum per push disappears.
- **Deleted:**
  - `KeyCap` (:327-332), the fields `capture`/`keys` (:319-322), the capture arm of `new_ext` (:362-374, replaced by `merge_key_ok`), the per-row capture block (:551-584);
  - the NULL check at :529 becomes `self.key_file.as_ref().is_some_and(|k| k.col == i)`;
  - `parquet_field` (:242) becomes `pub(crate)`.
- **The companion writer:**

```rust
/// A one-column parquet file written IN LOCKSTEP with the data file. Every data row group is
/// followed by a key row group built from the same builder (`cols[col]`), before the builders
/// clear. Nothing is copied and nothing survives a flush.
pub(crate) struct KeyFile {
    pub(crate) col: usize,
    schema: Arc<Type>,               // group("schema"){ parquet_field(&names[col], &delivered[col], id) }, id = ids[col] or col+1: the data column's own fn
    writer: Option<SerializedFileWriter<SharedBuf>>,
    pub(crate) out: SharedBuf,
    rows: u64,                       // rows in the OPEN key file
}
```

- `open_writer` (:422-428) also opens `key_file.writer` on `key_file.out` with the shared `props`.
- `flush_row_group`: after `rg.close()` (:656), before the clears:

```rust
if let Some(k) = &mut self.key_file {
    let pe = |e| Error::Transfer(format!("parquet key file: {e}"));
    let mut rg = k.writer.as_mut().expect("key writer open").next_row_group().map_err(pe)?;
    let mut col = rg.next_column().map_err(pe)?.expect("one column");
    match &self.cols[k.col] {
        ColBuf::I64(v)   => { col.typed::<Int64Type>().write_batch(v, Some(&self.defs[k.col]), None).map_err(pe)?; }
        ColBuf::Bytes(v) => { col.typed::<ByteArrayType>().write_batch(v, Some(&self.defs[k.col]), None).map_err(pe)?; } // uuid already hyphenated (:208-218): byte-identical by construction
        _ => return Err(Error::Transfer("merge key builder is not I64/Bytes: gate bypassed".into())),
    }
    col.close().map_err(pe)?; rg.close().map_err(pe)?;
    k.rows += self.rows_buffered() as u64;
}
```

- `finish_file(&mut self) -> Result<Option<u64>>`: flush, then close the data writer. If there is a key file, close its writer, `let n = k.rows; k.rows = 0;` and return `Some(n)`. Then `open_writer()`. S3, GCS, BigQuery and dest_ice ignore the value.
- **Call sites:**
  - s3.rs:934 → `self.row_group`
  - gcs.rs:789 → `self.row_group`
  - bigquery.rs:1119 via new `BqLoader::open(.., row_group: RowGroup)` (:1104), caller :2057, and any test call
  - iceberg.rs:1082 → `new_ext(.., self.merge_key.map(|(i,_)| i), self.row_group)`
  - dest_ice.rs:164 → `RowGroup::cdc_window()`
  - dest_ice.rs:424 (test), bqparquet.rs:789 and :839 (tests) → `RowGroup::Mib24`

#### 3.4 Plumbing the shape: `pipeline/dispatch.rs`, `read_impl.rs`, the sinks

- **`SinkCfg`** (dispatch.rs:99-116): `budget: usize` (:108) becomes `shape: PipeShape` ("the planner's fitted shape; parquet sinks read `row_group`, pooled sinks `pipes`").
- **`DstScheme`** (:200-207): `connect(url, dest_table, cfg)` loses `parallel`, and `shared(url, cfg)` loses `budget`. Everything reads `cfg.shape.pipes`:
  - PgTo pool `cfg.shape.pipes + 1` (:219) and `+ 8` (:224);
  - GcsTo `cfg.shape`.
- **`one()`** (:331-360):

  ```rust
  let shape = super::knobs(opts, &profile, <D::Sink as crate::sink::Sink>::pipe_residency(opts.mode))?;
  let shape = PipeShape { pipes: shape.pipes.min(profile.table_pipe_cap).max(1), ..shape };
  ```

  (:347, kept after the fit exactly as today.) Then `SinkCfg { .., shape, .. }`, `S::connect(src_url, shape.pipes + 1)`, `D::connect(dst_url, dest_table, &cfg)`, and `super::run(.., shape.chunk, shape.pipes, exact(shape.pipes), ..)`.
- **`many()`** (:365-390): same `knobs`, `budget = shape.pipes`, `run_many(.., shape.chunk, shape.pipes, ..)` (:388). The semaphore stays in pipes. That is honest because every table of a run has one sink type and one mode, so one per-pipe price.
- **Sink signatures:**
  - `S3Sink::bind(conn, dest_table, shape: PipeShape, run)` (s3.rs:700) stores `row_group`.
  - `GcsSink::bind(conn, dest_table, shape, run)` (gcs.rs:533) uses `shape.pipes` at :541 and stores `row_group`.
  - `IcebergSink::bind(conn, dest_table, shape, run)` (iceberg.rs:513) stores `row_group`.
  - `BqSink::connect/bind(.., shape, run)` (bigquery.rs:1546-1563) uses `shape.pipes >= 4` at :1568 and stores `row_group`.
- **read_impl.rs:128-155:**

  ```rust
  let shape = pipeline::knobs(&topts, &profile, PipeResidency::STREAMING)?;
  let (chunk, mut parallel) = (shape.chunk, shape.pipes);
  if opts.parallel.is_none() {
      if let Some(m) = pipeline::mem_limit_bytes() {
          parallel = pipeline::fit(pipeline::READ_PIPE_CAP, Some(chunk), Some(m), &pipeline::PipeCost::READ).pipes;
      }
  }
  ```

  `fit` with `pipe_chunks = 0` gives `((m-16 MiB)/22 MiB).min(8).max(1)`, which is `auto_pipes` (:70-72) exactly. Delete `auto_pipes` and move its test (:436-454) into pipeline tests. The read lane's chunk is still chosen by the transfer model, as in 0.56.0 (so 2 MiB at 128 MiB). That is kept for bit-identity and listed in §8.
- **Cross-cluster.** This cluster owns the pipe count and `SinkCfg.shape`. Cluster C consumes `shape.pipes` as its loader count (`Pipes::new(loaders)`, `launch_loaders`) and adds its lineage `parent` next to `shape` in `SinkCfg`. *[§0 D1/R4 override: C consumes `PipeShape.pipes` via `Pipes::open`; `parent` is a dispatch parameter, not a `SinkCfg` field.]* Order: E guard → A + C lineage → B → **D** → C pipes. D's read_impl edit lands before C's `launch_loaders` edit.

#### 3.5 Iceberg merge: `sink/iceberg.rs` (+ `Multipart` in `sink/s3.rs`)

**`sink/s3.rs`:** one multipart state machine, used by S3Loader (once) and IcebergLoader (twice). It replaces the duplicated fields and methods at s3.rs:1015-1085 and iceberg.rs:1981-2064.

```rust
pub(crate) struct Multipart { pub(crate) key: String, upload_id: String, etags: Vec<(u32, String)>, part_no: u32, pub(crate) bytes: u64 }
impl Multipart {
    pub(crate) async fn open(conn: &S3Conn, key: String) -> Result<Self>             // create_multipart
    async fn put(&mut self, conn: &S3Conn, b: Vec<u8>) -> Result<()>                 // today's flush_part (s3.rs:1029-1041 / iceberg.rs:1995-2007), skips empty
    pub(crate) async fn drain(&mut self, conn: &S3Conn, out: &SharedBuf) -> Result<()> { if let Some(b) = out.take_ready() { self.put(conn, b).await?; } Ok(()) }
    pub(crate) async fn complete(mut self, conn: &S3Conn, tail: Vec<u8>) -> Result<Multipart> // put(tail) then complete_multipart; on Err abort_multipart_noted, return the ORIGINAL error (s3.rs:1072-1078)
    pub(crate) async fn abort(&self, conn: &S3Conn)                                 // abort_multipart_noted
}
```

S3Loader becomes `{ conn, mp: Multipart, pq, rows }`. Its 0-row rule (`mp.part_no == 0 && tail.is_empty()`, :1061) is unchanged.

**`sink/iceberg.rs`:**

```rust
struct DeleteDone { path: String, key: String, rows: u64, bytes: u64 }
struct FileDone { path: String, key: String, rows: u64, bytes: u64, wm: Option<String>, delete: Option<DeleteDone> } // `keys: KeyCap` (:74) deleted
pub(crate) struct IcebergLoader { s3: S3Conn, path: String, data: Multipart, del: Option<(Multipart, String /*path*/)>, pq: ParquetEncoder, rows: u64, done: Arc<Mutex<Vec<FileDone>>> }
```

- `use` at :48 drops `KeyCap`, and the comment at :1746 no longer names it.
- **`loader()`** (:1076-1101): data as today. When `self.merge_key.is_some()`, also open a delete multipart:
  - key `format!("{}data/{}-{n:05}-deletes.parquet", self.key_prefix, self.run_id)`
  - path `format!("{}/data/{}-{n:05}-deletes.parquet", self.location, self.run_id)`
- **`send`:**

  ```rust
  self.rows += self.pq.push(&buf)?;
  self.data.drain(&self.s3, &self.pq.out).await?;
  if let (Some((d, _)), Some(k)) = (&mut self.del, &self.pq.key_file) { d.drain(&self.s3, &k.out).await?; }
  ```

- **`finish`** (order matters; no completed object may be orphaned):
  1. `let key_rows = self.pq.finish_file()?;` then take `data_tail = pq.out.take_all()` and `key_tail = key_file.out.take_all()`.
  2. If `rows == 0`: abort both, `Ok(0)` (:2031-2034).
  3. If `del` is Some: `key_rows != Some(self.rows)` → abort both, `Err(Transfer("iceberg merge: delete file has {n} keys for {rows} rows"))`. This is §3.16's check (:1198-1203), moved per file. Then `d.complete(key_tail)`; on Err abort `data` and return Err.
  4. `self.data.complete(data_tail)`; on Err, `let _ = s3.delete(&del.key)` (it completed), then return Err.
  5. Push `FileDone { .., delete: Some(DeleteDone{..}) }`.
- **`abort`:** abort both.
- **`commit`** (:1163-1224): replace the `delete_file` block (:1186-1224) with a call to a pure function (testable, T6):

  ```rust
  fn delete_data_files(files: &[FileDone], mode: Mode, fid: Option<i32>) -> Result<Vec<DataFile>>
  // Merge: one DataFileBuilder…content(EqualityDeletes).file_path(d.path).file_format(Parquet)
  //        .partition(Struct::empty()).record_count(d.rows).file_size_in_bytes(d.bytes)
  //        .equality_ids(Some(vec![fid])) per f.delete; Err if any f.delete is None or d.rows != f.rows.
  // Not Merge: Err if any f.delete is Some; else empty.
  ```

  - Manifest (:1272-1291): `if !delete_files.is_empty()` → the same m1 deletes manifest via `write_manifest_avro(.., true, &delete_files, ..)`, which already loops (:1434-1437).
  - Summary (:1327-1333): `added-delete-files = delete_files.len()`, `added-equality-deletes = Σ record_count`.
  - Requirements and `commit_table` (:1387-1398) are unchanged. The delete DataFiles are immutable across the 3 retry attempts; m1 is rebuilt per attempt as today.
- **Sweeps, written once:**

  ```rust
  async fn sweep(s3: &S3Conn, files: &[FileDone]) { for f in files { let _ = s3.delete(&f.key).await; if let Some(d) = &f.delete { let _ = s3.delete(&d.key).await; } } }
  ```

  It is called from `discard` (:1125-1128), `finalize` rows==0 (:1136-1139) and commit failure (:1148-1150). This also closes a 0.56.0 leak: the single merge delete object PUT at :1210 was never swept on commit failure.
- **`write_delete_parquet`** (:1469-1539, CDC lane `cdc_commit_inner`): `merge_key_ok(d)?`, and the hand-spelled field (:1482-1493) becomes `crate::wire::bqparquet::parquet_field(name, d, field_id)?`. The delete-file schema is then spelled once for both lanes. dest_ice gates the pk to int/text/uuid oids (dest_ice.rs:122-131), which map to exactly those arms.

#### 3.6 API per engine

| engine | API change |
|---|---|
| S3 / Iceberg storage | merge only: one more CreateMultipartUpload / UploadPart × ceil(key_bytes / 8 MiB) / CompleteMultipartUpload per pipe |
| Iceberg REST | unchanged `commit_table(requirements, updates)` (:1395-1398); the m1 deletes manifest carries N entries instead of 1 |
| GCS / BigQuery | none; only the row-group size inside the files. BigQuery's lane rule (:1568) now sees the fitted pipe count |
| Postgres / ClickHouse / MySQL | none |

### 4. Invariants and the argument

**I1 — the model holds for every real parallel run.**
- *Claim:* for every unpinned run with a known limit and `pipes ≥ 2`, `pipes × pipe_bytes(chunk, row_group) + reserve ≤ mem`. When `pipes == 1`, `chunk` is the fat (or pinned) chunk and `row_group` is the largest rung one such pipe fits, else `Mib4`.
- *Walk:* a candidate's score s ≥ 2 means raw `allowed ≥ s`, so it fits. Only candidates with s ≥ 2 can replace the initial `(0, ..)`, and then only one with s ≥ 1. The `≤ 1` arm handles everything else.
- *Residual:* a parquet lane in a cage below 107 MiB (117 MiB for merge) runs one pipe above the model. 0.56.0 ran 3 pipes × 83 MiB there. §8.

**I2 — STREAMING is bit-identical to 0.56.0.** For every `(mem, ask, pinned)`, `fit(STREAMING)` equals 0.56.0 `knobs`. §3.2 gives the argument; T1 checks it exhaustively against a verbatim oracle.

The design text's claim "(256 MB → 5 × 4 MiB)" was wrong for `ask = 32`. Both 0.56.0 and `fit` give 10 × 2 MiB. Five is the fat-only `allowed`, which T1 now asserts through `PipeCost::allowed`. This is the attacker's T1 fix.

**I3 — the headline tier is unchanged except Iceberg merge.** At 0.5 CPU / 256 MiB every route plans exactly as 0.56.0, except Iceberg merge (row group 24 → 8 MiB).
- The four parquet sinks ask 2: Mib24 gives `216/107 = 2`, the first candidate, which nothing strictly beats.
- mysql→ch asks 8 and gets (2 MiB, 8) either way.
- Read is unchanged.
- **Fragility, stated:** the parquet 256 MiB / ask 2 cell sits at 254/256 of the model. Raising `PER_ROW_GROUP` to 3 costs that pipe. T3b pins the cell with that comment.

**I4 — the encoder never grows with the rows.**
- `group_bytes ≤ row_group.bytes() + one row`, and it is 0 right after `flush_row_group`.
- The encoder has no field whose length grows with total rows. `KeyCap` is gone. `KeyFile` holds only a writer and `out`, which drains at `SEND_THRESHOLD`.
- *Walk:* each tuple adds its resident bytes, then the check runs, then a flush clears.

**I5 — the key file mirrors the data file.** With a key companion:
- after `finish_file`, key rows = data rows;
- key row groups = data row groups (both are written inside the same `flush_row_group` call, which returns early only when `rows_buffered() == 0`, :601-603, for both);
- the key column's physical type, logical type and field id equal the data column's, because both come from `parquet_field`.

**I6 — merge commits are one-to-one.**
- `added-delete-files == added-data-files` and `added-equality-deletes == added-records`.
- Every delete file has `equality_ids == [fid]` and `record_count == its data file's rows`.
- A run with a data file lacking its delete file fails in `delete_data_files`, before `commit_table`.
- *Semantics are unchanged.* All files of one snapshot share its sequence number, and an equality delete applies only to data files with a strictly smaller data sequence number (Iceberg spec v2). N delete files therefore remove exactly the rows one file removed and exempt the same new data. Readers union the key sets.
- *Interleavings:*
  - A sibling pipe fails: every loader `abort`s both multiparts. Completed FileDones are swept, delete objects included, by `discard`.
  - Delete completes and data completion fails: the delete object is deleted in `finish` step 4.
  - Commit conflicts 3×: `sweep` removes data and delete objects.
  - A crash between completion and commit leaves unreferenced objects, as 0.56.0 did for data files (no snapshot references them).

**I7 — every sink declares.** `Sink::pipe_residency` has no default, so a new sink does not compile without a declaration. `parquet_residency` is the only parquet spelling, and `SEND_THRESHOLD` is one symbol. `RowGroup` has no integer constructor outside tests, so an encoder cannot be built with a size the planner did not choose.

**I8 — explicit parallel is never overridden.** STREAMING routes print nothing new.

**I9 — the user API is unchanged.** `transfer`/`transfer_many` signatures are unchanged and no knob or environment variable is added.

**Conservative corners, stated.** The pricing is a design point, so these over-price rather than risk OOM:
- The merge bootstrap run (effective mode Replace, `opts.mode = Merge`) is priced with the companion.
- The GCS/BigQuery CSV lanes are priced as parquet.
- BigQuery at 128 MiB / ≥2 cores now fits 2 pipes, so the `pipes >= 4` rule (:1568) picks the CSV lane where 0.56.0 picked parquet at 4 pipes (372 MiB modeled). A true CSV model (`10c + 8 MiB + 2c`) also gives 2 pipes at 128 MiB, so no pipe is lost.
- GCS `format=csv` at 256 MiB / ≥4 cores runs 5 pipes where a CSV-exact model would allow 6.

### 5. Per engine

| engine | mechanism | what is weaker there |
|---|---|---|
| Postgres sink | `STREAMING`; arithmetic bit-identical | none |
| ClickHouse sink | `STREAMING`; the ladder route | none |
| MySQL sink | `STREAMING` | declared from whole-run peaks, not a buffer census; pg→my raw-plane +200 MB (profiling.md Knife 1) deserves its own cell |
| S3 (parquet) | `parquet_residency(false)`; RowGroup from fit; `Multipart` shared | none; E1 gates it |
| GCS (parquet / csv) | same declaration, worst of both formats | CSV priced as parquet (−1 pipe at 256 MiB / ≥4 cores); **not gateable** (the service account is BigQuery-only), so WAIVED in E's matrix and covered by construction (shared `parquet_residency` + T2) |
| BigQuery (parquet / csv) | `parquet_residency(false)`; lane rule sees fitted pipes | CSV priced as parquet; the lane switches to CSV at 128 MiB / ≥2 cores; server-side load memory correctly not counted |
| Iceberg replace / append | `parquet_residency(false)` | none |
| Iceberg merge | `+SEND_THRESHOLD+PAGE_TRANSIENT` companion; streamed per-file delete files | 1 pipe at 128 MiB where replace runs 2; N delete files per commit (compaction is the catalog's job) |
| Iceberg CDC (dest_ice) | `RowGroup::cdc_window()`; schema via `parquet_field` | the window's delete set is still materialised (bounded by the drain budget) |
| Arrow `read()` | `PipeCost::READ` through the same `fit`; bit-identical | chunk still chosen by the transfer model (2 MiB at 128 MiB): a pre-existing oddity kept for identity |

### 6. Tests

Implement in the order of §3.0 → §3.5. Each RED is observed before its fix lands.

**Unit tests:**

- **T1 `pipeline::tests::mem_budget_matches_the_measured_ladder`** (rewrites :746-759; the lock).
  - Fat-only ladder through `PipeCost::transfer(STREAMING).allowed(4 MiB, Mib24, mem).max(1).min(32)` → 1/1/1/2/5 at 44/64/80/128/256 MiB. The 4 GiB cell with ask 5 → 5.
  - `fit(32, None, 128 MiB, STREAMING) == (2 MiB, Mib24, 4)` and `fit(32, None, 256 MiB, ..) == (2 MiB, Mib24, 10)`.
  - Oracle grid: a `#[cfg(test)] fn knobs_0_56(ask, pinned, mem)` holds :293-311 + :263-268 verbatim. For mem ∈ {16,44,64,80,100,128,200,256,512,1024,4096} MiB, ask ∈ {1,2,4,5,8,16,32} and pinned ∈ {None, 64 KiB, 1 MiB, 8 MiB}, assert `fit(..STREAMING)` chunk/pipes equal the oracle and `row_group == Mib24`.
  - *RED:* mutate `s > best.0` to `>=` → (256 MiB, ask 2) picks 2 MiB and the grid fails. Remove `.max(1)` → the 64 MiB cell picks the thin chunk and fails. `PIPE_CHUNKS = 8` → the 256 MiB ladder cell becomes 6 and fails.
- **T2 `parquet_pipes_fit_the_cage_they_are_planned_into`.** With `r = PipeCost::transfer(parquet_residency(false))`, for (mem, ask) ∈ [(128,4), (128,2), (256,8), (256,4), (256,2), (1024,8)]:
  - assert `model_peak ≤ mem`;
  - assert shapes exactly (2 MiB, Mib4, 2), (2, Mib4, 2), (2, Mib4, 5), (2, Mib8, 4), (4, Mib24, 2), (4, Mib24, 8).
  - *RED first:* write it with a local `plan()` helper that delegates to `knobs_0_56` (with `Mib24`). (256, 8) → (2 MiB, 8) → 8×83+40 = 704 > 256, RED. (128, 4) → 372 > 128, RED. Switch the helper to `fit`: GREEN.
- **T3 `iceberg_merge_keeps_two_pipes_at_the_headline_tier_by_shrinking_the_row_group`.**
  - `fit(2, None, 256 MiB, merge) == (4 MiB, Mib8, 2)`; non-merge `(4 MiB, Mib24, 2)`; merge at 128 MiB `(4 MiB, Mib8, 1)`.
  - *RED:* declare Iceberg with `parquet_residency(false)` for merge → Mib24, RED. Ladder `[Mib24]` only → 1 pipe, RED.
- **T3b `every_parquet_sink_keeps_two_pipes_at_the_headline_tier`.** For each of `S3Sink/GcsSink/BqSink/IcebergSink::pipe_residency(Mode::Replace)`, `fit(2, None, 256 MiB).pipes == 2`. The comment says: raising `PER_ROW_GROUP` to 3 costs this pipe; re-derive before changing it. *RED:* set `PER_ROW_GROUP = 3` → 216/131 = 1, RED.
- **T3c `explicit_parallel_is_never_overridden`.** `explicit(8, None, 256 MiB, parquet)` → pipes 8, Mib4. `explicit(4, ..)` → Mib8. `explicit(16, .., STREAMING)` → Mib24 (no note). *RED:* route explicit through `fit` → pipes 5, RED.
- **T4 `bqparquet::tests::merge_key_file_streams_per_row_group_and_retains_nothing`.**
  - Setup: Int key col 0, `RowGroup::Test(64 << 10)`, 20 000 rows in 1 KiB slices.
  - After every `push`, `group_bytes < 64 KiB + one row`.
  - `finish_file()? == Some(20_000)`.
  - The key bytes open in `SerializedFileReader` with `num_row_groups() == data's num_row_groups() > 1`, values 0..20000 in order, field id and physical type equal to data column 0.
  - A Uuid case asserts the key bytes equal the data column's 36-byte strings.

RED, because `len == rows`. Mutation control on the new tree: comment out the key write in `flush_row_group` → row-group count mismatch, RED.
- **T4b `running_group_bytes_equals_the_resident_sum`.** Random rows over every `Delivered`, NULLs included: after each push, `enc.group_bytes == Σ cols.bytes() + Σ defs.len()*2` (the `#[cfg(test)]` oracle). *RED:* the uuid arm returns `f.len()` → mismatch.
- **T4c `short_wide_rows_cannot_overshoot_the_row_group`.** 12 one-char text columns, `RowGroup::Test(256 << 10)`, one 4 MiB input chunk: max `group_bytes` seen is at most 256 KiB + one row. *RED* on the 0.56.0 check placement (after the loop, :482): about 40 MiB of builders from one chunk.

**e2e legs:**

- **E1 `benchmarks/e2e_parquet_capped.py`** — "parquet pipes fit the cage they are planned into". Requires `iceberg` (MinIO at 127.0.0.1:9100).
  - *Rig check:* the leg asserts `os.cpu_count() >= 4`, else exits 2 with "rig: needs ≥4 host cores". It switches to E's `cpu` capability once that lands.
  - *Seed* (kept, never dropped): `e2e_cap_src (id bigint PRIMARY KEY, v text, n bigint)` with 10M rows (`g, md5(g::text), g*7`) in apitap-bench-pg-src, created if absent.
  - *Container:* runs `transfer(SRC, 's3://apitap-bench/e2e_cap?format=parquet&endpoint=http://127.0.0.1:9100&access_key_id=bench&secret_access_key=benchbench', table='e2e_cap_src')` with no knobs inside `docker run --network host --memory=X --memory-swap=X --cpus=Y -e APITAP_DEBUG=1`. There is no `--rm`. The image is `python:{sys.version_info.major}.{minor}-slim`, with the invoking interpreter's site-packages mounted read-only as `PYTHONPATH`, so the leg tests whatever wheel the gate interpreter holds. The container's last command is `cat /sys/fs/cgroup/memory.peak`, a kernel fact.
  - *Cells:* (128m, 2), (256m, 4), (256m, 0.5).
  - *Asks DOCKER:* `docker inspect -f '{{.State.OOMKilled}} {{.State.ExitCode}}'` == `false 0`; then `docker rm`.
  - *Asks the KERNEL:* `peak ≤ 0.90 × cap`, the recalibration rule. A breach fails with "raise PER_ROW_GROUP (bqparquet.rs) and re-derive T2/T3".
  - *Asks MINIO through DuckDB httpfs* (the duck() settings of e2e_logbased_dests.py:75-85):
    - `SELECT count(*), sum(id), sum(n) FROM read_parquet('s3://apitap-bench/e2e_cap/e2e_cap_src/part-*.parquet')` equals the source's;
    - the listing under `e2e_cap/e2e_cap_src__apitap_staging/` is empty;
    - `e2e_cap/e2e_cap_src/` holds only `part-NNNNN.parquet`, and their count equals the planned pipes (2, 4, 2). One part per loader: a server fact for the pipe count.
  - *Records:* the `[plan]` line is recorded, not asserted.
  - *Cleanup:* the dest prefix is deleted at the end.
  - *RED:* the same cells with the 0.56.0 wheel. (128m, 2) plans 4 × 2 MiB × rg24 and (256m, 4) plans 8 × 2 MiB × rg24. At the measured ≈ 75-87 MiB/pipe that is 340-740 MiB, so the expected result is OOMKilled / 137. If one survives, the part count (4 or 8) fails it. The (256m, 0.5) cell must be GREEN on both wheels; it is the headline regression guard.
- **E2 `benchmarks/e2e_iceberg_merge_capped.py`** — "an Iceberg merge delta does not grow memory". Requires `iceberg`.
  - *Seed:* `ice_merge (id uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(), updated_at timestamptz DEFAULT now(), v text, n bigint)`, 2 000 000 rows.
  - *Run 1:* `mode="merge", cursor="updated_at"` into `iceberg://127.0.0.1:8181/e2e_cap?endpoint=…` (uncapped).
  - *Delta:* `UPDATE ice_merge SET n = n + 1, updated_at = now()` plus 100 000 inserts.
  - *Run 2:* in `--memory=256m --memory-swap=256m --cpus=0.5`.
  - *Asks DOCKER:* OOMKilled false, exit 0.
  - *Asks the KERNEL:* `peak ≤ 0.90 × cap`.
  - *Asks the CATALOG* (`GET /v1/namespaces/e2e_cap/tables/ice_merge`, the idiom at e2e_logbased_dests.py:56-61), current snapshot summary:
    - `added-records == "2100000"`;
    - `added-equality-deletes == "2100000"`;
    - `added-delete-files == added-data-files` and ≥ 2;
    - `operation == "overwrite"`.
  - *Asks DUCKDB:* `iceberg_scan` gives `count(*) == 2100000` (4 100 000 if deletes were not applied) and `sum(n)` equals the source.
  - *Cleanup:* `DELETE …?purgeRequested=true`. The seed table is kept.
  - *RED:* 0.56.0 wheel. `KeyCap` holds about 2.1M × 72 B ≈ 150 MB beside 2 × ~87 MiB of pipes, so OOM is expected. If it survives, `added-delete-files == "1"` against ≥ 2 data files fails the summary assertion.
  - This is also the first gate leg for bulk Iceberg merge at all.
- **gate.py LEGS** (:44-90), two rows:
  - `("e2e_parquet_capped.py", "parquet pipes fit the cage they are planned into", {}, "iceberg", [])`
  - `("e2e_iceberg_merge_capped.py", "an Iceberg merge delta does not grow memory", {}, "iceberg", [])`
- **E's CLAIMS matrix.** Cluster E owns the format. This cluster supplies:
  - `memory.parquet-fits-cage` → e2e_parquet_capped.py;
  - `memory.iceberg-merge-flat` → e2e_iceberg_merge_capped.py;
  - `memory.gcs-parquet` WAIVED ("service account is BigQuery-only; same `parquet_residency` + T2/T3b").
