- **profiling.md "Session 5 (2026-09): the sink declares its residency".** Record the three E1 cells and the E2 cell: planned `(chunk, row_group, pipes)`, `memory.peak`, peak/cap, wall. Add the 0.56.0 wheel's result in the same cells as the before column. Add the rule: any cell above 90 % of its cap → raise the constant, re-derive T2/T3/T3b, re-run.

### 7. Docs

Cluster D alone owns the memory sentences. Cluster E must not write the usage.md:172-180 or README:105-106 memory text.

- **docs/usage.md:173-181.** Replace from "Each pipe budgets ~10 × `chunk_bytes`…" through "…TCP backpressure." with:
  > Each pipe is priced at 10 × `chunk_bytes` (fitted to measured whole-container peaks, benchmarks/profiling.md) plus what the destination's loader declares it holds per pipe. For Postgres, ClickHouse and MySQL that is nothing more. For the parquet lanes (S3, GCS, BigQuery, Iceberg) it is two row groups, the 8 MiB part buffer and a 1 MiB frame buffer, plus one more part buffer for an Iceberg merge. A 40 MiB reserve comes on top. In a small container the parquet row group shrinks (24 → 8 → 4 MiB) before a pipe is given up, and the engine thins the chunk to 2 MiB only when that buys a pipe under the full price. Memory is `parallel × (chunk-scale buffers + destination residency)`: never the table size, never the size of a merge delta. Bytes stream with TCP backpressure.

  Follow it with the 128 MiB / 256 MiB / 1 GiB rows of this section's arithmetic table, plus E's claim marker `memory.parquet-fits-cage`. The sentence "Memory use scales with `parallel × chunk_bytes`" becomes false and is deleted.
- **docs/usage.md:749-751** (merge bullet). The last sentence becomes:
  > the delta's merge-key values land as one equality-delete file per data file, streamed row group by row group and committed in the same snapshot; memory does not grow with the delta.

  Add marker `memory.iceberg-merge-flat`.
- **README.md:105-106.** Becomes:
  > **Bounded memory.** Bytes stream with TCP backpressure; memory is `parallel ×` (chunk buffers + what the destination holds per pipe), never the table size. A 256 MB container moves 10M+ rows.
- **crates/apitap-core/src/lib.rs:100-101** (`parallel` doc):
  > `None` = auto (route-specific CPU heuristic, capped by the container's memory, counting what the destination holds per pipe).
- **docs/failure-modes.md "Rules of thumb" (:437-450),** new item 4:
  > **In a memory-capped container, leave `parallel` unset.** Auto never plans more pipes than the memory model fits. An explicit `parallel` runs exactly as asked; on a parquet destination it gets the smallest row group (4 MiB) and a note saying the model fits fewer.
- **pipeline/mod.rs:592-593** doc: `budget × pipe_bytes + reserve`.
- **CHANGELOG / 0.57.0 notes:**
  > §3.15 and §3.16 closed. At 0.5 CPU / 256 MiB every route plans exactly as in 0.56.0, except Iceberg `mode="merge"` (row group 24 → 8 MiB). Capped multi-core parquet runs plan fewer pipes (for example 256 MiB / 4 cores: 8 → 5). BigQuery at 128 MiB on ≥ 2 cores now takes the CSV lane with 2 pipes. Iceberg merge commits N equality-delete files, one per data file.

### 8. What this does not fix

- `PER_ROW_GROUP = 2`, `LOADER_CHUNKS = 2` and `PAGE_TRANSIENT` are design points. The showdown peaks (85-88 MiB/pipe against the 107 modeled) support them at 4 MiB / Mib24, but no capped cell has confirmed them yet; the E1/E2 peaks decide. The 128 MiB and 256 MiB/4-core cells sit at 98-99 % of the model.
- A parquet lane in a cage below 107 MiB (117 MiB for merge) runs one pipe above the model. That tier was never claimed, and it beats 0.56.0's 3-pipe plan there.
- GCS and BigQuery CSV lanes are priced as parquet: −1 pipe for `gcs format=csv` at 256 MiB / ≥4 cores. BigQuery's CSV/parquet choice still keys on pipe count (:1568), not on CPU.
- For single-stream routes (`table_pipe_cap = 1`) into a parquet sink, the row group is fitted for the uncapped ask and may be smaller than necessary. That is safe, not optimal, and was kept for STREAMING bit-identity of the chunk.
- The read lane's chunk is still chosen by the transfer model.
- The Iceberg CDC lane still materialises each window's delete set (`write_delete_parquet`, iceberg.rs:1815). It is bounded by the drain budget; only its schema is now shared.
- The MySQL sink is declared STREAMING from whole-run peaks; the pg→my raw-plane +200 MB observation needs its own cell.
- A single row wider than the row group is still unbounded (failure-modes.md "Wide values").
- An explicit `parallel` above the model can still OOM; that is the caller's documented choice.
- Iceberg readers open N delete files per merge; compaction is the catalog's job.
- GCS cannot be gated on this rig (the service account is BigQuery-only), so its lane is WAIVED in the matrix.

---

## §2.E Upgrade compatibility, honest docs, and a gate that proves it

### 1. Root cause

In 0.56.0 three kinds of claim were never declared, and so nothing could check them:

- **Claims about other versions.** Every guard decision was written against the current apitap only.
  - What 0.55.1 scans for is the `Artifact::Staging` scan at `acd9cd6 sink/postgres.rs:96-122`. v0.55.1 `naming.rs` has no `Artifact::Lock`.
  - What 0.55.1 hides from discovery is `v0.55.1 naming.rs:633-634` (`name == STATE_TABLE || suffix`). Neither fact appears anywhere in the tree.
  - Consequences: a 0.56.0 drain announces with a lock that 0.55.1 cannot see (§3.5). A rollback replicates `_apitap_lease` and `_apitap_cdc_pending` (§6 low). The change in what the changelog stamp means had no procedure (§3.6).
- **Claims about engines.** The announce/scan/collect loop is open-coded eleven times:
  - `sink/postgres.rs:52-106`, `sink/mysql.rs:543-683`, `sink/clickhouse.rs:541-716`, `sink/bigquery.rs:1645-1755`, `sink/s3.rs:769-888`, `sink/gcs.rs:608-713`, `sink/iceberg.rs:631-716`
  - `logbased/dest_pg.rs:128-144,194-200`, `dest_my.rs:186-273`, `dest_ch.rs:214-300`, `dest_bq.rs:263-325`
  - The per-engine rules drifted:
    - The `_N` worker-index strip exists in the bulk BigQuery lane (`bigquery.rs:1690-1693`) and not in its CDC twin (`dest_bq.rs:288-296`).
    - What "mine" deletes differs by sink: pg ignores it (`naming.rs:470`); my, ch and bq drop it (`mysql.rs:613`, `clickhouse.rs:654`, `bigquery.rs:1710`).
    - The per-engine extra kinds (`Old` on MySQL at `mysql.rs:561`, `New` on ClickHouse at `clickhouse.rs:547`) are invisible to the CDC lane.
- **Claims about the gate.** The gate is a flat list of 40 tuples (`benchmarks/gate.py:44-91`) with no link to the sentences they prove. So `failure-modes.md:339-340` could promise a BigQuery re-check with no leg asking BigQuery anything, and four legs (§6) could pass while asserting nothing.

It is one defect: nothing forced anyone to write down a claim about another version, another engine or another leg, so nothing checked it.

### 2. The decision

1. Compatibility becomes a compiled table: `naming::compat::Generation`. The set of markers a run announces is derived from it. A 0.57.0 drain writes its `__apitap_lock` and also an empty tokenized `__apitap_staging` marker, so every 0.55.1+ reader refuses beside it. Unit tests fail the build if any supported reader cannot see a current run.
2. One `guard.rs` loop replaces the eleven. It runs over a per-engine `GuardStore` adapter that only spells catalog calls. It carries:
   - a `#[must_use]` `Announced` value;
   - a `Released` proof that is the only argument a lease close accepts;
   - one claim per token, with the refusal made before any lease is read;
   - a raw-versus-canonical listing, so BigQuery's `_N` tables are both classified and deleted correctly.
3. The gate declares coverage:
   - Each `Leg` names the claims it proves.
   - `benchmarks/_claims.py` lists every published claim × engine, and every empty cell carries a waiver sentence that must appear in the docs.
   - `gate.py --matrix` exits 3 on a gap.
   - The gate can run a second wheel and pause a drain.
   - **Cost:** +1 CREATE and +1 DROP per drained table per run. About 650 duplicated Rust lines are deleted (net about −250). About 1,400 lines of new legs. About 6 minutes more gate time and two prepared venvs on the VPS.

### 3. What changes

#### 3.0 Rules other clusters must follow (decided here)

- **R1 — one collector loop.** `guard::{announce, check_peers, release}` is the only announce, scan and collect code.
  - Cluster A strikes `lease::collect_or_refuse`, `Fence::announce`, `Fence::list_peers` and `Fence::drop_lock`, and B strikes F1 (a collector calling `lease_close`) — the other clusters' sections still carry them.
  - A's `Tenure::acquire` and `Tenure::release` hold `Vec<guard::Announced>` and call this module in the order of §3.5.
  - A's `Claim` enum is `guard::Claim`. A's tokenized-scratch sweep becomes the `GuardStore::sweep_run` hook (§3.2).
- **R2 — collectors never delete a victim's lease row.** After a claim the row stays `collected = true` on every engine: pg, my and bq by the claim UPDATE, ch by the biased claim row (A).
  - Only the owner deletes its own row, via `lease_close(Released)`.
  - Reason: "no row" is ambiguous. It is not an owner in pg's `fence_tx` "missing row" branch (`dest_pg.rs:82-95`, §3.10) or in ClickHouse's empty `argMax` (attack trace).
  - Cost: one inert row per collected run, which is the status quo (§6 low).
- **R3 — ClickHouse lease TTL (owned by A; this cluster's docs depend on it).** `sink/clickhouse.rs:65` becomes `TTL toDateTime(expires_at) + INTERVAL 7 DAY DELETE WHERE collected = 1`. Ownership predicates require `count() > 0`.
  - Without both, "a killed drain's lock clears itself" is false on ClickHouse after about a day. The claim cell (`lease.killed-drain-self-heals`, ch) is proven by leg N3 case "stale".
- **R4 — no data under a Cdc token.** Every Cdc-kind token is minted only by `RunId::mint_drain` (run.rs). Every object named with a GUARDED suffix under a Cdc token is created only by `guard::announce`, empty.
  - Cluster C's bootstrap child mints its own bulk-kind token via `mint_within`/`owns`. Cluster B's parent-token reuse must not ship. *[§0 R2 overrides: there is no `owns()`; the parent's artifacts classify `Found::Parent`, and every delete uses `is_self`.]*
  - This is the premise that makes a Cdc-token `Staging` collectable (§3.1.4).
- **R5 — delete only your own.** Any delete or sweep of "mine" uses `RunId::is_self` (exact token). `owns` (C: self or parent) is used only to decide what blocks. *[§0 R2 overrides: there is no `owns()`; the parent's artifacts classify `Found::Parent`, and every delete uses `is_self`.]*
- **R6 — one leg set.** The eviction and lease legs are named `e2e_cdc_evict_ch.py`, `e2e_cdc_lease_bq.py`, `e2e_cdc_fence_my.py` and `e2e_bq_multi_drain.py`. Legs A, B, C or D would have given the same purpose (`e2e_cdc_lease_paused.py`, `_sticky.py`, `e2e_bq_lease.py`) use these files.
- **R7 — the memory text belongs to D.** `usage.md:172-180` and `README.md:105-106` memory text are cluster D's, with marker `memory.parquet-fits-cage`. This cluster does not edit them.
- **Implementation order across clusters:** E guard (this section, steps 1-2 of §3.8) → A plus C lineage → B → D → C pipes → E gate legs and docs.

#### 3.1 `crates/apitap-core/src/naming.rs`

**3.1.1 `RunId`** (`:217-316`)

- `RunId::mint(kind, src)` (`:272`) is renamed `fn mint_token(kind, src)` (private) and gains two public constructors:
  - `pub(crate) fn mint_bulk(kind: BulkKind, src: &str) -> RunId`, where `pub(crate) enum BulkKind { Swap, Incremental }` maps onto `LandKind`.
  - `pub(crate) fn mint_drain(src: &str) -> RunId`, which always mints Cdc.
- `pipeline/dispatch.rs:139-146` `land_kind` returns `Result<BulkKind>`. The `Mode::LogBased` arm becomes `Err(Error::InvalidInput("log_based reached the bulk dispatcher"))`. That arm is unreachable today (`lib.rs:361`, `:414` route `LogBased` away), and making it an error makes R4 a type fact.
- `run.rs:905` and `:1138` use `mint_drain`.
- Add:

  ```rust
  pub(crate) fn kind(&self) -> LandKind { LandKind::from_letter(self.token.as_bytes()[8] as char).expect("minted") }
  pub(crate) fn is_self(&self, token: &str) -> bool { self.token == token }
  ```

**3.1.2 `mod compat`** (new, after `OWN_TABLES` at `:947-948`)

```rust
pub(crate) mod compat {
    use super::{Artifact::{self, *}, LandKind::{self, *}, GUARDED, OWN_TABLES, STATE_TABLE, CDC_PENDING_TABLE};
    /// What a SHIPPED apitap writes beside a table and what it scans for. Cells are facts about
    /// released wheels and are never edited; a release that changes either adds a row.
    #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
    pub(crate) enum Generation { G0, G1, G2, G3 }
    impl Generation {
        pub(crate) const CURRENT: Generation = Generation::G3;
        /// G0 (<0.55.0) writes the untokenized name → Found::Legacy → refused, never coexisted with.
        /// 0.55.0 is not a neighbour: the stability page says upgrade to 0.55.1 first.
        pub(crate) const SUPPORTED: &'static [Generation] = &[Generation::G1, Generation::G2, Generation::G3];
        pub(crate) const fn versions(self) -> &'static str {
            match self { Self::G0 => "<0.55.0", Self::G1 => "0.55.1", Self::G2 => "0.56.0", Self::G3 => "0.57.0+" }
        }
        /// On disk while a run of `kind` must be visible to a peer, from the end of `prepare` on,
        /// on the SQL catalogs and BigQuery (object stores: see "does not fix").
        pub(crate) const fn announces(self, kind: LandKind) -> &'static [Artifact] {
            match (self, kind) {
                (Self::G0, _) => &[],
                (Self::G1, Cdc) => &[],                 // the hole: a 0.55.1 drain writes nothing
                (Self::G1, _) => &[Staging],
                (Self::G2, Cdc) => &[Lock],
                (Self::G2, _) | (Self::G3, _) => &[Lock, Staging],   // G3 Cdc: Lock + empty Staging MARKER
            }
        }
        pub(crate) const fn scans(self) -> &'static [Artifact] {
            match self { Self::G0 => &[], Self::G1 => &[Staging], Self::G2 | Self::G3 => GUARDED }
        }
        pub(crate) const fn excludes(self) -> &'static [&'static str] {
            match self { Self::G0 | Self::G1 => &[STATE_TABLE], Self::G2 | Self::G3 => OWN_TABLES }
        }
    }
    pub(crate) const KNOWN_HOLES: &[(Generation, LandKind)] = &[(Generation::G1, Cdc)];
    pub(crate) const ROLLBACK_LEAKS: &[(Generation, &[&str])] =
        &[(Generation::G1, &[CDC_PENDING_TABLE, crate::lease::LEASE_TABLE])];
    /// The ONLY place a marker set is chosen. `guard::announce` creates exactly these, empty.
    /// Bulk Staging is not a marker: the sink's own `prepare` creates it with data.
    pub(crate) const fn markers(kind: LandKind) -> &'static [Artifact] {
        match kind { Cdc => &[Lock, Staging], _ => &[Lock] }
    }
}
```

**3.1.3 `blockers`** (`:448-486`) returns a struct and takes the kinds and a raw/canonical listing

```rust
pub(crate) struct Scan { pub(crate) blockers: Vec<Blocker>, pub(crate) mine_leftovers: Vec<String> /* raw names */ }
pub(crate) fn blockers<'a>(bare: &str, limit: usize, run: &RunId, kinds: &[Artifact],
                           listed: impl IntoIterator<Item = (&'a str /*canonical*/, &'a str /*raw*/)>) -> Scan
```

- Classification is `classify(canonical, bare, a, limit, run, now)` for each `a` in `kinds` (today `GUARDED`, `:455`).
- `Blocker::{Legacy, Live}.name` holds the **raw** name, which is what gets refused, dropped and printed.
- `Found::Mine` pushes `raw` to `mine_leftovers` only when:
  - `a != Artifact::Lock`, and
  - `parse_peer(canonical, a).is_some_and(|p| run.is_self(&p.token))` (R5).
- `guard_verdict` (`:402-413`) is deleted; its only callers are tests (`:1160-1193`), which move to `blockers(..).blockers.first()`.

**3.1.4 `collectable`** (`:498-503` replaced)

```rust
/// Only what a DRAIN announces is collectable: under R4 every Cdc-token Lock/Staging is an empty marker
/// made by guard::announce. A bulk run's staging holds data, a bulk lock has no lease, Legacy has no token.
pub(crate) fn collectable(b: &Blocker) -> Option<&str> {
    match b {
        Blocker::Live { artifact, peer, .. }
            if peer.kind == LandKind::Cdc && compat::markers(LandKind::Cdc).contains(artifact) => Some(&peer.token),
        _ => None,
    }
}
```

Observable change from 0.56.0 for a bulk lock: none. Today it returns a token, `lease_get` finds no row, and the message is `blocker_error(.., None)`. Now `None` arrives one step earlier, with the same text.

#### 3.2 `crates/apitap-core/src/guard.rs` (new; `lib.rs` gets `mod guard;`)

```rust
use crate::naming::{self, Artifact, RunId};
use crate::error::{Error, Result};

pub(crate) struct Listed { pub(crate) canonical: String, pub(crate) raw: String }
pub(crate) enum Claim { Taken, Refused, Absent }          // Absent = no row; A's enum, homed here
pub(crate) enum Mine { Keep, DeleteLeftovers }

#[async_trait::async_trait]   // main dep already (Cargo.toml:80); needed for &dyn GuardStore
pub(crate) trait GuardStore: Send + Sync {
    fn limit(&self) -> usize;                                    // PG_IDENT_MAX / MY_IDENT_MAX / ROOMY
    fn dest_label(&self, bare: &str) -> String;                  // lease key AND refusal name — must equal Dest::lease_key
    fn scan_kinds(&self) -> &'static [Artifact] { naming::GUARDED }
    fn spelling(&self, a: Artifact) -> Artifact { a }            // Iceberg: Lock → Staging (its claim vocabulary since 0.55.x)
    async fn list(&self, bare: &str, kinds: &[Artifact]) -> Result<Vec<Listed>>;
    async fn create_marker(&self, raw: &str) -> Result<()>;      // idempotent CREATE of an empty object
    async fn drop_object(&self, raw: &str) -> Result<()>;        // Ok only when observed gone (IF EXISTS / 404 = Ok)
    async fn lease_get(&self, key: &str, token: &str) -> Result<Option<crate::lease::Lease>>;
    async fn lease_claim(&self, key: &str, token: &str) -> Result<Claim>;
    async fn lease_close(&self, proof: Released);                // OWNER's own row only (R2)
    async fn sweep_run(&self, _bare: &str, _token: &str) -> Result<()> { Ok(()) }  // A fills: victim's tokenized scratch
}

#[must_use = "an announcement that is not released wedges the table for the next run"]
pub(crate) struct Announced { key: String, token: String, names: Vec<String>, done: bool }
impl Announced {
    /// The caller acknowledges it could not release; prints what is left standing.
    pub(crate) fn abandon(mut self) { self.done = true;
        eprintln!("apitap: {}: could not drop {} — the next run will name it", self.key, self.names.join(", ")); }
}
impl Drop for Announced {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        if !self.done && !std::thread::panicking() {
            eprintln!("apitap: internal: announcement {:?} on {} dropped unreleased", self.names, self.key);
            debug_assert!(false, "Announced dropped without release/abandon");
        }
    }
}
/// Every marker of one announcement observed gone. Constructible only in this module.
pub(crate) struct Released { pub(crate) key: String, pub(crate) token: String, _priv: () }

pub(crate) async fn announce(s: &dyn GuardStore, bare: &str, run: &RunId) -> Result<Announced> {
    let mut names = Vec::new();
    for &a in naming::compat::markers(run.kind()) {
        let n = naming::artifact_ident_run(bare, s.spelling(a), s.limit(), run);
        if let Err(e) = s.create_marker(&n).await {
            for d in &names { let _ = s.drop_object(d).await; }      // a half-announcement is worse than none
            return Err(e);
        }
        names.push(n);
    }
    Ok(Announced { key: s.dest_label(bare), token: run.token().into(), names, done: false })
}

pub(crate) async fn check_peers(s: &dyn GuardStore, bare: &str, run: &RunId, mine: Mine) -> Result<()> {
    let kinds = s.scan_kinds();
    let listed = s.list(bare, kinds).await?;
    let scan = naming::blockers(bare, s.limit(), run, kinds,
                                listed.iter().map(|l| (l.canonical.as_str(), l.raw.as_str())));
    let dest = s.dest_label(bare);
    let now = naming::now_unix();
    // 1. Early refusal: an uncollectable blocker refuses before ANY lease row is read or claimed.
    if let Some(b) = scan.blockers.iter().find(|b| naming::collectable(b).is_none()) {
        return Err(naming::blocker_error(&dest, b, now, None));
    }
    // 2. One decision per TOKEN (a dead 0.57 drain has two markers; one claim covers both).
    let mut by_tok: std::collections::BTreeMap<String, Vec<&naming::Blocker>> = Default::default();
    for b in &scan.blockers { by_tok.entry(naming::collectable(b).unwrap().to_string()).or_default().push(b); }
    for (tok, bs) in by_tok {
        let lease = s.lease_get(&dest, &tok).await?;
        let Some(l) = lease.as_ref().filter(|l| l.lapsed()) else {
            return Err(naming::blocker_error(&dest, bs[0], now, lease.as_ref()));
        };
        match s.lease_claim(&dest, &tok).await? {
            Claim::Taken => {}
            Claim::Refused => return Err(naming::blocker_error(&dest, bs[0], now, Some(l))),
            Claim::Absent  => return Err(naming::blocker_error(&dest, bs[0], now, None)),
        }
        // Scratch before markers: if the sweep fails, the markers still stand, the lease stays
        // collected (claim predicate is `lapsed OR collected`), and the next run retries the whole step.
        s.sweep_run(bare, &tok).await?;
        let failed: Vec<&str> = { let mut f = vec![];
            for b in &bs { if s.drop_object(b.name()).await.is_err() { f.push(b.name()); } } f };
        if !failed.is_empty() {
            return Err(Error::Transfer(format!("{dest}: took the lapsed claim of run {tok} but could not drop {}; \
                 the claim stays taken and the next run finishes the collection", failed.join(", "))));
        }
        for b in &bs { eprintln!("apitap: {dest}: collected {} — the run that wrote it stopped renewing its \
                                  claim on this destination's own clock. Resuming.", b.name()); }
        // No lease_close here (R2).
    }
    if let Mine::DeleteLeftovers = mine { for raw in &scan.mine_leftovers { s.drop_object(raw).await?; } }
    Ok(())
}

#[must_use]
pub(crate) async fn release(s: &dyn GuardStore, mut a: Announced) -> std::result::Result<Released, Announced> {
    let mut all = true;
    for n in &a.names { if s.drop_object(n).await.is_err() { all = false; } }
    if !all { return Err(a); }
    a.done = true;
    Ok(Released { key: a.key.clone(), token: a.token.clone(), _priv: () })
}
```

`blocker_error` (`:507-517`) keeps its signature. `Blocker::name()` is now raw, which is also what the S3 remedy text needs (§3.3).

#### 3.3 Adapters (spelling only; no `classify`, `blockers`, `collectable` or lease decision inside)

| adapter (file) | `list` | `create_marker` / `drop_object` | `scan_kinds`, `spelling`, canonical | lease fns |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PgGuard { pool, schema }` (sink/postgres.rs) | per kind: `SELECT c.relname FROM pg_class c JOIN pg_namespace n ON n.oid=c.relnamespace WHERE n.nspname=$1 AND c.relkind='r' AND c.relname LIKE $2`, with `$2 = esc(head)%esc(suffix)` (today `:60-73`); canonical = raw | `CREATE UNLOGGED TABLE IF NOT EXISTS {schema.q} ()` (`:34-40`) / `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS {schema.q}` (`:45-50`, now returning `Result`) | GUARDED. `schema` is resolved by cluster A's single `resolve_parts` (to_regclass namespace, else `current_schema()`, the rule at `:880-898`). Markers are always schema-qualified. | `:171-310` unchanged. `lease_claim` maps `Ok(true)`→Taken, `Ok(false)`→Refused, `no_lease_table`→Absent. `lease_close` = `:301` |
| `MyGuard { pool, db, extra: bool }` (sink/mysql.rs) | per kind: `SELECT table_name FROM information_schema.tables WHERE table_schema=? AND table_type='BASE TABLE' AND table_name LIKE ? ESCAPE '|'` (`dest_my.rs:214-226`) | `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS {fq} (t TINYINT) ENGINE=MEMORY` (`dest_my.rs:199-204`) / `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS {fq}` | `&[Lock, Staging, Old]` (today's bulk set, `mysql.rs:561`) for **both** lanes | `sink/mysql.rs:177-235`. The claim decision itself is the MySQL cluster's §3.9 fix. |
| `ChGuard { ch, on_cluster: Option<String> }` (sink/clickhouse.rs) | per kind: `SELECT name FROM system.tables WHERE database = currentDatabase() AND name LIKE '{ch_str(pat)}' FORMAT TabSeparatedRaw` (`:599-613`) | `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS {id}{oc} (t UInt8) ENGINE = Memory` (`:559-571`) / `drop_artifact` (`:700-716`: `… {oc} SYNC` when clustered). The CDC lane passes `on_cluster: None` (`refuse_clustered`). | `&[Lock, Staging, New]` (`:543-548`) for both lanes | `sink/clickhouse.rs:59-160` (TTL per R3) |
| `BqGuard { conn }` (sink/bigquery.rs) | one `tables_with_prefix(head)` (`:311`) per distinct head over the kinds; each raw → `Listed{ canonical: staging_base(raw, suffix)?, raw }` for the one suffix it matches (`staging_base` `:1942-1952` moves here) | `table_create(raw, [{"name":"t","type":"INT64"}])`, with an Err containing `409`/`Already Exists` → Ok / `table_delete` (`:284-288`; 404 = Ok) | GUARDED | `dest_bq.rs:93-134` (`lease_get`, `lease_claim`) move here as free fns shared by both lanes; `lease_close` = `dest_bq.rs:255-262` |
| `S3Guard` / `GcsGuard` (sink/s3.rs, sink/gcs.rs) | one `conn.list(staging_root)`; each key → today's `classify(root,key)` (`s3.rs:667-700`, `gcs.rs:500`), translated:<br>• `Run{seg}` → canonical `"{head}{seg}__apitap_staging"`, raw `"the objects and multipart uploads under s3://{bucket}/{prefix}{seg}/ (aws s3 rm --recursive, aws s3api abort-multipart-upload)"`<br>• lock entry → canonical `"{head}{tok}__apitap_lock"`, raw = that key<br>• `Legacy` → canonical `artifact_ident(bare, Staging, ROOMY)`, raw `"the objects under s3://…"`<br>Deduplicate by canonical. | `put_object(lock_key, [])` / `delete(key)` | GUARDED; `dest_label` = `s3://{bucket}/{prefix}{bare}` | `lease_get → Ok(None)`, `lease_claim → Ok(Absent)`, `lease_close` no-op |
| `IceGuard { s3, prefix }` (sink/iceberg.rs) | `s3.list(claim_prefix)` (`:665`); canonical = last path segment, raw = key | put the claim body (`:653-662`) / `delete` (`release_claim` `:717-721`) | `spelling(Lock) = Staging`, so the claim name stays what 0.55.1 and 0.56.0 Iceberg runs scan for | stubs as S3 |
| `NoGuard` (logbased/run.rs, for `Dest::Ice`) | `Ok(vec![])` | `Ok(())` / `Ok(())` | — | stubs; Iceberg drains stay exactly as unguarded as `run.rs:144-151` states |

The S3/GCS/Iceberg sinks pass `Mine::Keep`, as today (`s3.rs:824-826`, `iceberg.rs:692`). Every SQL and BigQuery bulk sink passes `Mine::DeleteLeftovers`, restricted to `is_self` (R5). For pg that is new, and it is unobservable, because a token is minted per dispatch and pg never re-prepares a table inside one.

#### 3.4 Sinks

Each of `PgSink`, `MySink`, `ChSink`, `BqSink`, `S3Sink`, `GcsSink`, `IcebergSink` gets `announced: std::sync::Mutex<Option<guard::Announced>>` and `fn guard(&self) -> &dyn GuardStore`. `prepare` does:

```rust
*self.announced.lock().unwrap() = Some(guard::announce(self.guard(), &self.bare, &self.run).await?);
guard::check_peers(self.guard(), &self.bare, &self.run, Mine::DeleteLeftovers /* Keep: s3/gcs/ice */).await?;
```

- This replaces `postgres.rs:841-842`, `mysql.rs:916-917`, `clickhouse.rs:1307-1308`, `bigquery.rs:2015-2016`, `s3.rs:919-920`, `gcs.rs:758-759`, and Iceberg's `claim_and_check_peers` call.
- `release_lock` (`sink/mod.rs:229-231`, signature unchanged) and every internal `self.release()`:

  ```rust
  let a = self.announced.lock().unwrap().take();   // guard dropped before the await
  if let Some(a) = a { if let Err(a) = guard::release(self.guard(), a).await { a.abandon(); } }
  ```

- pg/my/ch still release at the end of `prepare`, after staging exists (`postgres.rs:859`, `mysql.rs:934`, `clickhouse.rs:1358`). bq, s3, gcs and ice release in `finalize`/`discard`, as today.
- `pipeline/mod.rs:440-446` is unchanged.

#### 3.5 `logbased/run.rs` and the `dest_*` modules

**`Dest`:**
- Add `fn guard(&self) -> &dyn GuardStore`. `PgDest`/`MyDest`/`ChDest`/`BqDest` build their guard from their pool/conn in `connect`; `Ice` returns `&NoGuard`.
- Delete `announce` (`:151-159`), `release_ok` (`:227-235`), `check_peers` (`:237-246`) and `release` (`:248-257`).
- `Dest::lease_close(&self, key, run)` (`:201-209`) is deleted. Call sites use `self.guard().lease_close(proof)`, so **a lease close without a `Released` does not type-check**.

**CDC group** (`run.rs:909-938` and the MySQL twin `:1142-1170`), in order:

1. `dest.set_run(&run)`.
2. `dest.lease_open(&lease_keys, &run)?` — lease first, unchanged.
3. Announce every member:

   ```rust
   let g = dest.guard(); let mut held: Vec<Announced> = vec![];
   for c in &ctxs { match guard::announce(g, bare(&c.dest_table), &run).await {
       Ok(a) => held.push(a), Err(e) => { give_back(g, held).await; return Err(e) } } }
   ```

4. Check every member: `for c in &ctxs { if let Err(e) = guard::check_peers(g, bare(&c.dest_table), &run, Mine::Keep).await { give_back(g, held).await; return Err(e) } }`
5. Keeper spawn (`:945-953`) unchanged.
6. Teardown: `keeper.stop().await; give_back(g, held).await;` replaces `:1039-1043` and `:1333-1337`, where:

   ```rust
   async fn give_back(g: &dyn GuardStore, held: Vec<Announced>) {
       for a in held { match guard::release(g, a).await { Ok(p) => g.lease_close(p).await, Err(a) => a.abandon() } }
   }
   ```

7. Bootstrap handover (`:1006-1011`, `:1219-1224`): owned by cluster C's lineage. The parent keeps `held` through `bootstrap_group`, the child classifies the parent's markers `Mine` through `owns` and never deletes them (R5), and the handover release is deleted. *[§0 R2 overrides: there is no `owns()`; the parent's artifacts classify `Found::Parent`, and every delete uses `is_self`.]* If C slips, the fallback is `give_back(g, std::mem::take(&mut held))` exactly where the release is today.

**Deletions in `dest_*`:** `dest_pg.rs:128-144, :194-200`; `dest_my.rs:186-273`; `dest_ch.rs:214-300`; `dest_bq.rs:93-134` (moved), `:213-253` (`lease_get_self`, `lease_claim_self`) and `:263-325`. Their `lease_key` fns remain and must equal `GuardStore::dest_label(bare)`; unit test `lease_key_is_dest_label` per engine.

- `sink/postgres.rs:51-106` (`check_peers`) and `:427-437` (`announce`, `release`, `reap_and_check_peers`)
- `sink/mysql.rs:543-683`
- `sink/clickhouse.rs:541-716` (`drop_artifact` body moves into `ChGuard::drop_object`)
- `sink/bigquery.rs:1645-1755`
- `sink/s3.rs:769-888` (the dead `reapable` closure and the empty multipart sweep go with it)
- `sink/gcs.rs:608-713`
- `sink/iceberg.rs:631-716` (`release_claim` → `IceGuard::drop_object`)
- `naming::guard_verdict` (`:402-413`)

**`benchmarks/gate.py`:**

- `LEGS` (`:44-91`) becomes a list of `Leg = collections.namedtuple("Leg", "script what env requires argv proves")`:
  - `requires` is a `frozenset`, so `req in have` becomes `requires <= have`.

  - `e2e_cdc_fence.py` → `fence.evicted-writes-nothing:(pg,)`, `keeper.skips-own-held-row:(pg,)`, `lease.killed-drain-self-heals:(pg,)`
  - `e2e_sigterm.py` → `graceful.stop-releases-lock:("ch(pg-src)",)`
  - `e2e_sigterm_my.py` → `graceful.stop-releases-lock:("ch(my-src)",)`

- **`--matrix`:** prints, for every `(claim, engine)`, the leg labels, or `WAIVED: caveat`, or `GAP`. It also greps `docs/*.md README.md py-apitap/README.md benchmarks/README.md` for `<!-- claim: (\S+) -->`. The *paragraph* is the text from the marker to the next blank line or next marker. Failures:
  - an unknown id ("the docs claim something the gate does not know");
  - a CLAIMS id with no marker ("dead claim");
  - a missing `must_say` phrase in any paragraph of that id;
  - a missing waiver `caveat` in all paragraphs of that id.

  Any failure exits 3 with the list.
- **After a run**, the summary prints the same matrix with each cell PASS, FAIL or SKIP. A cell is SKIP when every leg proving it was skipped. Any SKIP cell makes the run exit 3, as `:274` already does for skipped legs.
- **Capabilities** (`:123-144`) add:

  ```python
  def need_wheel(cap, env, want):
      py = os.environ.get(env)
      if not py: why[cap] = f"{env} is unset — point it at a venv python holding apitap=={want}"; return
      r = subprocess.run([py, "-c", "import apitap,os;print(apitap.__version__);"
                          "print(os.path.dirname(os.path.realpath(apitap.__file__)))"], capture_output=True, text=True)
      got, where = (r.stdout.split() + ["", ""])[:2]
      import apitap as mine
      if r.returncode or got != want: why[cap] = f"{env} has apitap {got or '(none)'}, want {want}"
      elif where == os.path.dirname(os.path.realpath(mine.__file__)):
          why[cap] = f"{env} resolves to the gate's own apitap at {where} — an upgrade leg would compare a wheel with itself"
      else: have.add(cap)
  need_wheel("wheel-0551", "APITAP_PY_0551", "0.55.1"); need_wheel("wheel-0560", "APITAP_PY_0560", "0.56.0")
  q = open("/sys/fs/cgroup/cpu.max").read().split() if os.path.exists("/sys/fs/cgroup/cpu.max") else ["max"]
  cpu = os.cpu_count() if q[0] == "max" else int(q[0]) / int(q[1])
  (have.add("cpu>0.6") if cpu > 0.6 else why.__setitem__("cpu>0.6", f"cgroup quota {cpu:.2f} core"))
  if not os.environ.get("APITAP_MY_GTID_URL"): why["my-gtid"] = "APITAP_MY_GTID_URL unset — a GTID-enforced MySQL"
  else: have.add("my-gtid")
  ```

  The gate never installs anything. `~/gate-0551-venv` and `~/gate-0560-venv` are prepared once from PyPI, as documented in `benchmarks/README.md`.
- **`self_test()`** (`:147-193`) grows these cases:
  - every `Leg.script` exists;
  - every `proves` key ∈ CLAIMS and every engine ∈ that claim's engines;
  - **matrix RED control:** the matrix over `LEGS` minus `e2e_cdc_lease_bq.py` reports GAP at `(lease.killed-drain-self-heals, bq)` and exits 3, while the full `LEGS` reports none there;
  - a temporary doc carrying `<!-- claim: bogus -->` fails the grep;
  - a temporary paragraph missing a `must_say` phrase fails;
  - `need_wheel` pointed at `sys.executable` reports the same-install reason.

**`benchmarks/_rig.py`** (new, shared by the new and repaired legs):
- `pause(p)` / `resume(p)` = `os.kill(p.pid, SIGSTOP/SIGCONT)`. Legs own their drains as `Popen` children, so no `docker pause` is needed.
- `wait_for(pred, secs)`.
- `locks_{pg,my,ch,bq}(T)` and `markers_{…}(T)` (`__apitap_staging` with a Cdc token letter at token position 8).
- `live_leases_{…}(T)`; the ClickHouse form is `e2e_cdc_lease.py:62-83` with `count() > 0`.
- BigQuery through REST `tables.list` and the `bq()` helper of `e2e_bq_cdc.py:40-46`. Job assertions use `INFORMATION_SCHEMA.JOBS_BY_USER`, never `JOBS_BY_PROJECT`.
- `token_of(name)`.
- `rig_fail(msg)`, which prints `(rig) … FAILED`.

#### 3.8 Order for the implementer

1. **naming.rs:** `RunId` split, `compat`, `Scan`, `collectable`, and the tests of §6.1, each seen RED first.
2. **guard.rs** with a `FakeStore`, and its tests RED first.
3. **Adapters, one engine at a time** in the order pg, my, ch, bq, s3, gcs, ice. Delete each engine's loop as its adapter lands. `run.rs` goes last. After each step, the `no_guard_decision_outside` test's allow-list shrinks by that file.
4. **`gate.py`, `_claims.py`, `_rig.py`** and the self-test.
5. **Legs** N1–N10 and the repairs, each run RED against `APITAP_PY_0560` or with the named one-line revert.
6. **Docs** sentence by sentence. `gate.py --matrix` must exit 0 before the docs commit.

### 4. Invariants and the argument

**I1 — a supported older reader sees every current run.**
- Invariant: `∀ g ∈ SUPPORTED, kind: CURRENT.announces(kind) ∩ g.scans() ≠ ∅` (a unit test).
- Interleaving: a 0.57 drain has run `announce` before its scan. A 0.55.1 `replace` of T then scans Staging:
  - It lists `<T><tok_l>__apitap_staging` (a pg `relkind='r'` UNLOGGED table; a MySQL MEMORY table is a BASE TABLE; a ClickHouse Memory table is listed; on BigQuery, v0.55.1 `staging_base` returns an unindexed name as is, `v0.55.1 bigquery.rs:1890-1893`).
  - `parse_peer` reads `l` → Cdc (v0.55.1 `naming.rs:229`) → `peer_blocks(Swap, Cdc)` → `Error::Locked`, before its staging CREATE.
- If 0.55.1 scanned first, it has already created its staging (`relkind='r'`) before the drain's scan, the drain sees `Live(Swap)` and refuses. That is announce-then-check with no tie-break, unchanged.

**I2 — the current scan sees every supported older writer, or the hole is declared.**
- Invariant: `KNOWN_HOLES` equals exactly the empty-announcement pairs.
- The one hole, a 0.55.1 drain, is documented and pinned by leg N1c.

**I3 — BigQuery, both lanes.**
- A 0.55.1 bulk run's only artifacts are `<T><tok_r>__apitap_staging_N`. `BqGuard::list` yields `Listed{canonical: …staging, raw: …staging_N}` and `classify` sees `Live(Swap)`, so the drain refuses (§3.5 reverse closed).
- `mine_leftovers` and collection delete **raw** names, so a run's own `_3` worker table is deleted rather than a nonexistent canonical name (graft).

**I4 — only empty markers are ever collected.**
- By R4 plus `collectable`: every name collectable returns `Some` for is a Cdc-token Lock or Staging, and those are created only by `guard::announce`, empty.
- A Swap/Incremental token is never collectable, and neither is Legacy.
- Corner: a child bootstrap (C) mints a bulk token, so its data staging is `Live(Swap)` and never collectable. If B's parent-token reuse shipped, a data staging would sit under a Cdc token, which R4 forbids.

**I5 — refuse before touching any lease.**
- If any blocker is uncollectable, `check_peers` returns before `lease_get`. No dead drain is claimed on the way to a refusal the run would issue anyway.
- Pinned by call-log unit test G3.

**I6 — one claim per token, and a victim's lease is never closed by a collector.**
- A dead 0.57 drain has a lock and a marker, which means one `lease_claim`, `sweep_run`, then two drops.
- Attack "F1 × two markers": previously the collector deleted the lease after the lock DROP, and a failed marker DROP left a marker with no lease, uncollectable for ever with a false message.
- Now the lease row stays `collected = true`. Every engine's claim treats `collected` as lapsed (`lease.rs:69-71`; pg `:278`; my after §3.9; ch sticky per A). So the next run re-claims, re-sweeps and re-drops. Unit test G5 drives exactly this.

**I7 — scratch is swept before markers.**
- If `sweep_run` fails, the markers remain, so the victim stays visible and a later run retries.
- If the order were reversed, a failed sweep after the markers were dropped would leak scratch for ever: scratch is not in GUARDED and age-reaping is forbidden.

**I8 — a lease is closed only after every marker is gone.**
- `lease_close` takes `Released`, which only `guard::release` constructs, and only when every `drop_object` returned Ok.
- This replaces the comment-enforced order at `run.rs:1033-1043` with a type.

**I9 — no silent forgetting.**
- `Announced` is `#[must_use]`. Its `Drop` is loud and `debug_assert`s, and is skipped while panicking so it never aborts on an unwind.
- `abandon()` is the only quiet exit, and it prints the names left standing.

**I10 — a child never deletes its parent's marker.**
- `mine_leftovers` uses `is_self`. The parent's `<t><L>__apitap_staging` classifies `Mine` for the child (C's `owns`), so it is neither a blocker nor deleted. *[§0 R2 overrides: there is no `owns()`; the parent's artifacts classify `Found::Parent`, and every delete uses `is_self`.]*
- A 0.55.1 bulk run beside a first-run bootstrap is therefore still refused. This closes the "§3.5 forward reopens during bootstrap" trace.

**I11 — every decision lives in one place.**
- A unit test `no_guard_decision_outside` reads every `src/sink/*.rs` and `src/logbased/*.rs` via `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR`. It asserts that none contains `naming::classify(`, `naming::blockers(`, `naming::collectable(` or `peer_blocks(`.

**I12 — docs cannot out-claim the gate.**
- Every marker names a CLAIMS key and every key has a marker.
- Every cell is proven or waived with a published caveat, and the `must_say` engines appear in the paragraph.

**I13 — no lost race is ever a PASS.** The repaired legs of §6.3 turn "the precondition did not happen" into `(rig) FAILED`, observed on the server.

**Corners the attacks raised and where they close:**
- ClickHouse TTL resurrecting a lease, or the weekend wedge: R3, owned by A, pinned by leg N3 "stale" and "ttl-merge".
- Two collector loops: R1.
- Engine-wrong failure-modes sentences: §7's rewrite from A's design plus the `must_say` engine lists.
- N8's RED control dead under B: §6.2 N8 uses the 0.56.0 wheel as the control.
- BigQuery 0.56.0 victim beside a 0.57.0 collector (two winners): documented under the `compat.bq-0560-victim` claim, pinned by N2 case 7.
- Postgres `search_path` blindness: A's `resolve_parts` feeds `PgGuard.schema`.

### 5. Per engine

| engine | mechanism | weaker there, honestly |
|---|---|---|
| Postgres | `PgGuard`. A drain holds `<T><tok>__apitap_lock` and `<T><tok>__apitap_staging` (zero-column, UNLOGGED, schema-qualified) for its whole life. Bulk announces a lock only and drops it after the staging CREATE. Fence: `dest_pg.rs fence_tx`, unchanged. Rollback pre-flight: `SELECT count(*) FROM _apitap_lease WHERE NOT collected AND expires_at > now()`. | Nothing new. |
| MySQL / MariaDB | `MyGuard`, scanning `[Lock, Staging, Old]` in both lanes. MEMORY markers. Pre-flight uses `UTC_TIMESTAMP(6)`. | `collect.claim-then-crash` stays RED until the MySQL cluster's §3.9 fix, and the matrix shows that cell with its red leg (N4 case 4), not as a gap. |
| ClickHouse | `ChGuard`, scanning `[Lock, Staging, New]` in both lanes. Memory markers; `ON CLUSTER … SYNC` for bulk only. | A check, not a fence: an evicted drain can land the one owned statement already executing (A's bound). The lease depends on R3's TTL restriction. |
| BigQuery | `BqGuard` with a raw/canonical listing in both lanes. INT64 marker tables. Lease fns shared by both lanes. | Fencing is by A's per-run lock-table conflict. *[§0 L10 overrides: the BigQuery fence is the per-run table `_apitap_fence<tok>`, not the lock table; §0 L7 strikes the ClickHouse TTL.]* A 0.56.0 BigQuery drain has no check at all, so during an upgrade it must be stopped first (`compat.bq-0560-victim`). The no-billing path is waived. |
| S3 | `S3Guard`, bulk only: the segment→canonical translation, lease stubs. | No lease store: a dead run's lock says "nothing collects it", as today. Staging appears only with the first part, so a 0.55.1 bulk run is blind to a 0.57 S3 bulk run until then (pre-existing). |
| GCS | `GcsGuard`, same as S3. | As S3, plus no gate leg at all (waived: the SA is BigQuery-only). |
| Iceberg | `IceGuard` for bulk (Lock spelled as the Staging-suffixed claim); `NoGuard` for drains. | Drains are unguarded, now stated in usage.md's CDC section. |

- `only_a_drains_markers_are_collectable` replaces `only_a_lock_is_ever_collectable` (`:1541-1560`), whose Cdc-staging assertion is now intentionally reversed. Assertions:

#### 6.2 New e2e legs (each asks the server; `(rig)` failures are FAIL)

**N1 `e2e_rolling_upgrade.py <0551|0560> <pg|ch|bq>`**
- Requires `wheel-0551`/`wheel-0560` (+`bq`). Proves the `compat.*` cells.
- **(a)** A NEW drain child with a 40k backlog and `APITAP_CDC_WINDOW_BYTES=262144`.
  - Wait until the server lists both the lock and the marker (`wait_for`, 60 s, else rig).
  - OLD `replace` under the OLD venv → rc ≠ 0, with `locked` and "draining changes into it" in stderr.
  - Poll the server every 0.5 s during the drain: no OLD `<T>_<tok_r>__apitap_staging` ever appears.
  - NEW drain rc 0, and dest count == source count.
  - RED: NEW = `APITAP_PY_0560` → the OLD replace succeeds and staging appears.
- **(b)** An OLD 10M `replace` child; wait for its staging (`_N` tables on bq).
  - The NEW drain raises `LockedError` (class).
  - RED on bq: NEW = 0.56.0 → the drain proceeds.
- **(c)** An OLD 0.55.1 drain child: poll for its whole life for any `__apitap_lock`/`__apitap_staging` beside T → none. This pins the hole.
- **(d)** After a NEW drain, OLD 0.55.1 `transfer(dest_as_src, scratch, tables="*")` → the scratch catalog lists `_apitap_lease`.
  - Run the documented DROPs and repeat → absent.
- **(e)** SIGKILL a NEW drain → the lock and the marker are on the server.
  - An OLD 0.56.0 run → refused, naming `__apitap_staging`.
  - A NEW run after TTL+3 → both gone, and the watermark advanced.

**N2 `e2e_cdc_lease_bq.py`** (`bq`; the eviction case also needs `mariadb`)
- Cases 1-5 of `e2e_cdc_lease.py` against `bigquery://`:
  - locks and markers via `tables.list`;
  - leases via `SELECT token, collected, TIMESTAMP_DIFF(expires_at, CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(), SECOND) FROM _apitap_lease`;
  - case 3 also asserts the marker is gone;
  - the epoch-zero plant is refused and left in place;
  - a live drain is not collectable.
- Case 6 (eviction):
  1. Drain A with a backlog; once one window has landed (the state row count moved), `pause(A)`.
  2. Sleep TTL+3. Drain B prints "collected", rc 0. Read `n1 = SELECT COUNT(*) FROM _apitap_state WHERE dest_table='<T>'`.
  3. `resume(A)`. A exits rc ≠ 0 with "no longer holds".
  4. `n2 == n1`, and A's row still has `collected = TRUE`.
  - RED: A under the 0.56.0 wheel → n2 > n1, rc 0.
- Case 7 (`compat.bq-0560-victim`, needs `wheel-0560`): the same with A = 0.56.0 and B = NEW. The leg asserts n2 > n1 and prints it as the documented residual. It is two-sided: a 0.57 A never writes.

**N3 `e2e_cdc_evict_ch.py`** (`mariadb`; TTL 30)
- **Eviction:** drain A; wait until the server `count()` moves; `pause`; sleep 33; B prints "collected" and `wm_b`; `resume` A → rc ≠ 0, "no longer holds".
  - Sample `SELECT count() > 0 AND argMax(collected, seq) = 1 FROM _apitap_lease WHERE token='<A>'` every second for 3×renew_secs → always true.
  - `watermark() == wm_b`, and dest == source.
  - RED: A = 0.56.0 → the sample flips within 3 s (`dest_ch.rs:173-183`).
- **ttl-merge:** after B collects, `OPTIMIZE TABLE _apitap_lease FINAL`, then resume A → A still exits ≠ 0 with the watermark unchanged.
  - RED: restore the unconditional TTL, with `expires_at` planted 2 days in the past.
- **stale:** plant a lock plus a lease row with `expires_at = now64(6) - INTERVAL 2 DAY` and collected 0, run `OPTIMIZE … FINAL`, then run the table → collected and resumes.
  - RED: the current TTL deletes the row → the run is refused with "nothing collects it".

**N4 `e2e_cdc_fence_my.py`** (pg→MySQL :3307). Legs as in the design:
1. Evicted at lock appearance; `after == at_eviction` exactly.
   - RED: skip `fence_tx` at `dest_my.rs:535`.
2. A holder `FOR UPDATE` → refused in < 17 s with "applying a window right now".
3. Sibling expiry advances during a hold.
   - RED: the 1205 arm returns Err.
4. Already-collected plant → collected.
   - RED until the §3.9 fix.
5. TRUNCATE under eviction: count == N.
   - RED until the §3.8 fix.

**N5 `e2e_state_contract.py <pg|my|ch|bq>`**
- The hard-coded `DST` (`:32`) becomes a selector. Per engine, `_apitap_state` is read on that server (ch `FINAL`; bq REST).
- Leg 4: after an `append` bootstrap, `log_based` is refused and names the cursor, with dest count and state row count unchanged.
- RED: `my` under 0.56.0 re-bootstraps (`dest_my.rs:281-285`).

**N6 TRUNCATE-only window** (owned by the §3.4 cluster; declared here as `truncate.only-window`)
- Cases in `e2e_mariadb_cdc.py` and `e2e_mysql84.py`: `TRUNCATE` with no following rows → rc 0, server count 0, watermark advanced.
- A 2-table group with sibling traffic.
- RED: 0.56.0 → "missing WAL column list".

**N7 `e2e_slots.py`** (pg→pg)
- Tables `[a,b,c,d]`, `slots=2`. Evict `{a,b}` at lock appearance.
- Exit ≠ 0 with a `MultiTransferError` naming a and b. c and d equal the source with their watermarks advanced; a and b equal their at-eviction counts.
- Two-sided: a single run-wide lease key fails the c,d assertion.

**N8 `e2e_changelog_upgrade.py <ch|bq>`** (`wheel-0551`, `wheel-0560`, +`bq`)
- OLD 0.55.1 drains a window touching keys 1..10 with key 1 last.
- The pre-flight query returns > 0 (the collision exists).
- NEW 0.57 drains a window touching key 1 first, with **no** idle drain.
- `SELECT v FROM <t>__current WHERE id=1` equals the NEW value; no `(_apitap_lsn, _apitap_seq)` pair has count > 1; the new rows' `min(_apitap_seq)` at the boundary stamp == old max + 1.
- RED: NEW = `APITAP_PY_0560` → `__current` shows the OLD value.
- If B slips, this leg runs the documented idle-drain procedure instead, with RED = skip the idle drain.

**N9 `e2e_bq_guard.py`** (`bq`)
- Plant `<T>_0000000l000abcd__apitap_lock` via REST.
- `replace` raises `apitap.LockedError` (class) and the lock is still listed.
- Delete it; the control replace succeeds.

**N10 `e2e_guard_matrix.py <my|ch|bq|s3|ice>`** (`requires`: none / none / bq / iceberg (MinIO :9100) / iceberg)
- Plant a live-looking Swap staging (or S3 segment, or Iceberg claim) with a fresh token → `replace` raises `LockedError` and the plant is left.
- Delete it; the control succeeds.
- my/ch/bq: plant a Cdc lock plus a live lease row → a drain is refused and the plant is left.
- Proves the `guard.bulk-vs-bulk` and `guard.drain-vs-bulk` cells.

**N11 `e2e_bq_multi_drain.py`** (owned by A; declared as `bq.multi-drain-one-dataset`)
- 4 drains on one dataset for 10 min: 0 non-zero exits, and retries counted via `JOBS_BY_USER`.

Delete the `all([])` shape (`:455-460`). | Revert `no_lease_table` to a message-only match (`postgres.rs:128-131`) → B raises `RuntimeError` |

### 7. Docs (exact replacements; every marker is checked by `--matrix`)

**`failure-modes.md:335-342`** (indented two spaces, inside the bullet) becomes:

> **Where the lease is a fence, and where it is only a check.** <!-- claim: fence.evicted-writes-nothing --> On Postgres, MySQL and BigQuery an evicted drain writes nothing more. On Postgres and MySQL the drain's apply transaction holds its lease row. On BigQuery the collector's claim writes into the victim's own lock table, and every apply script touches that table, so a script that overlaps a claim is aborted by BigQuery's conflict detection and rolls back whole. <!-- claim: check.evicted-stops-within-statement --> ClickHouse has no transaction to hold, so every statement a drain writes there carries its own ownership predicate, and each statement is bounded server-side to half the lease TTL. An evicted drain lands at most the one statement already executing. That statement carries its window's start LSN, and the next drain's replay appends nothing for it. In 0.56.0 BigQuery's apply read no lease at all, and ClickHouse's evicted drain overwrote the collector's claim on its next renewal. Both are proven now by `e2e_cdc_lease_bq.py` and `e2e_cdc_evict_ch.py`.

These sentences describe cluster A's resolved design. If A lands differently, the `must_say` phrases in `_claims.py` change with the text, and the matrix stays red until the named legs are green.

**`failure-modes.md:347-351`** ("A killed CDC BOOTSTRAP … a new way to die.") becomes:

> A killed CDC bootstrap leaves the drain's lock and marker, which clear themselves, plus the full load's staging table, which does not — drop it by hand; the error names it. <!-- claim: lease.killed-drain-self-heals --> A drain killed outright at any other point leaves only its lock and marker, and they clear themselves once the lease lapses, on every guarded destination — on ClickHouse the lease table's TTL never deletes a row that has not been collected, so this holds after a weekend too. <!-- claim: ch.lease-survives-ttl-merge --> A drain partitioned for longer than the TTL can be collected by a peer; it then writes nothing more on Postgres, MySQL and BigQuery, and at most one statement on ClickHouse, and exits at its next statement or renewal, even after a ClickHouse merge has run on the lease table.

**`failure-modes.md`: new `## Upgrading and rolling back`,** inserted before `## Rules of thumb` (`:437`):

> A run of one apitap must refuse correctly beside a run of another. From 0.57.0 that is a designed property: `naming::compat::Generation` records what every supported release writes and scans, and a unit test refuses to build a release that a supported older reader cannot see. <!-- claim: compat.old-bulk-refused-by-new-drain --> A 0.57.0 drain announces itself with both a `__apitap_lock` and an empty tokenized `__apitap_staging` marker, because a 0.55.1 bulk run scans only for staging, so a 0.55.1 or 0.56.0 `replace`/`append`/`merge` is refused beside it. <!-- claim: compat.new-drain-refused-by-old-bulk --> The reverse holds too — a 0.57.0 drain is refused beside a 0.55.1 bulk run on every guarded destination, including BigQuery's per-worker staging tables.
>
> **The one hole, and the order that avoids it.** <!-- claim: compat.old-drain-writes-nothing --> A 0.55.1 drain writes no artifact at all, so nothing can refuse beside it. In a rolling upgrade, upgrade the `log_based` jobs first and the bulk jobs after, and until every drain is 0.56.0+, do not schedule a bulk run of a table a 0.55.1 drain may be draining. 0.55.0 is not a supported neighbour; move it to 0.55.1 first. <!-- claim: compat.bq-0560-victim --> On BigQuery, stop every 0.56.0 BigQuery drain before any 0.57.0 run of the same dataset starts: a 0.56.0 drain never reads its lease, so if a 0.57.0 run collects it while it is paused, both write.
>
> **Rolling back.** <!-- claim: compat.rollback-leaks --> A 0.55.1 apitap does not know that `_apitap_lease` and `_apitap_cdc_pending` are apitap's, and a `tables="*"` transfer that reads this destination as a source replicates them. Before rolling back to 0.55.1: stop every drain; confirm that no live lease remains — Postgres `SELECT count(*) FROM _apitap_lease WHERE NOT collected AND expires_at > now()`, MySQL the same with `UTC_TIMESTAMP(6)`, BigQuery with `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP()`, ClickHouse `SELECT count() FROM (SELECT token, argMax(collected, seq) c, argMax(expires_at, seq) e FROM _apitap_lease GROUP BY dest_key, token) WHERE c = 0 AND e > now64(6)` — then `DROP TABLE _apitap_lease` and `DROP TABLE _apitap_cdc_pending`, or list tables explicitly. Rolling back to 0.56.0 needs nothing, with one cost: a 0.57.0 drain killed outright leaves a lock and a staging marker, and 0.56.0 collects the lock but refuses on the marker, naming it — drop it by hand, once.
>
> **`changelog=True` across the boundary.** <!-- claim: changelog.upgrade-stamp-boundary --> 0.55.x stamped `_apitap_lsn` with a window's end; 0.56.0+ stamps its start, which is the watermark it drained from, so the last old window and the first new one share a stamp. 0.57.0 continues `_apitap_seq` above the rows already at that stamp, so `<t>__current` (ordered by `lsn DESC, seq DESC`) shows the newer value. Upgrade 0.55.x straight to 0.57.0 — do not stop at 0.56.0 on a `changelog=True` destination, because 0.56.0 restarts `_apitap_seq` at 0. As a diagnostic (optional, not a step), this counts the rows at the current watermark's stamp: ClickHouse `SELECT count() FROM <t> WHERE _apitap_lsn = (SELECT watermark FROM _apitap_state FINAL WHERE dest_table='<t>' AND source_id NOT LIKE 'server-identity:%') AND _apitap_op != 'B'`, BigQuery the same without `FINAL`.

If cluster B's seq continuation slips, the last paragraph instead reads: "…share a stamp and `_apitap_seq` restarts at 0 under it, so upgrading a `changelog=True` destination is unsupported until the release that continues the sequence; until then, stop the schedule, let the drain finish, run one more drain on the old version with the source idle, confirm the query above returns 0, then upgrade." N8 then switches to that procedure.

**`stability.md`:**

- **`:23`** append to the destination-artifacts row: " — and, from 0.57.0, the guard artifacts: a run of a supported older apitap (0.55.1, 0.56.0) refuses beside a newer one's run and vice versa, except that a 0.55.1 *drain* is invisible to everything ([upgrading and rolling back](failure-modes.md#upgrading-and-rolling-back))."
- **`:25`**, replace "a drain and a bulk run refuse each other in both directions, and so do two drains." with "a drain and a bulk run refuse each other in both directions, and so do two drains — on a fleet that is entirely 0.56.0+, and from 0.57.0 also beside 0.55.1 bulk runs; a 0.55.1 drain is visible to nothing, so upgrade drains before bulk jobs, and stop 0.56.0 BigQuery drains before 0.57.0 runs start ([the procedure](failure-modes.md#upgrading-and-rolling-back))."
- **`:25`**, also replace "A killed drain's lock **clears itself** once its lease lapses (`APITAP_LEASE_TTL_SECS`, 300s by default)" with "A killed drain's lock **clears itself** once its lease lapses (`APITAP_LEASE_TTL_SECS`, 300s by default) on every guarded destination; an evicted drain writes nothing more on Postgres, MySQL and BigQuery and at most one statement on ClickHouse (each proven by the gate's eviction legs)".
- **`:47`** becomes `apitap==0.57.0`.
- **`:58-64`**, replace "A 36-leg suite … the version number depends on someone remembering." with "A suite whose coverage is declared runs before every tag: `benchmarks/gate.py --matrix` prints every published claim × engine and the leg that proves it by asking the server, prints every waived cell with the caveat published beside the claim, and exits non-zero when a claim in these pages has no leg. It runs a second wheel for the upgrade legs and pauses drains for the eviction legs. A human still starts it."

**`README.md:200-203`**, replace "Since 0.56.0 `mode="log_based"` drains … stays the scheduler's job." with "Since 0.56.0 `mode="log_based"` drains enter the same guard: a drain and a bulk run refuse each other in both directions, and so do two drains — and since 0.57.0 that holds across an upgrade as well: a 0.55.1 bulk run is refused beside a 0.57.0 drain, because the drain also announces itself in the older vocabulary. A 0.55.1 drain is the one thing nothing can see; upgrade drains first. Iceberg destinations are the exception — their drains are still unguarded, and one drain per Iceberg table stays the scheduler's job."

**`py-apitap/README.md:97-99`**, replace "a drain and a bulk run refuse each other, and so do two drains (Postgres, MySQL, ClickHouse and BigQuery destinations; Iceberg drains are not guarded)." with the same text followed by " — from 0.57.0 also beside a 0.55.1 bulk run, though a 0.55.1 drain is invisible to every other run, so upgrade drains before bulk jobs."

**`usage.md`:**

- **`:375-380`**, replace "A run killed with SIGKILL … the loser's error is uglier." with "A bulk run killed with SIGKILL leaves its staging object behind, and later runs of that table refuse until you drop it; the error names it. A `log_based` drain killed the same way leaves a lock that clears itself once its lease lapses (`APITAP_LEASE_TTL_SECS`, 300 s), and the next run resumes from the watermark — except during a first run's full load, which also leaves the load's staging table for you to drop. Two runs that start in the very same instant can both be refused; nothing is written when they are. See [Two runs, one table](failure-modes.md#two-runs-one-table) for the matrix and for why nothing collects a bulk leftover automatically."
- **`:861-862`**, replace "An overlapping run fails fast (the slot shows active)." with "An overlapping run is refused before a row moves — by the destination guard on Postgres, MySQL, ClickHouse and BigQuery (0.56.0+), and on a Postgres source also by the slot showing active. Into Iceberg only the slot check applies: Iceberg drains are unguarded, so one drain per Iceberg table is the scheduler's job."
- **`:904-909`**, append to the Scope bullet: " Iceberg drains are not covered by the destination guard (bootstraps are)."
- **`:1284-1285`**, replace "re-run, and the only cost is a staging table left on disk until the next run drops it." with "drop the staging table the next run's error names, then re-run — nothing collects a bulk run's staging on its own, because a timestamp cannot tell a crashed run from a slow one."

**Other docs:**
- **`benchmarks/README.md`:** preparing `~/gate-0551-venv` and `~/gate-0560-venv` (`python3 -m venv …; …/pip install apitap==0.55.1`), exporting `APITAP_PY_0551`/`APITAP_PY_0560`, `APITAP_MY_GTID_URL`, the `gate.py --matrix` release pre-flight, and the regression-claim table carrying the `leg.*` markers.
- **Code comments:** `naming.rs`'s `collectable` doc (`:488-497` "A LOCK only. Never staging") is rewritten to "only a drain's empty markers; never a bulk run's staging". `guard.rs` and `compat` carry the generation table's rationale.

### 8. What this does not fix

- **A 0.55.1 drain is invisible to every run** (KNOWN_HOLES). Only the upgrade order closes it; N1c pins that it really writes nothing.
- **Rollback to 0.55.1 still replicates `_apitap_lease`/`_apitap_cdc_pending`** under `tables="*"` unless the operator drops them. v0.55.1's `is_artifact` (`v0.55.1 naming.rs:633-634`) cannot change.
- **After a rollback to 0.56.0 or 0.55.1,** a killed 0.57 drain's staging marker is refused with "nothing collects it", which means one manual DROP.
- **A 0.56.0 BigQuery drain beside a 0.57.0 collector** can be two winners. The only remedy is operational (stop them first). N2 case 7 records it.
- **Object stores:** a bulk run's staging exists only from the first part, so a 0.55.1 S3/GCS bulk run is blind to a newer one until then (pre-existing; no drains exist there).
- **0.55.0 (not 0.55.1)** was not verified for an age horizon on tokenized names; it is declared unsupported.
- **The failure-modes sentences on ClickHouse and BigQuery** and the `lease.*` ClickHouse cells depend on cluster A (predicates, lock-table fence, R3 TTL) *[§0 L10 overrides: the BigQuery fence is the per-run table `_apitap_fence<tok>`, not the lock table; §0 L7 strikes the ClickHouse TTL.]* and cluster B (seq continuation). The matrix keeps them red until those legs pass, but this cluster ships none of those fixes.
- **N4 cases 4 and 5 stay red** until the MySQL cluster's §3.8/§3.9 fixes land. N6, N7 and N11 belong to other clusters; they are declared here.
- **The matrix checks that the words are present,** not that they are true. A wrong sentence that contains its `must_say` phrases and its waiver caveat still passes; that remains a reviewer's job.
- **`Announced`'s `Drop` is loud but cannot release asynchronously.** A panic unwinding through `run_group` still leaves markers, as does the §6 low `Keeper`-without-`Drop` item.
- **GCS and the BigQuery no-billing path have no gate leg** (waived, and each waiver is published).
- **Bootstrap liveness under a per-minute cron** on large groups (a child refused by a probe's transient markers) is unchanged from 0.56.0. Cluster C's lineage makes the fix available; it is not made here.

---

## §3 Order of work

Each step is one commit, with no Claude attribution trailer. "RED→GREEN" names the test that must be seen failing **before** the change and passing after, together with how it was made RED.

- **U** = unit test in the QA rig (`~/apitap-qa`, own `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`).
- **L** = e2e leg. Its RED is observed against `~/gate-0560-venv` (the PyPI 0.56.0 wheel) unless stated otherwise.
- **◆ CHECKPOINT** = a wheel build plus the full gate is mandatory before the next step (§6.2).

### Phase 0: rig and gate hardening (Python only)

0. **Rig (not a commit).** On the VPS:
   - build `~/gate-0551-venv` and `~/gate-0560-venv` from PyPI;
   - `run-server.sh` brings up `apitap-bench-my-gtid` (the `run-server.sh` edit is committed in step 1);
   - export `BQ_SA`, `APITAP_PY_0551`, `APITAP_PY_0560`, `APITAP_MY_GTID_URL`;
   - record baseline receipts on the 0.56.0 PGO wheel: pg→ch CDC 10M, pg→pg 10M, mysql→ch 10M, all at 0.5 CPU / 256 MB (§6.3);
   - run the gate on 0.56.0 and record 40/40.
1. **gate.py → `Leg` namedtuple.**
   - Files: `benchmarks/gate.py`, `benchmarks/_claims.py`, `benchmarks/_rig.py`, `benchmarks/run-server.sh`, `benchmarks/README.md` (venvs, env vars).
   - Adds: `frozenset` requires, the capabilities in T3, `proves` on the 40 existing legs, `--matrix`, the post-run matrix, and the self-test cases (E §3.7).
   - RED→GREEN: `gate.py --self-test`. The matrix-RED case must report GAP at `(lease.killed-drain-self-heals, bq)` when `e2e_cdc_lease_bq.py` is absent, and the `need_wheel` same-install case must fail.
   - Legs: none new. `--matrix` is expected to exit 3 from here until step 43.
2. **Repair existing legs** (E §6.3, plus A's `e2e_cdc_fence.py` rewrite). Files:
   - `e2e_concurrent_runs.py` leg 7;
   - `e2e_sigterm.py`, `e2e_sigterm_my.py` (graceful asserts: `locks()==[]`, `live_leases()==[]`);
   - `e2e_failure_modes.py` case 2 (`wait_for`, delete `kill_after`);
   - `e2e_cdc_fence.py` :180-182, which asserts both `after == at_eviction` and `count(*) WHERE synced_at > t_ev = 0`.

   All must be GREEN on 0.56.0. Each is RED under its named one-line revert, done on the VPS copy only:
   - revert `no_lease_table` to message-only matching → leg 7 raises `RuntimeError`;
   - comment out the graceful release → the lock survives;
   - use a 200-row backlog → case 2 is FAIL, not PASS;
   - remove the `fence_tx` calls at dest_pg.rs:403/:420 → `after == total`.

## §4 Explicitly deferred

| Item | Why not now | Where it is stated |
|---|---|---|
| ClickHouse remains a **time** fence: a lightweight-DELETE mutation killed at ttl/2 may finish in the background, and a server clock step can shift the bound. | ClickHouse has no transaction; the predicate plus the bound is the strongest tool without pull-apply (rejected by the project's rules). | failure-modes, §2.A §8 |
| ClickHouse WAL TRUNCATE is O(rows) (a lightweight delete). | A `TRUNCATE` cannot carry a predicate. | usage.md CDC section |
| MySQL TRUNCATE: the gap between the implicit commit and the MDL-X request inside one statement. | A server-internal gap; the only alternatives are O(rows) or wedge-prone. | §2.A §5 |
| BigQuery rests on documented conflict and queue semantics. A collector may wait up to 6 h for one in-flight script. Chunk-level partial commit remains, and is healed by the marker. | No client-side lock exists. | §2.A §5 |
| A 0.56.0 BigQuery victim beside a 0.57.0 collector gives two winners. | 0.56.0 cannot be patched. | Operational procedure, `compat.bq-0560-victim` |
| Iceberg drains unguarded; bulk-lane locks carry no lease. | By design. | README, usage.md |
| Postgres bulk staging unqualified; CDC `_apitap_state` unqualified. | Moving the state table orphans existing watermarks. | §2.A §8 |
| `_apitap_lease` rows are never garbage-collected (now including ClickHouse). | Age-based reaping is forbidden; only the owner's close deletes a row. | stability.md |
| The `mod store` boundary is pinned only by the lint test and review. | — | §2.A §8 |
| ClickHouse `_apitap_cdc_pending` / `_apitap_state` are `ReplacingMergeTree(at/synced_at)`, so a merge after a backward clock step can keep an older version. | Fixing it needs a table rebuild. | §2.B §8 |
| A legacy marker naming a stamp that holds only 0.55.x rows is read as a prefix. | Identical to 0.56.0; the mixed case is refused. | §2.B §8 |
| BigQuery PRUNE escape (> 31 days of rewind). | Narrow; restarts seq on one stamp. | §2.B §8 |
| One source transaction that alters a tracked table between its own row changes is refused, not split. | Refusing loudly is correct. | usage.md |
| A genuine server-side double execution of one BigQuery job id is not defended. A consumer that read trimmed replay rows sees them again under a new stamp. | The stream cannot seek. | usage.md |
| `compact_pending_if_bloated` may meet a concurrent-update conflict with a sibling's script. | Best effort, outside scripts, above 4096 rows only; `cdc_script` retries the sibling. | This brief |
| MySQL-source layout from `information_schema`: a same-count column reshuffle between event and catalog is undetectable. | Pre-existing. | §2.B §5 |
| A hung sibling delays the bulk error. SIGTERM does not stop bulk spans. | Changing SIGTERM semantics is a separate decision. | §2.C §8 |
| Bootstrap livelock under a frequent schedule; the "child ignores peers" fix is unsound. | Shown by the counterexample in §2.C §8. | usage.md: do not schedule first runs faster than a bootstrap takes |
| A kill during the bootstrap leaves the child's staging (manual). | Staging holds data and is never collectable. | usage.md :375-380 (E) |
| BigQuery `jobs.cancel` is best effort; a double transport failure leaves one staging table re-creatable. | Google's API. | §2.C I5 |
| GCS has no gate leg (the service account is BigQuery-only); the BigQuery sandbox path has no leg. | Rig limit. | WAIVED rows in §5 |
| D's constants are design points (`PER_ROW_GROUP`, `LOADER_CHUNKS`, `PAGE_TRANSIENT`); cages below 107/117 MiB run one pipe above the model; CSV lanes are priced as parquet; the read-lane chunk still comes from the transfer model; the Iceberg CDC delete set is materialised; the MySQL sink is declared STREAMING from peaks; a single row wider than the row group; an explicit `parallel` above the model can OOM; N delete files per merge. | Each is safe, not optimal, or the caller's choice. | §2.D §8, profiling.md |
| A 0.55.1 drain is invisible to every run; rolling back to 0.55.1 replicates bookkeeping; after a rollback, one manual DROP of a marker; object-store staging is visible only from the first part; 0.55.0 is unsupported. | Old releases cannot change. | failure-modes "Upgrading and rolling back" |
| The matrix checks that words are present, not that they are true. `Announced::drop` cannot release asynchronously on a panic. | Review stays human. | §2.E §8 |
| ClickHouse CDC lease key on the unstripped `dest_table` (audit §6 low). | Moot: the CDC lane cannot address a dotted destination; `lease_key_is_dest_label` pins the equality. | This brief |

## §6 How to verify the whole

**6.1 Per commit** (the rules against building locally and against stale rsync builds):
1. Edit on the MacBook.
2. `rsync -az --delete` from an **absolute** source to `~/apitap-qa`, excluding `.git target dist benchmarks/wheels* benchmarks/.env`.
3. `find crates -name '*.rs' -exec touch {} +`.
4. `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=~/apitap-qa-target cargo test -p apitap-core`.
5. Confirm the new test names appear in `cargo test -- --list`.
6. Record the RED first. For a unit test, revert the one line on the VPS copy only, run the single test, restore. For a leg, run it under `~/gate-0560-venv/bin/python`.
7. Then record GREEN.

Never pipe pip or cargo output to `/dev/null`.

**6.2 At each ◆ CHECKPOINT:**
1. rsync to `~/apitap-lib`.
2. `docker run … ghcr.io/pyo3/maturin build --release -m py-apitap/Cargo.toml`, then `sudo chown -R ubuntu:ubuntu ~/apitap-lib/target`. Launch long builds with `setsid nohup … < /dev/null &` and confirm by artifact (`docker ps --filter ancestor=ghcr.io/pyo3/maturin`), never by `pgrep`.
3. Install the wheel under its PEP 427 name into `~/gate-venv`. The md5 of the installed `_apitap.abi3.so` must equal the md5 of the `.so` inside the wheel; abort on mismatch.
4. `~/gate-venv/bin/python benchmarks/gate.py --self-test`, then the full `gate.py` with `BQ_SA`, `APITAP_PY_0551`, `APITAP_PY_0560` and `APITAP_MY_GTID_URL` set and every container up. Pass = 0 FAIL, 0 SKIP of registered legs, and the matrix GAP count not higher than at the previous checkpoint.
5. End every bench run with a destination-table drop (keep seeds).

**6.3 Receipts** (headline tier 0.5 CPU / 256 MB, PGO wheel against the PyPI 0.56.0 PGO wheel, n ≥ 3, written to `benchmarks/profiling.md`):
- pg→ClickHouse CDC 10M (window-apply seconds; T9);
- pg→pg 10M bulk;
- mysql→ClickHouse 10M bulk;
- D's E1/E2 cells, including `memory.peak`.

A regression above 10 % on any of them goes to the owner before tagging.

**6.4 Release:**
1. Bump `Cargo.toml:6` to `0.57.0` (and `Cargo.lock`), `docs/stability.md:47`, `py-apitap/README.md`. `gate.py --matrix` must exit 0.
2. Build the PGO wheel with `benchmarks/pgo-build.sh`, including the added pg→ClickHouse `log_based` training (T10), confirmed by artifact.
3. Install the PGO wheel into `~/gate-venv` and verify the md5 as in 6.2.
4. Run the full `gate.py`: **exit 0 on the PGO wheel that ships.** Record the leg count and the `.so` md5.
5. Release commit:
   - `git rm --cached dist/apitap-0.56.0-*.whl`;
   - `git add -f dist/apitap-0.57.0-cp39-abi3-manylinux_2_17_x86_64.manylinux2014_x86_64.whl`;
   - `git ls-tree -r HEAD dist/` shows exactly **one** file;
   - message body = the release notes (G3) plus §1's upgrade paragraph plus "Gate N/N on this PGO wheel, including BigQuery".
6. Strip every `Co-Authored-By` trailer from `origin/main..HEAD` with the filter-branch recipe. `git diff --quiet <old-HEAD> HEAD`, and `git log origin/main..HEAD --format=%B | grep -c Co-Authored-By` → `0`.
7. Push `main`. Tag a never-used name `v0.57.0` and push the tag alone. If it has to be redone, use a fresh name (`v0.57.0-publish`); never re-push the same tag.
8. Watch the Actions `smoke-py39` → `pypi` jobs, then poll PyPI.

**6.5 Pull back from PyPI:**
1. Create a fresh venv and run `pip install --no-cache-dir apitap==0.57.0`.
2. Check that `apitap.__version__ == "0.57.0"`.
3. The md5 of the installed `_apitap.abi3.so` must be **identical** to the gated PGO `.so` from 6.4. If it is not, the release is not the gated binary: stop and report.
4. Record the wheel sha256 and the `.so` md5 in the audit follow-up and in project memory.
5. Point `~/gate-0560-venv`'s successor (`~/gate-0570-venv`) at the pulled-back install for the next release's RED runs.
6. Redeploy apitap.dev docs from the web repo and run its live assertions, as done for 0.56.0.
